Merge is last-writer-wins key by key, as for the toggles. The client still sends only what the player changed this visit, so a late server reply never has defaults sent over it. `SettingsModel.migrate` is the hook for a future change of meaning (raise `SettingsSpec.VERSION`, add a step); there is none yet.

### ROBLOX-52 - What settings deliberately do not do (Claude, 2026-10-18)

No key or button remapping (the scheme is one for all three devices and the help card must stay true). No per-device settings, so the same look speeds apply to a phone and a laptop the player owns (touch has its own look speed, which is the only difference). No difficulty or rules settings (Standard and Free play are chosen at the menu). No graphics-quality setting: the lower-detail tier for touch-only devices is automatic and unchanged. No screen-reader or text-to-speech work. Colour choices beyond the grey eye-view switch are not offered. The PlayStation names (Cross, Circle, L1, R1, Touchpad) come from the same ControlText table as the rest of the game. Cannot be verified here: that the panel reads well on a real PlayStation or a lower-end tablet, and that thumb reach feels right (Studio tests measure text and geometry only).

### ROBLOX-53 - Settings Windows review and acceptance (Chet, 2026-10-18)

Unsupported future settings_version values make the visit read-only, preserving stored bytes. Normal schema-1 prefs still migrate to schema 2. This closes a review finding where validation accepted a future version but returned the current version and dropped unknown values before the next write.

Studio's server runner checks construction and layout without trying to bind client-only controls. The live-client playback opens the actual settings panel, checks its bound priority, paused world, value changes and menu return. The tap-jump playback queues press/release in the same tick: waiting a Heartbeat under startup load could make the supposed tap longer than the aim threshold. Production jump timing is unchanged. Exact Windows evidence, native counts, manual checks and publication status are in verification/roblox-settings-windows/RESULTS.md.

Physical PlayStation and tablet acceptance remain Nathan's checks after the private playtest update. Public release still requires those checks, release presentation/science review and Nathan's account/audience decisions. The intended peacock price remains approximately US$0.99 as a one-time cosmetic pass; it stays free during testing.


### ROBLOX-54 — Food visibility, meal progress and controller aim (2026-10-18)

Nathan's PlayStation feedback: show food reserves, let the left stick aim a held jump, and ease catches only slightly. The food bar is always shown during play. Growth displays actual fed progress and an approximate count of remaining housefly-sized meals, preserving READY_FOOD=0.9 and partial-meal/save semantics. The aim stick stops moving the body while the existing jump plan is selected; release/cancel are unchanged. Housefly swept contact radius including the spider goes from 0.70 to 0.75. Regression tests retain obstruction and non-strike-fall refusals.

### ROBLOX-55 — Additive habitat and smaller fruit-fly-inspired prey (2026-10-18)

Nathan asked for more places to explore and a second prey type. Add two leaves/stems, a terracotta step and an upper wooden ledge without moving old supports or the retreat. Every third new mixed-population spawn is a small tan/red-eyed fruit fly, drawn at 60% housefly size, food 0.18 instead of 0.35. Reuse the fly model of senses/flight/feeding and its conservative clearance graph; no species-specific behavioural accuracy is claimed. Keep independent constant references for both profiles in checkpoints. Existing rests retain their flies and acquire mixed replacements over time. Keep the four-fly target and low-detail tier.

### ROBLOX-56 — Clear central eye view and muted surround (2026-10-18)

Nathan revised the regional-blur request to a colour centre and black-and-white-like periphery. Retain central colour and disable whole-screen blur; wider neutral edge gradients mute peripheral contrast/colour. This is expressly a grey wash, not per-pixel desaturation, regional blur, a spectral simulation, or a measured retinal field. It avoids extra scene rendering on consoles/tablets. Focus, retina scans, sensed motion cues, Reduced Motion and whole-view EyeGrey remain. Scientific/engine references and limitations are in HANDOFF-roblox-player-feedback.md.

### ROBLOX-57 — Twitch and visible jump preparation (2026-10-18)

Short irregular-looking body snaps run on both detail tiers, without moving the motor/camera/foot anchors. Nathan also asked to show hydraulic pressure building before a jump. A drawn brace/crouch while holding jump and brief extension of pairs III/IV after launch illustrate preparation; actual pressure is not measured or exposed, and the body does not inflate. Existing fixed-tick jump events drive it, with no solver, jump-power or input-delay change. Reduced Motion suppresses these poses; courtship retains its own pose. Nabawy et al. 2018 reports muscular capacity sufficient for P. regius jumps, so do not claim hydraulic pressure is their sole power source. See the handoff's primary research links.

### ROBLOX-58 — Soft-skin movement after moulting (Nathan, 2026-10-18)

Walking and stalking run at 65% speed while the existing soft-cuticle timer is active; the existing 0.7 jump-reach factor remains. Both restore at hardening and derive from the saved growth state. Scale the automatic walk-to-edge timeout too so lowering on silk still works. HUD explains slower steps and shorter jumps. Values are game tuning, not measured biology. No change to gravity, airborne velocity, silk reeling or the underlying species profile.


### ROBLOX-59 — Dragline pose and the falling leaf tip (2026-10-18)

Nathan asked for a more convincing suspension and Godot's falling end platform. Draw the body head-down from its actual spinneret and narrow the hanging legs; retain the motor, camera and tether physics. A committed jump toward leaf_tip drops the surface and its art, allowing the existing line to catch the miss. Restore after eight simulated seconds only when clear; validate and persist the optional cooldown. Reduced Motion gives a static hanging pose and hides the falling animation. Research scope and evidence: HANDOFF-roblox-dragline.md.

### ROBLOX-60 — Grass and functional stalking cover (2026-10-18)

Fourteen sparse decorative grass patches cost 70 parts full / 42 low and never obstruct physics or sight. Four additional solid bark/leaf obstacles near hunting perches do block sight and support climbing in both detail tiers. Preserve retreat geometry and open jumping routes. Query tests distinguish real cover from decorative foliage. No higher prey population or per-blade physics.

### ROBLOX-61 — Clear meal counts and slightly faster hunger (2026-10-18)

Nathan superseded the proposed slower hunger: keep the normal meal goal, drain energy a bit faster, and count smaller prey proportionally. Live drain is 1.1 times the original LIFE rate; gentle mode retains its existing modifier. Display a count out of three housefly-equivalent meals, in half-meal increments. New small prey supplies exactly half a housefly (0.175 versus 0.35). Live growth credits food swallowed, not only energy that fits in the reserve, normalized to the existing 0.9 growth threshold. Partial progress and old saves remain; legacy fruit prey with 0.18 nutrition is still accepted. Godot golden fixtures retain original default rates.

### ROBLOX-62 — Longer wasp visits and an optional rival (2026-10-18)

Nathan requested greater danger. Live wasp search lasts 72 rather than 45 seconds, notice accrues 20% faster, and close inspection is 1.2 rather than 1.5 seconds. Preserve the eight-second arrival warning, daily limit, first-day/first-meal protection, safe crack and gentle-mode touches. Original defaults remain for reference replay tests.

A brown rival jumping spider patrols the far eastern sill after day one and the first meal, appearing only away from the player. It sees through the same world query as prey; cover breaks sight. A one-second visible windup precedes a committed lunge; jumping or dropping during preparation breaks it. It does not attack during a wasp visit or pursue into the crack. A player's strike can catch it before its lunge; staying still eats it for two housefly-equivalent meals. It can end a standard life, with the existing return-to-rest/another-spider options; gentle play only touches. Its consumed state is saved so rest recovery cannot create another meal in the same saved world. No gore, payment, or requirement to hunt it.

This is an illustrative salticid encounter, not a new species simulation. Field evidence for spider prey in P. audax: Okuyama (2007), https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/aez/42/4/42_4_663/_article . Exact timings, reward and geographical encounter are game design.

### ROBLOX-63 — Hard mode remains a design (2026-10-18)

HARD_MODE_DESIGN.md proposes an explicit optional life with separate saves and 50% greater food demand than revised Normal. It is not enabled in this build. Preserve accessibility, safe retreat, warning and reliable movement. Implement and test save migration before exposing its menu choice.

Addendum (2026-10-19): this entry is stale in its numbers. The "revised Normal" it measures against has moved twice (hunger drain 1.55 in ROBLOX-78 and 1.9 in ROBLOX-88, a day of 0.612 energy; five flies to a moult, not three; two wasp visits a day, not one), Easy and Free play have had their own gentle pacing since ROBLOX-91, and Hard's save would be a fourth slot beside main, free and easy. `docs/HARD_MODE_DESIGN.md` was rebased on the current numbers the same day, and its "50% greater food demand" is now a figure for Nathan to choose again (50% on top of 1.9 is a hard game). Hard mode is still a design and is not enabled.

### ROBLOX-64 - The third spider: the ant-mimicking Myrmarachne formicaria female, drawn and cosmetic only (Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan picked an ant-mimicking jumping spider as the next planned cosmetic add-on, alongside the peacock. It is a drawn appearance (`myrmarachne_formicaria_female`, 0.5 studs long: the playbacks' targets were 5 mm and 1 stud is 1 cm) and a pose, nothing more: the same motor, collider, path, speed, jump, senses, silk, LIFE and wasp behaviour as the first spider, so a cosmetic add-on never changes difficulty. `tests/client_ant.luau` walks the ant mimic and the first spider through the same 90 frames and requires the same ground covered. The female is drawn because the male's huge forward-pointing chelicerae are a possible later variant.

The ant look is a waist (a pedicel and a petiole-like node between a thorax and a long abdomen), a dark glossy cuticle (Reflectance 0.16 on the body segments, a new optional per-appearance look knob) and long thin legs. Cheeks, fan and fuzz are optional in `Anatomy` and absent here. Part budgets: 79 at full detail (budget 100), 69 at low (budget 70); the first spider is 624 / 266 and the peacock 119 / 80.

Documented (Shamble, Hoy, Cohen and Beatus 2017, Proc. R. Soc. B 284: 20170308, doi:10.1098/rspb.2017.0308) and used from `Data/AntMimic`: the mimic walks on eight legs; stationary, legs I are raised above head height and move in phase like ant antennae; it makes only short stops (83.3 +- 82.6 ms), so it looks almost always moving; its path is sine-like with a wavelength of about ten body lengths (range 5 to 10); in playbacks to Phidippus audax, non-mimic spider targets were attacked 4.5 times as often as ants and 3 times as often as mimics. Artistic and marked ART in the data: every rate and size of the drawing (antennae delay 0.25 s, rise 0.25 s, fall 0.12 s, wave 1.6 Hz and, for the display key, 3 Hz), the drawn yaw (12 degrees) and sway (0.04 studs), the ease times, every colour and proportion.

The ant walk is drawn, never walked. The real animal's zigzag cannot be walked without changing where the player's spider goes, so `AntMimicPose` turns the drawn body about its up axis and sways it sideways by a cosine and sine of the distance the motor really moved (wavelength ten body lengths), eased in and out, and holds the wobble through a stop shorter than the documented 83.3 ms. It depends only on distance, so slow motion and frame rate change nothing but speed. ReducedMotion halves the wobble and stops the waving (the legs stay up). The game makes no claim about where the species lives. The Display key (X, RB, touch "Wave") on the ant mimic raises legs I higher and waves them faster and wider; the paper reports no such display, so that is labelled artistic everywhere it appears.

Appearance is a Player attribute, saved in PlayerProgress; `goodId` already accepts any plain name of at most 48 characters, so the new id (29 characters) needed no validation change, only tests (`state.luau`, `state_client.luau`). An unknown id from a newer build is still kept and not put on.

### ROBLOX-65 - Choosing the ant mimic, and its notebook page (Claude, 2026-10-18)

In the silk-lined crack the change-spider key (C, RB / R1, touch Change) now cycles first spider, peacock spider, ant mimic, then back. `SpiderChoice` gains `displayKind` ("dance" for the peacock, "antennae" for the ant mimic, nil for the first spider) so the one Display key, its prompts, its status line and its touch button say the right thing per spider and are absent for the first spider.

The notebook gains one page, "Walking like an ant" (26 authored pages, 25 reachable at the time; only felt surface vibration is unwired. With ROBLOX-80's and ROBLOX-83's pages the book now has 28 authored and 27 reachable: felt is still the only one unwired, and buzz is wired). It opens when the player, as the ant mimic, keeps the antennae up for `AntMimic.noticeSeconds` (1 s), through the existing watcher pattern (`NotebookWatch.antennae`), and its To try entry is "Change to the ant mimic while resting, then stand still." The page's text is documented biology only; its separate game line says what the game simplifies (the look is drawn, the zigzag is not walked, the antennae are stylised). The full citation and what each sentence rests on are in `docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md`. It was written to the same sourcing rules as the peacock page: no range or continent is claimed, and the playback result is reported as the paper reports it (Phidippus audax as the predator, 5 mm targets).

### ROBLOX-66 - What the ant mimic deliberately does not do (Claude, 2026-10-18)

It does not change difficulty: not the speed, jump, camera, senses, fly or wasp behaviour, and the wasp is not fooled by the ant (the paper's protection is against jumping-spider predators, and the game's wasp is a different predator). It does not walk the real zigzag. It does not show the male, mimicry of a particular ant species, or the mimic's jump. It sells nothing: no Game Pass is wired, and the ant mimic stays free for testing, exactly like the peacock. Nathan's plan is a planned cosmetic add-on of about US$0.99 beside the peacock, to be implemented as a Game Pass on ROBLOX-48's pattern (server-checked ownership, replicated Player attribute, a Studio override, Studio specs for owned, not owned and purchase finished) when he says so; the first spider and the whole game remain free. Nothing here has run in Studio or on a device.


### ROBLOX-67 - The wasp comes every day (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: the new rival male and the wasp should always be present. For the wasp that is a visit every day, guaranteed, from the second day: `Data/Wasp.game.visitChance = 1.0` (`WaspDirector:useGameRules()` applies it, with Chet's live multipliers, which moved from a client line into the same table). The Godot values (`visitChance 0.6`, `firstDay 2`) are untouched, and a director that is never given the game's rules replays Godot, so `wasp_reference.json` and the goldens stay valid. Nothing else about a visit changed: at most one a day, in the warm window (10:00 to 15:30; the hour is drawn from the world's seed and the day, so it varies from day to day and is the same on a return to a checkpoint), the full eight seconds of warning (hum and caption) before it can look, never while the spider rests or moults, and every escape (out of sight, the crack, a jump or a drop) as before. The brand-new-life protections stay as they were: no visit on day 1 at all, and none before the first meal is finished. If the spider rests through the whole window there is no visit that day (a rest in the morning only delays it). Easy and Free get the same daily visit with their gentle outcome. Tests: `tests/wasp.luau` (a simulated ten days hour by hour).

### ROBLOX-68 - A rival every day (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

The brown rival is no longer optional: it is in every life from the first day it is allowed, and when it is eaten a new one arrives on the next morning. One rival at a time, the same eastern-sill territory, the same rules (no attack, warning or touch in the crack, a pause during a wasp visit, the one-second windup and the committed lunge unchanged). `Data/RivalRules` holds the arrival rules: first day 2 and first meal finished (the same protection as the wasp; Chet's rule, kept, so the rival's first morning is day 2), arrival from 07:00 (the spider's waking hour), not while the spider rests or the wasp visits, and not while the spider is within 8 studs of where it would arrive. The state `dormant` now means "has not arrived yet". An eaten rival stamps `day`, the day it was eaten, and `Rival.renew` turns the same table into a fresh rival when a later day reaches 07:00 (the world holds that table). A record from before this change has no `day`: it is a good record, its eaten rival is stamped the first time it is seen, and a new rival comes the morning after. A `day` that is not a finite number of at least zero is refused by checkpoint validation; a return to a rest restores that rest's state, so eating the rival and then returning to a rest does not give a second meal that day. Driven off: the rival is not driven away in the current design (a missed lunge only recovers and patrols), so only eating it ends it. Not announced on the screen: it is simply there.

Food balance, for Nathan (not retuned): a day costs the spider about 0.35 to 0.38 energy (11 awake hours at 0.021 x 1.1 plus 13 asleep at 0.007 x 1.1); a rival gives 0.7, two days of food and two thirds of a moult's food, and it is available every day. A player who catches the rival each day cannot starve, and moults sooner. It is not free (the rival hunts back and kills in standard rules, and getting within reach of it means a jump through its windup), and the energy cap of 1.0 wastes what is above it. If it turns out too generous in play, the simplest dials are `Rival.FOOD`, the arrival hour and day gap in `Data/RivalRules`, and the hunger factor.

### ROBLOX-69 - The rival stalks the whole sill (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "The male should stalk the whole environment as well." The rival is no longer kept to the eastern floor. It has its own `SpiderMotor` (the player's own surface-walker: the same collider, its own probe, parented to the unqueryable `JumpArc` folder and kept out of the player's ray filter, so neither the rival nor the player's rays see the other's motor) and walks wherever the player can: the floor, leaves and stems, the planter and its rim, the frames and the back wall, the cover pieces. `Predator/RivalRoute` makes a graph from `Layout.PIECES` alone (every box face a node, every seam between faces an edge, convex when it turns over a box's edge, concave where it meets another box's wall) and plans by Dijkstra over seam crossings, with the corners of boxes standing on a face as extra stops so a straight walk never runs into a box. `Predator/RivalDrive` is the brain between `RivalSpider`'s state and the motor: it picks a place to patrol to (about 100 places spread over every face, in a fixed order by the leg number `leg`, which the checkpoint keeps), plans the seams, and hands the motor an ordinary input (wish direction, focus on the spider during the windup). Decisions (sight, face, goal, progress) are made every `Rules.thinkSeconds` = 0.1 s; the motor steps every tick while it walks and every tenth tick while it stands. Replans are rare (a few per minute at most) and are the only costly work (a plan is 0.2 s in the CLI's slow Vector3 shim and a few ms in the engine).

What did not change: it never enters the retreat (the crevice boxes and a margin are a keep-out box that every route rounds, the crevice faces are not in the graph, and a last guard puts it back at its last place outside); it pauses during a wasp visit; the one-second windup and the committed lunge are as before; the jump-contact capture, the five seconds of eating, the food value, the daily respawn and the record are as before. What it does now: it patrols to the places above at 0.8 wu/s (it was 0.45 on the old east floor; faster to cross a bigger sill in a reasonable time, still slower than its 1.1 stalk and the spider's 2.5); it notices the spider by the same movement-only perception and line of sight, at any height, within 8 wu; it stalks at 1.1 wu/s toward the spider's face (surface), by the cover and obstacles on the way (the route rounds boxes; it does not hide behind one deliberately) and, if it loses sight, goes on to where it last saw the spider for three seconds and then gives up; it winds up when it is within 2.5 wu and the spider is on the same face or one that meets it along a seam (a leaf top and the leaf's edge, a floor and the planter's foot); the lunge goes to the spider's own place, wherever that is, and lands back on the surface it started from.

Record: `RivalSpider` has two new numbers, `up` (the face normal it stands on; the old record means the floor) and `leg` (the number of patrol places visited; the old record means none). Validation refuses a malformed `up` or `leg`, a position outside the sill's play bounds and a position in the retreat. A checkpoint taken mid-climb brings the rival back on the same wall on the same leg; the drive is plain scratch state made again from the record (it places its motor where the record says when the two differ, which is also how a returned rest works). Not done, on purpose: hops (jumps) between surfaces (the rival walks every seam the player's motor walks; a jump would need `JumpPlanner` and a landing rule for a creature that is not steered by a person), and an exact hide-behind-cover behaviour. The rival can fall off an edge or a leaf tip it cannot hold, as the player can; it lands and plans again from where it is.

### ROBLOX-70 - The wasp sometimes takes the rival instead (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "The wasp should kill the rival male like 10 percent of the time." In the game (`useGameRules`; Godot's director and its goldens are untouched), each visit has a draw, from a stream of its own so no other draw of the visit moves, with `Data.Wasp.game.takesRival = 0.10`. A visit that draws "the rival" flies in as always (the warning, the eight seconds) and, when it reaches the sill, looks for a free rival (`RivalSpider.target`: patrolling, stalking, winding up or standing, never mid-lunge, never held by the spider, never in the retreat). If there is one and the wasp can get to a place above it with a clear way down (the first of thirteen offsets from `Data.Wasp.rival.offsets`; 91 of the 101 places the rival patrols to qualify, the rest are undersides and nooks), the visit is for the rival and not for the spider: it flies there at 5 wu/s (`taking`), hangs over it for 0.8 s, strikes (`seizing`, 0.35 s) and carries it up and away over the frame under it like a spider (`bearing`, the same 3.5 s, the same legs-clutching pose, the rival drawn hanging under it). The wasp does not look for the spider at all during those phases and never notices it, so the spider is spared that day and the visit counts as that day's one visit (no second wasp). If there is no rival, or it is in the retreat, or the wasp cannot get at it, the visit is the spider's search as before; if the rival is caught by the spider or otherwise gone before the strike, the wasp goes on searching (and can then find the spider).

The rival: new state `taken` (not catchable, not perceived, not driven), then `consumed` with no food, `day` set to that day and `fate = "taken by the wasp"` (an eaten rival says `"eaten"`; no fate at all, as in older records, is fine; anything else is refused). A new one comes the next morning from 07:00 exactly as after an eaten one (ROBLOX-68). A record taken while it hangs under the wasp restores, and because a visit in progress is never kept (the wasp is away after a return), the rival in it is carried off at once the next tick: gone for the day, with its reason. A returned-to rest brings back that rest's state.

Told to the player only if the player could know: a caption in the existing sound style (`[the deep hum drops toward something on the sill, ahead-left]`, then `[a short scuffle, then the deep hum rising away with something, above]`) and the status line are shown only when the wasp is within 18 wu of the spider, or in clear sight of it within 24 wu (`WaspText.heardRival`), and a notice (`The wasp seized the other spider and carried it off. It is gone for today.`) with the same condition when it seizes. Far away and out of sight, the spider sees and hears nothing of it. Not done: the rival does not flee or struggle, and the player cannot intervene (it is paused during a visit anyway).

### ROBLOX-71 - A Spider row in the Settings, so every spider can be found (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan could only play the first spider in practice: the peacock and the ant mimic were reachable only by resting awake in the silk-lined crack and pressing the choose key, which nobody finds. Settings now has a section "Your spider" with one row, Spider, that shows the spider being played by name (`PeacockText.NAMES` with a capital: "The first spider", "The peacock spider", "The ant-mimicking spider") and steps first spider, peacock, ant mimic (`SpiderChoice.ORDER`) with the arrows, a tap on the word, Enter or Left and Right, like any other choice. It is the last section, so the panel still opens on Sound and a stray Enter cannot change the spider.

The spider is not a setting. It is kept in the save as `appearance` (the Player attribute `Appearance`, sent when the client calls `markDirty`), so the row is a live row: `SettingsSpec.LIVE` beside `SettingsSpec.LIST`, which stays the saved settings (22, unchanged, so the saved settings table and the Studio spec's count are as they were). `SettingsModel.spec`, `items`, `sections`, `step`, `valueText` and `line` know the live row; `ids`, `defaults`, `known`, `clean`, `cleanAll` and `get` do not, so it has no attribute of the id Spider, is never written to or read from the saved settings, and is not put back by Reset to defaults (a test saves with a `Spider` setting present and checks it is dropped). Stepping and words come from `SpiderChoice.step` and `PeacockText.title`. The panel's `io.set` can now answer a line (`string?`); a live row's answer replaces the saving line until the next change. The row's word is fitted to its button (two lines at normal size for the longest name).

Applying it: from the Settings, the spider can change anywhere it stands safely, not only in the crack. Safe is `SpiderChoice.safeToChange`: attached to a surface, not seized by the wasp, not preparing a jump, not mid-jump (`jump:isBusy()`), not on a silk line, not moulting and not in the fly struggle (`prey.feeding.struggling`). Otherwise it does not change: a toast and the panel's line say "Your spider will not change now. Stand safely on a surface first", and the row shows the spider it still is. The change itself is the existing `setAppearance` (the rig is built again; gait, eye view and the hidden body carry over), now reached through one `spiderPick.switch` that both ways of choosing use, which also marks the choice as the player's own (so a late save cannot overwrite it), calls `markDirty` and toasts. The state sits in an existing-style table `spiderPick` that replaced the `appearanceTouched` local, so the client's local count did not grow.

What deliberately did not change: the crack's key (C, RB / R1, the touch Change button) works exactly as before, still only while resting awake and not moulting, with the same toast otherwise. A changed spider is still only a drawn look and a pose (ROBLOX-64..66); the spider's speed, jump, senses and the rest do not depend on the pick. Reset to defaults does not touch the spider.

The ownership hook, for later: `SpiderChoice.locked(id, owned)` is the one place that says a spider is not the player's yet. It returns false for everything now, because the peacock and the ant mimic are free for testing (ROBLOX-66). When Nathan says the sales start, ROBLOX-48's Game Pass pattern (server-checked ownership, a replicated Player attribute, a Studio override) fills the `owned` table and `locked` returns true for a pass-only spider that is not in it. `SpiderChoice.step` (the Settings row) and `SpiderChoice.next` (the crack) both skip locked spiders, going either way round, and the client refuses a locked one chosen any other way; with everything else locked a choice stays where it is. Nothing is sold or checked now.

Discoverability: the controls card (H) gains one line under the crack's, `Spider: change it in Settings (O) when standing safe` (View, then Settings on a controller; Menu, then Settings on touch), from `PeacockText.settingsLine`. Tests: `tests/peacock.luau` (the hook, cycling past a locked spider both ways, an owned table reopening it, `safeToChange` for each unsafe state, the card's line on every device) and `tests/settings.luau` (the live row's place in the table, the model and the nav; not saved, not read, not reset; the panel's row, refusal and layout on phone and large text; and the real client: the row changes the spider on the open sill and keeps it as `Appearance` and never as a setting, refuses while seized, preparing, struggling or airborne with the row snapping back, and skips a locked spider). Not run in Studio or on a device: the panel's look with the long name on a real phone, `tests/studio/SettingsSpec.luau` (updated for the live row: five headers, 23 rows) and `tests/studio/PromptSpec.luau`'s check that the controls card still fits ten lines on a phone with the new line.

### ROBLOX-72 - Easier tapping when catching a fly (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "make the tapping a bit easier when catching the fly." Godot's struggle (frozen `feeding_controller.gd`, ported as `Prey/Feeding`): the grip starts at 0.4, drains 0.32 x the fly's strength a second (strength falls from 1 to 0.5 over the fly's 6 to 8 s of venom) plus a jerk of 0.12 x strength every 0.35 to 0.75 s, each tap adds 0.085, Automatic grip adds 0.4 a second, and the fly is subdued at a grip of 1 or after 12 s. In the game, with `Data/Catch.game`, three numbers change: `startGrip` 0.4 to 0.5, `tapGain` 0.085 to 0.12 and `jerkScale` 1.0 to 0.8 (a jerk takes 0.096 x strength, not 0.12). Nothing else does: the drain, the jerks' timing, the venom, Automatic grip, the 12 s cap, the wait for a landing and the meal are as they were.

The way it is done follows `Wasp.game` and `WaspDirector:useGameRules()`. `Feeding.new` has three fields holding Godot's numbers (`startGrip`, `tapGain`, `jerkScale`, defaults 0.4, 0.085 and 1), `capture` and `step` read them, and `Feeding:useGameRules()` copies `Data/Catch.game` into them. The client calls it once where it hands feeding the life's reserves. A controller never given the game's rules replays Godot bit for bit: a scripted struggle (4.5 taps a second from 0.2 s) ends at the same frame with the same grip to 17 digits as before the change, which `tests/prey.luau` pins, and the existing tests and goldens did not move.

What it feels like (measured over 60 to 100 flies with different random streams, taps spaced evenly at 60 Hz, `tests/prey.luau`). Left alone, a fresh catch tears free after 1.0 to 1.45 s, not 0.7 to 1.0 s. A steady 3 taps a second now wins from the start every time and lost every time before; the old game needed about 5 a second, and 4 a second is now certain where it was a toss-up. The first tap can come as late as 0.75 s and a steady 4 a second still wins (Godot's tears free first). After two seconds of 5 taps a second, a pause of 1.5 s no longer loses the fly (a pause of 1 s lost it before; any pause of that length did). Two taps a second, one, or none, still tear free, so the struggle is still a struggle: an idle player still loses the fly. The old claim in the brief that a lapse of about 1.5 s "no longer loses a fresh catch" is not true of a catch with no taps at all (it lasts about 1.15 s on average); it is true of a lapse after the first couple of seconds of tapping, as above. Not changed: the 12 s cap (the longest tuned struggle in the tests is under 12.02 s), Easy and Free (they get the same easier tap; there was no gentler struggle before), and Automatic grip.

If it is still too hard or too easy in play, the dials are those three numbers in `Data/Catch`. Not run in Studio or on a device: the feel under a real finger, and touch's Grip button, which sends the same tap.

### ROBLOX-73 - Striking the rival is a contest, and the rival presses harder (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "the rival male should also attack and be dangerous." Until now the spider's strike caught the rival whenever the strike's sweep passed within 0.65 wu with a clear line, while the rival had to stalk, wind up for a second and lunge to touch the spider. Two changes, both in the shared sim (`Predator/RivalSpider`, `Data/RivalRules`), so the CLI tests cover them.

The contest. A rival that has seen the spider coming is aware: it is stalking or winding up, or it is patrolling with a notice of at least `Rules.awareNotice` = 0.3 (the notice rises one a second while it sees the spider). A strike that reaches an aware rival is no longer a sure catch. A rival that is not aware (patrolling with a notice under 0.3, or recovering) is caught outright as before, with no draw, and a lunging rival still cannot be struck. For an aware rival the outcome is drawn: 55% caught (`contestCaptured`, as before: it is held and eaten for two housefly meals), 25% it sprang clear (`contestSprang`: the rival goes to `recover`, the spider lands where the strike carries it, nothing is lost) and 20% a counter-bite (the rest: the rival goes to `recover` and the spider loses `counterCost` = 0.25 energy, about two thirds of a day's cost of 0.35 to 0.38). Over 6000 simulated strikes the rates are 0.549, 0.251 and 0.200. In Easy and Free (`s.gentle`) the counter-bite is the same event and costs nothing, and its notice says so; the draw is the same.

The draw is `RivalSpider.contest(seed, day, n)`: a stream of its own (the port's Park-Miller, mixed from the world's seed 914, the day and `n` as the wasp's streams are, with its own salt), so no other draw in the game moves. `n` is `contests`, a new number in the rival's record: how many contests this rival has had, 0 for a new rival (and for a record from before this change, which has none). The same seed, day and count give the same outcome, and because the count is in the checkpoint, a return to an earlier rest brings back the count of that rest, so the same strike on the same day gives the same outcome and the draw cannot be rerolled by returning (a different day is a different draw, which costs the player a day). A record with a `contests` that is not a whole number from 0 up, or a `spared` that is not true or false, is refused. One rule keeps the contest real: a strike goes on for several ticks (up to 4 s in the air), and a rival that has just sprung clear is in `recover`, which is a free catch, so the rival is marked `spared` for the rest of that strike. The next strike, or any strike at a rival that is recovering from something else, is a free catch as before; a spared rival gets no cover from a second jump.

What it costs: the counter-bite goes through the needs model as a new approved call, `NeedsModel:injure(amount)` (never more than the spider has, nothing in the gentle modes, no starving hours of its own; the reserve is already in the record). The step event for it is `("counter", cost)`: the number is the cost, not food, so the client does not grant it. The words are in `Shared/UI/RivalText`, shown as the life's notice: "Caught the rival. Stay still to eat; worth two housefly meals." (the old line, now from there), "It sprang clear of your strike." and "It bit back! Jump away!" ("It bit back, but you are safe in this mode. Jump away!" in the gentle modes). The client stays at its limit of 200 locals: the words sit in the `rivalLegs` table, the seed is a field of the rival's input.

The initiative. In `RivalRules`: `reachWu` 2.5 to 3.2 (the windup begins this close, on the same face or one that meets it), `rememberSeconds` 3 to 5 (it goes on stalking toward where it last saw the spider this long after losing sight), and the notice at which a patrolling rival starts to stalk, which was a literal 0.6 in `RivalSpider`, is now `Rules.stalkNotice` = 0.45 (it takes an interest after about 0.45 s of seeing the spider, not 0.6). Between a notice of 0.3 and 0.45 a rival is aware but still patrolling. What did not change: the windup is still one second and the lunge 0.4 s with contact at 0.6, which is the warning and the fairness; the sight range (8 wu), the walking speeds, the retreat's complete safety, the pause during a wasp visit, the food, the daily rival and the wasp taking it. Godot's non-driven rival (the CLI's floor-only checks) keeps its literal 2.5 reach. The killing touch is still standard rules only.

If it is too dangerous or too easy in play, the dials are all in `Data/RivalRules`: the three contest numbers, `counterCost`, `awareNotice`, `stalkNotice`, `reachWu`. Tests: `tests/rival.luau` (the numbers pinned, the aware states, an unaware strike still a free catch with no draw, the rates over 6000 strikes, determinism and a returned record, the rest of a strike, gentle, validation, `injure`, the words), `tests/rivalnav.luau` (a windup at 3.2 wu beyond the old 2.5, a rival that remembers for 4 s, the contest through the drive), `tests/checkpoint.luau` (the count kept, the same outcome after a return, bad records refused) and `tests/client_rival.luau` (the real client: each outcome's notice, the cost of 0.25 and none when gentle, an unaware rival still caught outright). Not run in Studio or on a device: how the new windup distance and the contest feel in play, and the Studio playback (`tests/client/SilkPlayback.luau`, which clears the rival's notice before its strike so it stays a free catch).

### ROBLOX-74 - More grass and detail, and the morning dew (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "expand the environment, add more grass and detail." Pure visual density, in the drawn decor only. Chet's grass (`Plants.grass`, `Flora.grass`: 14 patches of five blades, the last two of each fine) is extended, not copied: `Plants.grassBlades()` is now the one pure list of every blade (Chet's 14 patches first, built by the same formula, so the old 70 blades are exactly where they were), `Plants.grass` draws that list, and the dew reads the same list for its tips. Added in the full tier, from `Flora.worldSeed` = 914 (the number the prey, the wasp and the rival draw from) and a salt of its own for each kind:

- Grass: 20 more tufts of four blades (the last two fine), placed in five regions (the trough's soil 5, along the back wall 6, along the front lip 4, by the right frame 3, by the left frame north of the retreat 2), each at least 0.8 from any other foot. A tuft grows between short and lush; a blade is 0.35 to 1.6 tall (Chet's were 0.25 to 0.55; 1 stud is 1 cm), 0.06 to 0.1 wide, leans 0.08 to 0.5 rad; its colour is a mix of two of Chet's three greens times a shade of 0.84 to 1.14, and about one blade in eight is dry straw (`a8a468`; `dryShare` 0.12, drawn for each blade). Total 150 blades in 34 tufts (was 70 in 14).
- Moss on the shaded side: the sun is behind the window, so the trough's room-side face and the foot of the back wall's room-side face are in shade. Four patches of seven flattened mounds (0.3 to 0.75 wide, a third as thick) lying on those faces, 28 parts. The old moss on the soil is untouched.
- Pebbles and bark chips on the sill floor: 14 pebbles (0.3 to 0.85 long, 0.34 as high, a fifth sunk into the floor) and 8 bark chips (thin tilted slabs, four browns), 22 parts, each at least 0.8 from any other thing on the floor (grass feet, Wear's coin and fallen leaves, `Graphics.details` chips and crumbs, the added leaves), none over 3.5 mm high.
- Two fallen leaves: a dry gold one and an olive one, blown in from the garden: a pointed oval blade with one edge lifted so the other touches the floor, a stub of stalk and (full tier) a lighter midrib, 6 parts. The Pilea's three fallen leaves and the coin are as they were.
- Morning dew: 56 small translucent Glass droplets (0.07 to 0.15 across, transparency 0.28 to 0.45, reflectance 0.25): 25 on the tips of the tallest blade of most tufts, 25 round the rims of the five green Pilea leaves (`Plants.leafEdge`, 0.4 in from the edge), 6 on the fallen leaves. They are built invisible and follow the hour, not the daylight (daylight is already full by 08:00): `DayLight.dewAt(hour)` is 0 from 09:30 to 20:00, rises smoothly from 20:00 to 1 at 05:00 (about 0.42 at midnight), holds 1 until 07:30 and falls smoothly to 0 at 09:30 (0.5 at 08:30). Every drop has a `Lag` (0 to 0.55), the share of the dew at which it appears and is gone, so they come and go one by one, and a `Base` transparency; `DayLight.applyDew(decor, hour)` sets their Transparency (1 minus the drop's share times 1 minus Base), only when the level has moved by 0.01 (or reached 0 or 1), keeping the last level in the Dew folder's own attribute `Level`, so the client holds no state: one line after the daylight in the client's frame (the client stays at its 200 locals). A rest or a return to a checkpoint changes the hour and the next frame sets the drops. At the compressed clock a morning's burn-off (07:30 to 09:30) takes 150 s of play; the night's gathering passes during sleep.

The hard constraint, and how it is kept. `WindowsillLayout.PIECES` is not touched (a test pins the 28 boxes by a checksum of every corner), nor `DECOR`, so `Predator/RivalRoute`'s graph, the player's walkable surfaces and every raycast stay byte for byte as they were. Every new part is built by `Habitat/Shapes` (anchored, `CanCollide`, `CanQuery` and `CanTouch` all false, no shadow), and a test shoots 624 rays down through the whole sill with and without the whole decor and gets the same piece at the same point every time. `Shapes.clearAt` reads the layout to choose places (not inside a piece that rises from the surface, not within 4.5 of the spawn, not within 1.5 of the retreat) and changes nothing in it. The new code is the new module `Habitat/Dressing`, one `attempt` for each of moss, litter, windfall and dew in `Scenery.decor` (a failure leaves the rest, as for the other looks), and the added data in `Data/Flora`.

Part counts (`tests/world.luau`): the full tier adds 192 parts (grass 80, moss 28, pebbles and bark 22, fallen leaves 6, dew 56) and decor goes from 964 to 1156 parts, inside the existing budget of 1500. The low tier (what `FullOnly` and `Client/WorldDetail` leave) keeps 85 of them (grass 40: the two core blades of each added tuft; moss 12: three mounds of each patch; litter 10: 6 pebbles and 4 chips; leaf parts 4: blades and stalks; dew 19: the drops on core grass blades of every second tuft and two on each Pilea leaf, none on the fallen leaves) and decor goes from 207 to 292, inside the budget of 450. A drop never stands on something the tier removes: a test checks that every remaining grass drop is on a remaining blade. Seeded: building the decor twice gives the same fingerprint, another world seed gives another sill, and the fingerprint is pinned.

What deliberately did not change: Chet's tufts, moss on the soil, the trough's pebbles and the Pilea; the lighting and `DayLight.apply`; gameplay of every kind (a spider walking through a tuft or a drop simply passes through it, as through the existing grass and condensation). Not run in Studio: how any of it looks (the blade sizes at the camera's usual distance, the Glass material's look at 0.1 across, the dew's gathering, the moss's colour in shade), the real frame cost on a phone (85 more parts in the low tier), and the Studio spec was not extended. If it is too much or too little, the dials are in `Data/Flora`: `grass.more.regions[].n`, `moss.patches`, `litter`, `dew` (the hours, the sizes, the counts).

### ROBLOX-75 - The wasp visits twice a day (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "the wasp needs to be around more, maybe a couple times through the day." ROBLOX-67 gave one guaranteed visit a day from day 2, at a seeded hour in 10:00 to 15:30. In the game (`Data/Wasp.game`, applied by `WaspDirector:useGameRules()`; Godot's director and its goldens are untouched) there are now two: one in a morning window, 09:30 to 12:00, and one in an afternoon window, 13:30 to 16:30 (`Wasp.game.windows`). The hour of each is drawn from the world's seed, the day and the visit's place in the day (the first visit's stream is the one Godot's replay uses, the second has the next salt), in the first part of its window, up to an hour before the window's end, as 10:00 to 14:30 was in 15:30. The afternoon's hour is at least `minGapH` = 2.0 h after the morning's (`max(window start, morning + 2)`; enforced, so it still holds if the windows are changed to overlap, which a test does). With the windows as they are the planned hours are 09:30 to 11:00 and 13:30 to 15:30, so the planned gap is at least 2.5 h (3.4 to 5.4 h over 2000 days of seed 914). `visitChance` (1.0 in the game) is drawn for each visit alone. The hours stay a pure function of seed and day (`plannedHours(day)` returns both, `plannedHour(day)` the first).

Every visit is a full visit: the eight seconds of warning, the 72 s search, its own place from its own stream (visit number n of the life), its own `takesRival` draw (ROBLOX-70: about one visit in ten, 3000 pairs of visits take it 0.09 to 0.11 each time and twice only about once in a hundred, so a morning that takes the rival does not decide the afternoon; a taken rival is gone for the day, so the afternoon visit, if any, searches for the spider). Each has its own protections, with the exact rule of ROBLOX-67 applied to each window: no visit on day 1; none before the first meal is finished; a rest or moult at its planned hour only delays it, to the window's end, and never begins inside a rest; a rest through the whole of a window skips that visit and no other (the morning's is not made up in the afternoon, the afternoon's is not moved to the evening); a rest through both windows means no visit that day; Easy and Free get both, gentle. A visit lasts about 85 s of play (8 + up to 72 + 5), which is a little over an hour of the compressed clock (0.0133 h a second), so the two visits take about 2.3 of a day's 12 waking hours. The planned hours are at least 2.5 h apart; a rest can delay the morning visit to just before 12:00, so two visits can begin as little as 1.5 h apart (12:00 and 13:30), never overlapping (a visit of about 85 s is over by about 13:10 at the latest, before the afternoon window opens, and the director only begins a visit when the wasp is away).

Checkpoints: nothing is repeated and nothing rerolled. The wasp's record gains `last_visit_slot`, which of the day's visits the last one was (0 to 2), beside `last_visit_day`, `visits` and `escapes`. A director begins the first visit of the day not yet had whose window the hour is in, so a return to a rest after the morning's visit meets the afternoon's once and not the morning's again; a return to a rest before either meets both, the same two (hours from the seed and the day; the same visit numbers, so the same draws). A record from before this change has no slot: a `last_visit_day` in it counts as the day's last visit (slot 2), so no visit is ever repeated and an older record can only lose the day's second visit, never gain a repeat; a life not yet visited has 0. A slot that is not a whole number from 0 to 2 is refused by validation (`WaspDirector.problems`); a record without one is good. Godot's director has one window: a record of one visit on a day ends that day's visits, as it always did, and its hours are exactly the ones it always had (pinned in `tests/wasp.luau` from the code before this change, with `wasp_reference.json`, which did not change).

Tests: `tests/wasp.luau`, the ROBLOX-67 case rewritten for two visits (a simulated ten days hour by hour: two visits on days 2 to 10, none on day 1, none before the first meal, gentle too, a rest in the morning only delays, a rest through one window skips that visit alone, through both none) and a new case for the windows, the hours, the gap, the independent draws, and the checkpoint record. Not changed: the visit itself, what the wasp sees, its speed, the warning, the rival's daily arrival (ROBLOX-68), or anything about how a catch looks. Food and danger balance for Nathan, not retuned: a day now has twice the wasp, and the crack remains safe; if it is too much in play the dials are `Wasp.game.windows`, `minGapH` and `visitChance` (0.5 would make each visit a coin flip).

### ROBLOX-76 - Very light ambient music (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "some kind of very light ambient music." There is an original loop, `assets/audio/sill_music.wav`, made by `tools/make_sill_music.py` (numpy; nothing sampled or borrowed): 64 s, mono, 22050 Hz, 16 bit, 2,822,444 bytes, sha256 `c9de73c506cdbd76bc1e6139d56233c3bd242b1a3c19ae8562e60d36d04496f4`, in the pipeline's format. Four slow chords (Am9, Fmaj7, Cmaj7, Em7), 16 s each with 6 s equal-power crossfades computed circularly so the fourth folds back into the first, as a soft additive pad with a slow breath; seven sparse music-box notes of A pentatonic placed off the beat; a whisper of filtered air. Every source is exactly periodic in the loop, so it is seamless (the step from the last sample to the first is one LSB). Measured: peak -14.0 dBFS, rms -26.3 dBFS. Both files are new and are committed with this change. The WAV is not on Roblox: Chet uploads it, grants it to universe 10771103250 and pastes the id, like the other sounds.

`Client/Soundscape` gets a `music` voice: looped, 0.10 in the mix (`MIX.music`; the room is 0.09, the birds 0.16, the wasp's hum 0.42), a global sound (no position, so no roll-off), the player's Master volume times the new Music volume times the duck. `IDS.music = ""` is the placeholder: the voice is not created at all while the id is empty (no empty `SoundId` to load and fail), so the game is silent but otherwise exactly as it was until the id is pasted in; the manifest has the entry (`roblox_asset_id` null, `experience_access_saved` false) and its status note says what Chet does. Nothing else is needed in code when he does. It pauses with the menu and stops with the mute like every other voice, and it does not follow slow motion (the room tone slows to half pitch there; music an octave down would be the one thing that sounds wrong). Like everything in Soundscape it is presentation only and never feeds perception, the AI or the simulation (the header says so).

How loud it really is, for Nathan to judge in Studio: at 0.10 the loop's own rms (-26 dB) puts it at about -46 dB in the mix against the room tone's -33 dB and the birds' -32 dB, so it sits about 13 dB below the room tone: very light indeed, perhaps too light to be noticed in a quiet room. The one number to turn is `MIX.music` (0.2 would be about 6 dB up); I kept the 0.10 asked for.

The slider: `MusicVolume`, "Ambient music" in the Sound section, made exactly like the other three volumes of ROBLOX-49 (a slider from 0 to 1 in steps of 0.05, shown as a percent, default 1.0, no note), after Creature volume and before Captions. Saved settings are 23 (the Spider live row is not one), so the Studio spec's count is now 23 settings and 23 + 1 rows under five headers (`tests/studio/SettingsSpec.luau`, not run in Studio), the Sound section has 6 and `tests/settings.luau` says so. It is saved like the others (a save from before it has none and reads 1.0; nothing was renamed, so `SettingsSpec.VERSION` stays 1); the client passes it as the fourth argument of `setVolumes` (left out, it is 1).

Ducking: `Soundscape.updateWasp` already runs every frame with the director, so it sets a target there: 0.4 while the director's phase is anything but `away` (the eight seconds of warning while the wasp is still outside, the search whether it hovers, flies or walks, the strike and the carrying off, and the wasp taking the rival), 1.0 when it is gone, and `update` moves the duck toward it at 0.6 a second of real frame time, so the whole fall or rise takes a second and never overshoots; the music volume is 0.10 x master x music volume x duck. No new wiring in the client. Tests (`tests/settings.luau`): the slider like the other volumes; no voice and a healthy room with the empty id; with an id one looped voice at 0.10, master times music, a missing fourth volume is full, out of range held, the pause, the mute, slow motion; the duck half way at half a second, 40 per cent at a second, held, back in a second, begun at the warning, multiplied with the volumes; the real client with the empty id (no voice, MusicVolume reaches `soundscape.musicVolume`, a wrong-typed value reads full). Not run in Studio or on a device: any of how it sounds, whether Roblox streams a 2.8 MB WAV loop without a gap, and the music against the room tone; the real id is still to come.

Addendum (2026-10-19), what is true now. The mix is 0.18, not 0.10: the commit that wired the asset (da00869, 2026-10-18) raised `MIX.music` from the 0.10 asked for to 0.18, still under the buzz (0.23) and the hum (0.42), and ROBLOX-89's swell takes it from 0.18 to 0.288 at full volume. The "about 13 dB below the room tone" above is therefore about 5 dB less, nearer 8 dB. The WAV is on Roblox: it was uploaded on 2026-10-18 through the Creator Dashboard to the account socranates, with Nathan's go-ahead and no Robux charged, as asset `rbxassetid://114531800455832` (owner-only privacy, which covers the owner's universe). `assets/audio/manifest.json` has `roblox_asset_id` "114531800455832" and `experience_access_saved` true, and `IDS.music` carries the id, so the voice is made and plays; the empty-id placeholder described above is history, and the step "Chet uploads it" is done. The copy on Roblox is 2,828,210 bytes against the repo file's 2,822,444: the same samples with a different metadata chunk. That was recorded from the upload and not re-checked from this tree, which does not hold the uploaded file (the manifest's sha256 is the repo file's). The volume slider, the duck and the 23 saved settings stand as written.

### ROBLOX-77 - The struggle scales with the prey: small prey easier, the rival a hard meal (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "the rival spider should be harder to eat than normal prey, harder tapping mechanic" and "the smaller prey should be easier." Before this change there was one struggle, the house fly's (ROBLOX-72), and it began at a capture of any fly: the fruit flies (every third fly of the mixed population, half a housefly meal, `Species.fruit_fly_prototype`) had the same struggle as the house fly, and the rival had none at all: `RivalSpider.step`'s `captured` state went straight to five seconds of eating (0.14 food a second of 0.7), with no tapping, no failure and no grip bar.

Size scaling. `Data/Catch` gains `Catch.factorFor(profile)`, the difficulty of a catch against the house fly, read from the caught profile's `body.nutrition` (the one mass-like number a profile has) against the house fly's 0.35: `1 - 0.6 x (1 - ratio)`, held between 0.5 and 1.6 (`Catch.size`), so the house fly is exactly 1.0, the fruit fly (0.175) is 0.7, and any prey added later gets its struggle from its profile for free. `Catch.scaled(factor)` turns it into the struggle's numbers: the start grip of ROBLOX-72 (0.5), a tap worth `0.12 / factor`, the drain and the jerks `factor` times as much. `Feeding` has three more fields for Godot's remaining struggle numbers (`drainScale` 1, `tire` 0.5, the fall of the prey's strength over its venom, and `cap` 12) and a `game` flag; `Feeding:useGameRules()` sets the flag and the house fly's numbers, and at each capture a controller that has the flag applies `Catch.scaled(Catch.factorFor(profile))` (so a fruit fly after a house fly, or the reverse, each get their own). A controller never given the game's rules ignores the profile and replays Godot, and `tests/prey.luau` and the goldens did not move (1623 checks before the change, the same ones green after): a scripted 4.5-taps-a-second struggle still ends at the same frame with the same grip to 17 digits. The house fly through the new path agrees at every frame with a controller set up by hand the ROBLOX-72 way, for 40 seeds (`==`, not near). The struggle's arithmetic was moved, not changed: `Feeding:struggle` is the old branch of `step` unchanged but for `drainScale`, `tire` and `cap` standing where `1`, `0.5` and `12` did (multiplying by 1 is exact).

What it feels like (60 prey, taps spaced evenly from the first frame, `tests/prey.luau`). Fruit fly: untouched, it lasts 1.68 s in the jaws before it tears free (the house fly 1.15 s); a steady 2 taps a second wins it every time (the house fly: never); 4 a second wins in 1.0 s (the house fly 4.6 s); two seconds of 5 taps a second and then a pause of three seconds does not lose it (the house fly: loses it every time). It is clearly easier and is still a struggle: 1 tap a second or none tears it free. The house fly, its feel and the 12 s cap are exactly ROBLOX-72's.

The rival. A rival the strike has caught (ROBLOX-73's contest has already decided that: the captured outcome, 0.55 or no draw at all for a rival that had not noticed) is now held but not yet subdued. `RivalSpider` marks the record `subdued = false` at the catch; in that state `step` returns `"struggling"` and does nothing else, so the five-second eating timer does not run and nothing is eaten. The tapping lives in a second controller, `Predator/RivalGrip`: a second `Feeding` instance of which only `struggle` ever runs (never `step`, which would advance the reserves a second time), so the grip maths, the Grip button (the jump button is the tap, as for a fly), the grip bar and the touch "Grip" label are the fly's own. The client reaches it in three places and no new local: `rivalLegs.Grip` and `rivalLegs.grip` in the table that was already there, the jump handler (a tap goes to whichever grip is on), and the HUD's `view.grip` and status line, which say "Holding the rival - tap Space to keep your grip" (the fly's line in the rival's words, from `RivalText.struggle`). The words are the rival's own in `Shared/UI/RivalText`: the catch ("Caught the rival! It fights back. Tap to keep your grip."), subdued ("The rival goes still. Stay still to eat; worth two housefly meals.", the old catch line, now said when it is true) and tearing free ("The rival tore free of your fangs.", "... You are safe in this mode." in the gentle modes). A grip that reaches 1 subdues it (`Rival.subdue`: `subdued = true`) and eating goes on exactly as before (stay still, 0.7, the `eaten` fate, the day stamped, the meal counted, the daily renewal); a grip that reaches 0 means it tears free (`Rival.tearFree`): the rival goes to `recover` where it stands and carries on as the same rival, with its meal (0.7), its contest count and no day or fate, so it keeps its day and no new rival comes early (a new one comes only after an eaten or taken rival, ROBLOX-68). It is not a second contest draw, it costs the spider nothing and never harms it in any mode. Like a fly, the struggle waits up to a second for the spider to land (a strike catches in the air), and Automatic grip adds its 0.4 a second as it does for a fly.

The numbers, and a deviation from the brief that I want Nathan to know about. The brief suggested a difficulty of about 1.8 (faster drain, stronger jerks, taps worth less) and a feel of "a steady 4 taps a second wins in 6 to 9 s, lapses punished, no taps loses in about 2 s". Those cannot both be had by scaling the fly: at 1.8 times the fly's difficulty a tap is worth 0.067 against a drain of about 0.9 a second at the start, so it takes about 10 taps a second to win at all (a simulation outside the suite), and with no taps it tears free in 0.64 s. So the rival has numbers of its own (`Catch.rival`), chosen for the feel that was asked for: start grip 0.6, a tap 0.085 (Godot's, worth 0.7 of the game fly's 0.12), drain 0.5 times, jerks 0.8 as the fly's, the rival tires to 0.75 of its strength over its 6 to 8 s (the fly to 0.5), and no cap. In words: its drain is smaller than the fly's per second (not faster), and what makes it hard is that it asks for more, for longer: about 3.3 taps a second hold it (the house fly: 2.5), and it never gives up. Measured over 60 rivals (taps evenly spaced from 0.2 s, standard rules, `tests/rival.luau`): no taps tears it free after 2.06 s (the house fly 1.15 s); 2 taps a second tears it free every time; 3 a second never subdues it within 40 s (the grip hovers; the fight does not end by itself); 4 a second wins every time in 5.7 to 8.4 s, mean 7.2 s (about 29 taps; the house fly 4.6 s, about 22); 5 a second in 3.3 s, 6 in 2.1 s; a first tap as late as 0.5 s still wins at 4 a second; a one-second pause at 3 s costs 3.8 s more and a pause of three seconds loses it every time. Gentle (Easy, Free) uses the house fly's difficulty (`Catch.forRival(true)` is `Catch.scaled(1)`): no taps tears it free in 1.14 s, 4 a second wins in 5.5 s, 3 a second wins every time, and it still has no cap; it can still be lost by not tapping, and nothing in it harms the player. Automatic grip alone subdues it in 3.8 s in either mode. The 12 s auto-subdue of the fly does not apply: the rival has `cap = math.huge`, so waiting never subdues it (a fly past 12 s is still subdued, pinned). If it is too easy or too hard in play, the dials are those six numbers in `Data/Catch.rival` (a tap of 0.075 makes the threshold about 3.7 taps a second).

Checkpoints: the struggle is never recorded. `Checkpoint.boundaryProblem` already refused a rest while the rival is held (`captured`), as it does for a fly in the jaws; its reason now says "the rival in the jaws is still struggling" while it fights and "the spider is eating its rival" after. So a return can only land at a rest, where the rival is as it was then (a test: a rest, a catch and a fight, a return: it patrols and the old grip lets go). The grip itself is scratch in the client; the one new field in the rival's record is `subdued` (true, false or absent; anything else is refused; absent means eaten as before, so an older record of a held rival is eaten without a fight, and the Studio playback and `tests/wasp.luau`'s hand-set `captured` rivals still pass). A hand-made record of a held rival that is still fighting restores, and whoever finds it begins a fresh grip. The grip's jerk timing is a seeded stream from the world's seed, the day and the rival's contest count (`RivalGrip`, nothing else draws from it), so the same encounter fights the same way; the outcome belongs to the player's taps. A `Feeding` restored from a record made before the `game` flag has none, so the client turns the game's rules on again whenever the flag is not true (one line in the tick).

What deliberately did not change: the contest and its rates, the counter-bite, the rival's patrol, windup and lunge, the wasp's `takesRival` and the wasp, the food (0.7), the daily rival and its rules, Godot's `Feeding` defaults, the house fly's struggle, Easy and Free's counter-bite. A rival that tore free is in `recover` for three seconds, which is still a free catch (ROBLOX-73: a recovering rival is caught outright), so a player who strikes again at once catches it again, to fight again; I did not add a rule against that (the second fight is as hard as the first, and it costs the player the time and the risk of the windup that follows). Tests: `tests/prey.luau` (the factor, its limits and monotonic rise, `scaled(1)` equal to the game's numbers, the Godot controller ignoring profiles, the house fly bit for bit, the capture-time scaling both ways, the fruit fly's feel), `tests/rival.luau` (the numbers; held and not eaten; subdued then eaten with exactly 0.7 after five seconds; the feel above; no cap; tearing free keeping its day, meal and count and no early respawn; harmless in both modes; determinism; records and the old record; the words), `tests/rivalnav.luau` (the drive keeps it in the jaws through a fight), `tests/client_rival.luau` (the real client: the catch line, the status line, the grip bar, taps through the Space action that do not start a jump, subdued, eaten for 0.7, tore free to recover, the gentle line), `tests/checkpoint.luau` (no rest while it fights, a return lands at the rest, the flag validated). Updated deliberately, because they pinned "captured then eating": the first check and `eat()` in `tests/rival.luau`, the held rival in `tests/rivalnav.luau`, the catch line, and `tests/client/SilkPlayback.luau` (the Studio playback now taps until subdued before it waits for the meal). Not run in Studio or on a device: the feel under a real finger or a thumb on the touch Grip button (a long fight at 4 taps a second is about 29 taps, which may be tiring on a phone; the gentle modes are the house fly's), the Studio playback, and whether 7 seconds of holding a rival is the right length of drama.

### ROBLOX-78 - The food bar is a hunger bar, and hunger runs faster (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "food bar should be hunger bar and deplete faster." Two changes, one for the words and one for the number.

The words. The bar at the top of the screen was "Food 72%", the spider's energy reserve. It is now "Hunger 72%", with the Minecraft convention: the bar is full when the spider is fed, empties as it grows hungry and is filled by eating. Nothing underneath moved: it is the same energy value (`needs.energy`, 0 to 1) as before, so the bar, the checkpoint, the meals, the notebook's thresholds (hungry at 0.25, well fed at 0.85) and every save are exactly as they were. The label's words moved out of the HUD drawer into `Shared/UI/LifeText.hunger(fed, gentle)`, where the other player-facing life words live: "Hunger 100%", then " · hungry" at a quarter or less, " · starving" at zero and " · gentle" in the gentle modes (which never say starving). The Studio instances are renamed to match (`HungerBar`, `HungerFill`, `HungerText`; the Studio playback looks for them), the adult growth line "Adult: keep your food reserves up." now says "Adult: keep your hunger bar full.", and the README's line about the bar is changed. Words that stay: the status lines already spoke of hunger ("Hungry: time to hunt.", "Starving: ..."), the death cause "It went too long without food." (starved means no food, and the brief said it can stay), and the biology in the notebook, where "food" is what the spider eats and not a bar. The one notebook line that described the bar, the "In this game" note of the page "Going without food", now says "The hunger bar, full when your spider is fed, empties in about two days" (see the numbers); the page is therefore one more page with a Roblox line of its own, and Godot's line is kept as its `godotGame` (the notebook test's list of adapted pages gained it).

The number. Hunger drains in life mode at 0.021 energy per waking hour and 0.007 per sleeping hour (`NeedsModel.LIFE_DRAIN_*`), times a factor. Godot's factor is 1, and the golden fixtures (`life_reference.json`, the continuing and growth references) are replayed at 1; ROBLOX-61 had given the game 1.1 as a constant the client copied into the model (`PLAY_DRAIN`). The math for a day: the spider is awake from 07:00 to its evening rest and sleeps the rest of the night, about 11 waking hours and 13 asleep, so a day costs 11 x 0.021 + 13 x 0.007 = 0.231 + 0.091 = 0.322 at factor 1; at 1.1 that is 0.354 (the 0.35 to 0.38 of ROBLOX-68, which allowed for a later bedtime). To make a day cost about half the reserve the factor is 0.5 / 0.322 = 1.553, and the game's factor is now 1.55: a day costs 0.499. The test simulates a whole day of 11 and 13 hours from a full reserve and finds 0.4990 spent. The factor lives where the other game rules do: `NeedsModel.game = { drain = 1.55 }`, applied by a new `NeedsModel:useGameRules()` that sets the model's `drainScale`, as `Data/Wasp.game` is applied by `WaspDirector:useGameRules()` and `Data/Catch.game` by `Feeding:useGameRules()` (ROBLOX-67, 72). The client calls it where it used to copy the constant (three places: creation, a restore and a change of rules); `NeedsModel.PLAY_DRAIN` stays as the same number under its old name. A model that is never given the game's rules still has `drainScale` nil, which is 1, so nothing golden moved: no fixture, no reference and no default was edited. The gentle modes keep their own half-rate modifier and their floor of 0.1, now on top of the faster factor (a gentle day costs 0.25).

What it does to the game, for Nathan. A full reserve lasts two days, not 2.8; a housefly (0.35) is now 0.7 of a day (it was 1.0) and a fruit fly 0.35; the three houseflies that make a moult's 0.9 are worth 1.05 energy, a little over two days of living. Two flies a day (0.7) is now the comfortable pace, and one fly a day is a slow decline (0.15 a day short). The daily rival (0.7, ROBLOX-68) was food for two days and could be eaten every day with a surplus of 0.35; it is now about a day and a half (1.4 days) and a surplus of 0.2, so it no longer trivializes starvation by itself: a player who ignores the flies and wins the rival's hard fight (ROBLOX-77) every day still cannot starve, but they can no longer skip a day, and the reserve cap of 1.0 wastes less of what it gives. The starving clock (weak after 24 hours at zero, death at 48, `LifeLoop`) is in game hours and untouched, so a spider with no food at all lasts about four days from a full reserve, two to empty and two more to die, instead of nearly five. The dial is the one number `NeedsModel.game.drain`: 1.1 is the old pace, 1.0 is Godot's, and every 0.1 moves a day's cost by 0.032. Not changed: the thresholds, the meal values, the moult's food, the starving hours, the checkpoint (the factor is not saved; a restore applies the game's again), Easy and Free's rules, or the rest bar, which drains and restores by its own rates.

Tests: `tests/life.luau` (the factor and the day's cost worked from the constants; a model starts with Godot's rules and only `useGameRules` changes it; exactly 1.55 times the drain awake, asleep, standard and gentle; a whole simulated day from a full reserve costs 0.5; the two-meals arithmetic; `LifeText.hunger` at full, quarter, empty, gentle and out of range, and no "food" or "energy" in it), `tests/hud.luau` (the real drawer: the bar is named for hunger and no `FoodBar` remains, the fill is the reserve, the words at fed, hungry, starving and gentle, large text, hidden without a reserve, no "food" in any label on the screen), `tests/notebook.luau` (the adapted page). The goldens, `life_reference.json`, `growth_reference.json` and `continuing_reference.json` did not change. Not run in Studio or on a device: how a day at 0.5 feels in play (two flies a day is about one meal in the morning and one in the afternoon of a 15 minute day), and the bar's look.

### ROBLOX-79 - The mating cycle: a mate, courtship, the egg sac, the guard days and the next generation, in the shared sim (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan: "We need a mating cycle to complete the playthrough." The agreed design ends a life in the eggs hatching, the spiderlings ballooning away on silk, an ending card and a notebook page, and the player carrying on as one of the offspring: a new generation on the same sill and in the same save. This entry is the simulation and its records, all in `Shared` so the CLI drives whole cycles (`tests/mating.luau`); ROBLOX-80 is the client, the scene, the ending and the notebook page. Nothing in the game calls this code until ROBLOX-80, so this change alone changes nothing in play.

The pieces. `Data/MatingRules` holds every number (the dials). `Predator/Mate` is the mate's body and the courtship meter (`life.mate`, a plain record like the rival's). `Life/Mating` is the cycle (`life.mating`, a plain record) and `life.generation` is a plain number; all three sit on the `life` root that the checkpoint already records wholesale (ObjectRecord keeps any plain field, so none can be forgotten), and `Mating.tick(life, dt, snapshot)` is the one call that moves them, from the same kind of snapshot of the player that `RivalSpider.step` takes. `Predator/MateDrive` is its legs.

Eligibility. The spider is full-grown, which is the last of Growth's four stages (young juvenile, juvenile, subadult, adult: `Growth.ADULT`, `isAdult()`), and well fed, energy at least 0.6 (`MatingRules.energyMin`; the hunger bar at 60%, which after ROBLOX-78's day of 0.5 is a fed spider and not merely a living one), at a rest in the crack. `Mating.atRest` is the test; it sets `ready_day` to the day of the next 07:00 (tomorrow for a rest from 07:00 on, today for one before it) and never clears or moves it. It must be asked before the rest's checkpoint is made, or a return to that rest would forget it: the client's `life.save` asks it first (ROBLOX-80), and the tests' do the same. A rest at 0.59, or by a juvenile, wants no mate; a cycle already going sets nothing.

Arrival is the rival's rule (ROBLOX-68), shared by value with `RivalRules` in a test: not on day 1, from 07:00 (the spider's waking hour), not while the spider rests, not while the wasp visits, not while the spider is within 8 studs of where it would arrive. It also needs the cycle to want it (stage none, `ready_day` reached, not the day it was last driven off or left) and it is not begun from 16:00 (a visit not begun by then is for tomorrow). One mate at a time: while one is on the sill none arrives. It arrives at the east end of the floor (26, 0.31, 3), the rival's end; the crack is at the west end. It does not count as prey: no fly state, no food, no capture, no grip, nothing to eat, and it is not a target for anything. The wasp ignores it, on purpose and for scope: the wasp's director takes `RivalSpider.target` and nothing else, and teaching it a second creature would change a director with a replay against Godot's fixtures and ROBLOX-70/75's draws for a feature that is not about the wasp (a test checks the wasp's two visits on a day a mate is present, that the mate stands still for each as the rival does, and that the rival arrives as ever).

The mate's look is the played spider's own profile, scaled: the mate is the opposite sex of the played spider. The peacock spider is the male and the ant mimic the female; the first spider has no sex in its design (it is the daughter's pattern, "inspired by the bold jumping spider"), and I made it the game's female, so that its mate is a male as the rival is; that is a judgment call and `Mating.sexOf` is the one place to change it. A male mate is 0.9 of the look and a female mate 1.15, each varying by up to 5% (`Mate.scaleFor`, a seeded draw from the world's seed and the mate's number, so the same mate has the same size on any return). If the player changes spider in the Settings to the mate's sex, it leaves.

Legs. The mate walks on the rival's own infrastructure and not a new one: `MateDrive` gives `RivalDrive` (unchanged, as is `RivalRoute`) a record in the rival's vocabulary, so the mate has its own `SpiderMotor` over the whole sill, the keep-out around the crack and the same route graph. Wander is the rival's patrol (0.8 wu/s, about 100 places over every face, a different first place each day); approach is its stalk toward where it last saw the spider (1.1 wu/s; it notices a spider within 8 wu in clear sight, never one in the crack); attend and the pause together are its windup posture (it stands, facing the spider); leaving is a patrol whose one place is the exit, the floor waypoint in the back corner of the east end (26.4, 0.31, -10.84). It is a wrapper and not a mode of `RivalDrive` because the rival's drive is tied to the rival's states and a hostile mode would have put the rival's code at risk; `tests/rivalnav.luau` did not change and passes. The mate is gone within 1.5 wu of the exit or 120 s after it starts leaving, whichever is first. A wasp visit holds it, its timers and its meter still, as it holds the rival.

Courtship. In the states wander, approach and attend, each tick the meter fills by 1/8 (`courtSeconds` 8) when the spider is within 3.0 wu of the mate (`nearWu`), in clear sight of it, facing it (the mate within 40 degrees of where the spider looks, in the plane the spider stands on), standing still (attached, not moving, not on a line, not in the air) and, for the peacock spider only, dancing (`s.needsDance`, `s.dancing`: the dance, which until now was a pose for nothing, is what the peacock owes the female). It drains at 0.25 a second, twice as fast (4 s from full), when any of the other conditions fails; while the dance is the only thing missing it holds. Jumping at the mate (airborne within 2 wu) or striking it (the strike's sweep within 0.65 wu, as the rival is caught) drives it off: it walks away for the day, the meter empty, and `away_day` stamps today, so it returns from 07:00 the next morning. The three mornings: a mate comes at most three mornings (`visitsMax`), then the next arrival is another mate (`mates` counts them; a new size, the meter new) on the following morning, and so on for ever; the count is only to give a stubborn player a fresh start. Resting sends the mate away unseen for the day (the simplest sound rule, since a rest is the player leaving the sill for the night; the meter starts again tomorrow); a rest during the pause together lets the mating finish first. At 18:00 it leaves.

Mating. When the meter fills the pair are together for 5 s (`pairSeconds`; stage `courted`, the mate in state `pair`), one caption in the client and nothing else: the game is for children and nature, and shows nothing. Then the stage is `mated` and the mate walks off the sill and does not come again. The sac: after mating, the next sleep in the crack that reaches the morning (LifeLoop's `woke`, `Mating.woke`) makes it. The stage is `sac` on that morning (guard day 1), `guarding` on the next (guard day 2), and the eggs hatch on the morning after that, 07:00 on the third morning, by the clock (`stage` `hatched`, with `hatch_day`, `hatch_hour` and a `brood` of 8 to 12 spiderlings from a seeded draw of the world's seed, the generation and the day, so a return never rerolls it). A spider that stays up through the night is not asleep: the sac waits for a sleep, and the days are counted from the 07:00 it appears. Guarding is surviving: the sac is in the crack, which the wasp and the rival never enter, and nothing in v1 can destroy it; the player must live (eat, rest in the crack at night) through two days.
