Gentle modes are identical. It is the same sac for a male player, which bends the biology (see the notebook page in ROBLOX-80): the game follows one sill and one family.

The next generation (`Mating.carryOn`, which the client calls when the player takes the ending): `generation` goes up by one (a new field of the life, absent and so 0 in an older record), growth is the first stage again, energy is the fresh-start 0.55, rest is full, the starving books are cleared and the food ledger cursor is level; the day count and the hour go on (the sill's time is not reset), the notebook, the appearance and the settings are outside the life and untouched, the rival, the wasp and the world go on, and the mating state and the mate are new, so the cycle can happen again. A brood hatches only once and the player's life, in every other sense, is that of a spiderling.

Records. `Mating.problems` and `Mate.valid` refuse a malformed record: an unknown stage, a counter that is not a whole number from 0, more than three mornings for a mate or more guard days than there are, a sac without a morning, a guarded sac before its second day, a hatching with no day or no brood, a meter outside 0 to 1, an unknown sex or reason, a scale outside 0.5 to 2, a position that is not a number, outside the sill or in the crack. `Mating.problemsWith` also refuses the combinations that cannot be: a mate on the sill during the sac and the guarding, `courted` with the pair not together and the reverse. `life.generation` must be a whole number from 0 to a million. All fields are optional in a record: a record from before has none of them and restores as stage none, no mate and generation 0 (the restore clears the live fields, ObjectRecord's commit, and the next tick makes new ones). `Checkpoint.boundaryProblem` refuses a rest while the brood is hatching ("the brood is hatching"), since the ending is the player's to take and a rest there would keep a family that has already hatched; a restore of a record already in the stage hatched is valid (the client shows the ending at once). A restore never rerolls: the mate's size, every arrival, the brood and the hatch day are functions of the world's seed, the day and numbers in the record, and the test runs a sleep to the sac, returns to the rest and runs it again, comparing the events and the record of the cycle at every sample.

Tests (`tests/mating.luau`, 545 checks): the numbers and their agreement with the rival's rules; the sexes and the sizes of 120 mates; the eligibility gate (a juvenile, 0.59, 0.6, a rest by day and at 03:00, never cleared, a cycle already going); every arrival rule one at a time (day 1, before 07:00, from 16:00, the wasp, 7.9 and 8.1 wu, a rest, not wanted, a cycle going, the day it left) and the sex of a male player's mate; the meter (half full in 4 s, 0.25 a second lost moving, looking away, 3.5 wu, in the crack; the peacock's dance needed, held without it; a wasp visit holding it); the drive-off by a strike and by a jump at it, not by a jump 5 wu away, the return, the three mornings and the new mate; a whole cycle from the first rest on day 2 through the morning mate of day 3, the courtship (full after 8 s, the pause 5 s), the mating, the sac night, guard days on days 4 and 5, the hatching on day 6 with its brood, the carrying on and a second cycle in generation 2; rests and the evening (a rest in the courtship, a rest in the pause, 18:00, the 120 s walk, a change of sex, a rest through the morning); the rival and the wasp on a day a mate is present; the legs on the box world (it walks, notices a spider in plain sight 6 studs off and comes to about 2 wu, stands facing it, is courted by a spider standing still, mates, walks the whole way to the exit and is gone, never in the crack, stands still for a wasp visit, ignores a spider in the crack); the real checkpoint (a return repeats a sleep to the sac exactly, a mate in a rest comes back with its meter, an older record restores as nothing going on, seven kinds of bad cycle record refused with nothing changed, every stage restores, no rest while the brood is out).

What deliberately does not happen: no second spider ever enters the retreat (the mate and the rival share the keep-out and the retreat guard); nothing can destroy, eat or harm the sac, and the wasp does not take the mate or the sac; there is no cannibalism, no harm to the mate in either direction, and the mate cannot kill the spider; no claim that a male guards eggs (the guard days are the player's, whoever the player is; see ROBLOX-80's page); no second mate in a cycle; and nothing has run in Studio or on a device (the walk of the mate on the real sill, its look and the feel of eight seconds of standing still are Nathan's to judge; the dials are all in `Data/MatingRules`).

### ROBLOX-80 - The mating cycle in the client: the mate, the courtship meter, the egg sac, the hatching, the ending card, the next generation and the notebook page (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan's design, whose simulation is ROBLOX-79: the life ends in the eggs hatching, the spiderlings ballooning away on silk, an ending card and a notebook page, and the player carries on as one of the offspring. This entry is everything the player sees and touches; nothing in it changes what is true (the cycle, its numbers and its records are `Life/Mating`, `Predator/Mate` and `Data/MatingRules`), and none of it has run in Studio.

The mate is drawn with the rival's path: a `SpiderRig` of the played spider's own look at the "low" tier (`Client/MateStage.drawMate`), put on the mate's pose each frame with the same `SupportMath.bodyCFrame` the rival uses, made when the mate arrives and destroyed when it is off the sill. The size is the adult scale (Growth.SCALES, 1.5) times the mate's own factor from ROBLOX-79 (male 0.9, female 1.15, each varied up to 5% by a seeded draw from the mate's number), through a new `SpiderRig.setScale(g)` that `setGrowthStage` now calls (the same scaling, any size; the stage numbers and every existing look are unchanged). A peacock spider's mate is therefore drawn with the male's colours although the real female is plain brown; the notebook page says so. Its legs are `MateDrive` over the rival's drive, made in the client when it first arrives, with a `SpiderMotor` of its own like the rival's; the mate is in the spider's percepts as a moving thing (`Mate.percept`), never prey.

The courtship meter is a second bar in the hud column in the grip bar's style (`CourtshipBar` and `Courtship` in `SpiderHud`: 65% wide, 12 px high, the grip bar's place in the column, a dark rose track and a rose fill (226, 128, 164) so it is never mistaken for the grip's red). The view carries `court` (0 to 1) while a mate can be courted and is within the 6 studs it keeps attention to, or while the meter has anything in it. A status line says what the meter wants (`MateText.courtLine`): far, "A visitor is on the sill. Walk close to it." (within 14 studs); near, "Courting 40%: face it and keep still"; the peacock spider before it dances, "Courting: dance in front of it (X)", with the key of the device in use from `ControlText` (X, RB, R1 on a PlayStation pad, the touch label "Dance"). The pause together has one caption (`[a faint tremble in the silk]`, through the Captions setting), the guard days a line ("Guarding the eggs: day 1 of 2. Rest in the crack to pass the nights."), and the day line says "| Generation 2" from the second family on (nothing for the first).

`Shared/UI/MateText` holds every word: a notice for each beat of `Mating.tick` (arrived, with the mate's sex and for the peacock spider the dance key; noticed; driven; courted; mated; gone for dusk, a rest or the wrong sex; the sac; the second guard day; the hatching), the status lines, the captions, the card, the toast. The game is for children: nothing is shown or said of the mating but that the two spiders stay together a moment.

The egg sac is a silk sphere of the rules' size (`MatingRules.sacRadius` 0.42, so 0.84 studs across) at `MatingRules.sacAt` in the crack's north-west corner, held by three thin threads, 4 parts in all; it is drawn from the morning the sac is made until the family carries on (`Mating.hasSac`), is anchored, non-collidable, non-queryable, non-touchable and casts no shadow, like ROBLOX-74's decor and the draglines, so a sight cast, a jump probe or the spider's feet pass through it. It is in the rest's checkpoint: `life.save` now makes the cycle's two small steps before it records (`Mating.atRest`, so a rest by a full-grown, well-fed spider sets the mate coming; and the `woke` events that are pending, so the sac the night made is in the morning's record and a return never loses a night); the life-event drain then says the sac's notice.

The hatching is a scene of 26 seconds (`MateStage.SCENE_SECONDS`) that begins on the `hatched` event, in the crack, when the eggs hatch while the player is there or not. A seeded handful of spiderlings, the brood (8 to 12) on the full detail tier and at most 4 of it on the low tier (`countFor`), each three plain parts (a body ball 0.14 across, a head ball and, when ballooning, a thread), so at most 36 parts and the sac's 4 on full, 12 and 4 on low. `MateStage.plan(seed, generation, day, count)` draws each one's numbers from the world's seed, the family's number, the day it hatched and its own number (so a scene is the same scene however often it is seen and nothing is rolled while it plays), and `MateStage.pose(plan, t, reduced)` is a pure function of them and the clock: the first comes out of the sac at 0.6 s and the others 0.3 s apart (each with up to 0.3 s more), wanders on the crack's floor within its reach (0.6 to 2.0 studs from the sac, 0.18 to 0.38 studs a second, on a gently bending path; straight with Reduced Motion on), and then balloons one by one: the first at 8 s and the last by 20.5 s, each standing 1.2 s with its thread coming out and rising 4 s on it, 2.4 studs, slowly at first (a smoothstep), drifting a little with the air and fading out over the last 40% of the lift, gone before 26 s. The sac turns more translucent over the first 2 s. The scene is played by the render step with the simulation's frame time (a menu, a notebook or the Roblox menu holds it; slow motion slows it) and does not touch the camera or the controls: `modal()` is not set, W walks the spider away (and ends its rest), the notebook opens. Captions (`[tiny rustling from the egg sac]`, then `[silk threads lift the spiderlings away]`) follow the Captions setting.

The ending card is `CaughtPanel.showChoices`, the death screen's own panel, with one button, "Carry on": the title "The sill continues", "9 spiderlings hatched on day 6, climbed into the light and floated away on silk, one by one." and "You were generation 1. You carry on as one of them, small again, in the same crack on the same sill." It comes when the scene ends (faded in), and at once, with no scene, for a record restored into the hatching (a foreign or older record: `Checkpoint.boundaryProblem` refuses to keep a rest in the hatched stage, ROBLOX-79, so the client's own never has one), and not over another screen. The notebook page `eggs_and_wind` ("Eggs, silk and wind") opens at the hatching.

Carry on (`mateLegs.carryOn` in the client, `Mating.carryOn` for the rules): generation + 1, growth back to the first stage, a fresh reserve (0.55), the cycle and the mate new, the sac gone; the rig is built again by the same function the spider choice uses (so the first growth stage, the same look), the spider is placed in the crack at (-25.5, 0.31, 6), the hud comes back, the sensor and the memory start clean, the camera snaps, and a toast reads "Generation 2 — hatched on day 6". The days of the sill go on (the clock is not reset), and the notebook, the look and the settings are the player's own and are kept. No checkpoint is made at the moment of carrying on (the first rest of the new generation makes it); a player who leaves in the scene or at the card comes back to the last rest, the second guard day's, and sees the hatching again the next morning. The `fresh()` of a new life and the return to a checkpoint both call `mateLegs.reset()`, so nothing of a scene or a card outlives them.

The notebook page. `eggs_and_wind`, trigger `hatched` (a new trigger, the third the Roblox game added after `dance` and `antennae`, opened by the client as `dance` is, not by the watcher), is in `tools/gen_notebook_pages.py` with the other two pages the game added, so the generated `NotebookPages.luau` (CRLF, as it was) and the generator agree; the generator also now holds the `going_without_food` note ROBLOX-78 had edited by hand, so regenerating no longer undoes it. Its text is 487 characters (the limit is 520) and says only what its sources support; the sources are in the file's exact style and `docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md` says what was checked of each: Jackson (1978) J. Arachnol. 6: 1-29 (the PDF was opened: the female's silken nest, she stays until the spiderlings hatch and leave, eggs hatching about three weeks after laying (21.7 days) and spiderlings leaving about three weeks after hatching (20.3), about 208 eggs over one to five batches), Foelix (2011) (the general reference for the one sentence not in Jackson, that jumping spiders court by sight and the female decides; **not consulted for this page, the weakest claim on it**), Bell, Bohan, Shaw & Weyman (2005) Bull. Entomol. Res. 95(2): 69-114 (the record only: its abstract was not available and the paper not read), and Morley & Robert (2018) Curr. Biol. 28(14): 2324-2330 (the abstract: spiders climb to a high point, release silk and float away; an electric field triggered take-off). The game note says where the game departs: a game day of about fifteen minutes squeezes weeks into days; the real mother makes and guards the sac, here the sac is the same for either spider you play and no claim is made that a male guards eggs; a real brood is far larger than eight to twelve (about 200 in the study); the first spider's sex is the game's choice; the peacock spider's mate is drawn with the male's colours although the real female is plain brown; nothing can harm the sac; and the player carries on as one of the spiderlings, which a real one could not choose. The peacock page's game note used to say there is no mating simulation, which is no longer true; it now says the dance is how you court a mate. The notebook's checklist has a new line for the page.

Tests. `tests/client_mating.luau` (12 checks, about two minutes: the long waits run in frames of 0.1 s): the words for every beat and the button names by device including a PlayStation pad; the plan (deterministic, ordered, within the numbers above, capped 12 and 4) and the pose (every state in order, on the crack's floor, only rising and fading, a straight walk under Reduced Motion, gone by 26 s); the stage's parts (display parts only, the part counts, the tiers, a finished scene, reset); then the real client through a whole cycle on the box world: a rest by an adult at 0.8 sets the mate coming, it arrives on the next morning and is drawn at the right size and where it stands, walks the sill and never enters the crack, courting fills the bar in the hud in about eight seconds (and walking drains it), the pair are together about five seconds with their caption, the mate walks off, the next night's sleep makes the sac (drawn, non-queryable, in the checkpoint, with the guard line), the second guard day, the hatching with the page opened and the spiderlings out, walking during the scene, the threads and the lift, the captions, the card with its one button, Carry on (generation 1, the first stage, the toast, the day line, the notebook kept, a rest and a checkpoint of the new generation, a return that brings it back) and a record restored into the hatching going straight to the card and a second carrying on counting up; and the page's citations and game note. `tests/hud.luau` (86 checks) gained the courtship bar; `tests/notebook.luau` (852) the page count (24 of Godot's and the game's three), the stable ids, the trigger list and the page's own checks, the peacock note, and the client-owned `hatched` trigger.

What deliberately does not happen: no second spider ever enters the retreat (the mate and the rival share the keep-out; the spiderlings are drawn, not simulated, and are only ever in the crack); no egg destruction, no predation on the sac, the wasp never takes the mate or the sac; the wasp and the rival are untouched; no claim that a male guards eggs; no cannibalism and no harm to the mate; the scene is not skippable and has no sound (the soundscape is untouched); the spiderlings have no legs (a body and a head, tiny); the scene is not saved and the camera is not directed. Nothing has run in Studio: the spiderlings, the sac and the mate's scale are shown in the CLI against the box world and the shim, never on the engine's renderer.

Addendum (2026-10-19), the audit's changes to the page. Its courtship sentence now reads "Jumping spiders court mostly by sight, and often by vibration too", where it said "court by sight"; "laboratory" became "lab", which keeps the text inside the limit (515 of 520 characters); and the game note says the real peacock female is "mottled grey-brown", where this entry and the first version of the page said "plain brown". The sources list is as it was (four), and `docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md` records what was checked of each, including that Jackson (1978) was opened for the page and that Foelix (2011) still was not.

### ROBLOX-81 - The tarantula pack: the Mexican redknee and the Greenbottle blue, two drawn costumes on the first spider's body (Nathan, Perplexity, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan asked for a tarantula pack: two playable tarantula appearances, as cosmetic as the peacock spider and the ant mimic. The design was written by Perplexity, another AI on his team, and brought by Nathan; this entry is its Roblox adaptation, and where the design contradicted the codebase the codebase won (below). The pack is `brachypelma_hamorii` ("the Mexican redknee", Brachypelma hamorii) and `chromatopelma_cyaneopubescens` ("the Greenbottle blue", Chromatopelma cyaneopubescens), both with `pack = "tarantula_pack"` in `Data/Appearances`, both in `SpiderChoice.ORDER` after the ant mimic, so the crack's key, the Settings row and the controller's RB offer five spiders with no further wiring (the controls card says "five spiders"; `PeacockText.NAMES` gives "the Mexican redknee" and "the Greenbottle blue", short enough for the row's button). The pose is ROBLOX-82 and the notebook page ROBLOX-83; this entry is only what the spiders look like.

The colours are Perplexity's, as hex values (`Data/Appearances`' small `hex()` turns them into the table's 0 to 1 triples; `tests/look.luau` pins each one against the drawn part). Redknee: velvet black-brown; a broad low carapace whose dark centre is #1A1614 with a copper-orange rim #D67638; a plump abdomen #161312 with a ginger-rust patch #BA5028 at its dorsal rear; flame-orange patellae #EE5E22 (the knees, and the band on each pedipalp), a warm pale-orange band #E08642 on each tibia, dark velvety metatarsus and tarsus #181413, near-black femurs. Greenbottle: a metallic blue-green carapace #24767C (Reflectance 0.08) with a lighter turquoise rim and chelicerae #308E94; a dark-sapphire tubercle; cobalt legs #204AA8 (Reflectance 0.06) with brighter azure joint rings #2E62C4 (the knees and ankles) and, at full detail, pale golden-tan bristles #DAA86C; a vivid tiger-orange abdomen #E46226 with a dark midline and four pairs of diagonal bars, both #2A1C18. Shapes (all ART): a carapace about twice as wide as it is high (the first spider's head is 1.26 times); a small ocular tubercle standing out of it with eight tiny glossy eye beads (radii 0.015 to 0.021 studs, none of the salticid's big front pair, no sockets) and one catchlight on the first bead of each side, so two; chelicerae 1.3 times the first spider's, set wide, parallel, pointing forward and down; pedipalps 1.45 times as long and thicker; the redknee's legs 1.35 times the first spider's thickness (the greenbottle's 1.15), the body low (`bodyDrop` 0.09) and the reach no longer than the first spider's; two spinnerets of two segments behind the abdomen.

Where the codebase won. The size: a real tarantula's body is five or six centimetres, five times the first spider's, and would not fit the crack or the collider, which a costume must not change; so both are drawn at the first spider's size and through its growth stages (`look.scale` absent, so 1 to 1.5 times at the last stage; `SpiderRig:setScale` and the mate unchanged) and the notebook page (ROBLOX-83) says so. "Velvet" is carried by colour alone: every part is `SmoothPlastic` like the other spiders ("clean high-contrast silhouette"), because how `Enum.Material.Fabric` renders on a stud-sized part is only Studio's to say. The abdomen markings are flat dabs tilted to the slope under them (as the peacock's carapace stripes are), not decals. The mate (ROBLOX-79) is the played look scaled, at the low tier, so it is a tarantula too; `Mating.sexOf` is untouched, so a tarantula is the game's female as the first spider is, and its mate a male.

The schema, extended minimally and all optional, so the first three spiders' tables and their pinned look are as they were: `pack`; `tubercle` (a mound and its eight eyes, which `Anatomy.layout` places on the mound's surface in place of the head's eyes; `Layout.tubercle`, and the rig draws the mound as `OcularTubercle`); `marks` (abdomen dabs in their own colours: `mirror`, `fullOnly`, `bar`); `legColors` (a colour per leg segment and joint, where the patella is the knee ball) and `legBands` (a colour per banded segment: the redknee's one tibia band, the greenbottle's none; absent means the first spider's three white bands); `legGloss` and `outline` (the highlight's colour); `barFade`; `extras` gain `fullOnly` and `sweep` (the spinnerets, which ROBLOX-82's pose swings); `fuzz` gains `legsOnly` and `legTone` (bristles on the legs and nowhere else, all one tone: `Setae.body` and `Setae.palp` give nothing, `Setae.leg` one tone, and a leg-hair class whose `every` is 0 is now dropped explicitly instead of by a NaN). The rig's eye socket line no longer assumes a big front eye. The rim is an `extras` entry (`CarapaceRim`, a wider, flatter ellipsoid under the dark carapace so a ring of it shows round the edge) and takes the body gloss.

Part budgets (full detail at most 128, low and touch at most 80, counted by `tests/look.luau`): Mexican redknee 88 at full detail and 78 at low; Greenbottle blue 124 at full detail (90 parts and 34 leg bristles) and 78 at low (the second spinneret segments, two of the midline's three dabs, the foot balls and the bristles go at low). For comparison the first spider is 624 and 266, the peacock 119 and 80, the ant mimic 79 and 69. The greenbottle's bars soften at the last growth stage: the client already calls `rig:setGrowthStage` every frame, so `barFade` ({0, 0, 0, 0.6}) sets the bars' transparency by stage with no redrawing (the juvenile pattern fading; the dark midline and the redknee's patch stay); the mate, which `MateStage` puts at the adult stage, shows the faded bars.

Ownership: both are free, like the peacock spider and the ant mimic (ROBLOX-66). `SpiderChoice.locked` (ROBLOX-71's single hook) still returns false, its comment now names the pack, and `SpiderChoice.packOf(id)` says which pack a spider is in, so a later sale can ask the pack and not the spider. Nathan's default is both tarantulas in one pass of about US$0.99; whether each is sold on its own is his open call, and nothing here decides it. Nothing is sold or checked.

What deliberately does not change: the difficulty, the collider, the motor, the speed, the jump and its math, the camera, the senses and the spider-eye view, the dragline, the wasp, the rival, the hunger and the growth, the saved record (the appearance id is 29 characters at most and `goodId` already takes it) and the first three spiders' looks (their pinned tables and part counts). No pose yet: until ROBLOX-82 the tarantulas stand and walk like the first spider, and the display key does nothing for them. Nothing has run in Studio: how the dark colours read under the sill's lighting, the carapace rim, the tubercle's eyes at five studs and the bars' fade are Nathan's to judge, and every size is a dial in `Data/Appearances`. Tests: `tests/look.luau` (the colours, the proportions, the tubercle, both tiers' parts, the bars by stage, the scale, standing on the floor, the eye view; 816 checks), and the pinned three-spider cycle is now five in `tests/peacock.luau`, `tests/antmimic.luau`, `tests/settings.luau`, `tests/client_ant.luau` and `tests/client_smoke.luau`.

### ROBLOX-82 - The tarantulas' threat display and silk mat: a 6 second drawn loop on the display key (Nathan, Perplexity, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Decided. Perplexity's brief for the two tarantulas of ROBLOX-81 asked for two things the costume alone could not give: the rear-up threat posture and the silk mat. Both are one pose on the display key (X, RB / R1, the touch button, which says "Rear up" for these two spiders), the third use of the key after the peacock's dance (ROBLOX-29) and the ant mimic's raised antennae (ROBLOX-64), built the same way: `Shared/Rig/TarantulaPose.luau` is a pure, Vector3-free state machine that the rig reads (`rig.threat`), `Shared/Data/Tarantula.luau` holds every number, `Shared/UI/TarantulaText.luau` the words, and `SpiderChoice.displayKind` says "threat" for a spider whose profile has a `threat` table (the new `SpiderChoice.hasThreat`).

The loop, 6 seconds, in order: rear (0.5 s: the prosoma pitches up 32 degrees about the waist, legs I and the pedipalps lift), threat (2.2 s: held, legs I trembling by a hundredth of a stud or so at 2.2 Hz), settle (0.5 s: back down), turn (1.2 s: the drawn body pivots about its up axis to 180 degrees while the spinnerets swing side to side), mat (0.4 s: held turned about, the swing going on) and return (1.2 s: back to 0 as the swing dies away). Every level is 0 at both ends of the loop, so it repeats with no jump, and it repeats until the player ends it. The spinnerets swing 25 degrees either side at 1.4 Hz (the second segment 1.4 times as far); each pedipalp lifts 55 degrees at its base; legs I go up and out in front of the reared prosoma (`threat` in the profile: where the foot goes, how far the knee bows out and up). The rig does it with transforms only: the prosoma (the head, the tubercle, the palps and every leg's hip) is the body frame pitched about the waist, the whole drawn body is yawed for the turn, and the legs' planted feet are never touched, so the feet keep their spots and the legs come back to the motor when the pose ends. All of it is nothing but a pose: the root part stays at the collider's frame.

Any walk input, a jump aim, Cancel (Q), a rest, a drop, a menu, leaving the surface or the key again ends it at once (the same standing test as the dance); the pose then eases out over a quarter of a second from where it was, and a pose started after that ramps in over a tenth of a second. The pose is stepped by the simulation's time, so slow motion slows it as it slows everything and a menu holds it. ReducedMotion halves every angle and stops the tremble; the posture still shows. A press of the key while walking or in the air is refused with "Stand still on a surface to rear up".

Where the codebase won, and what is honestly art. The brief wanted a threat posture and a silk-laying turn; no paper gives these angles, so unlike the peacock's dance (Girard et al. 2011) and the ant mimic's walk (Shamble et al. 2017) every number in `Data/Tarantula` is marked ART, and the file says so. What the brief calls the display is a costume on a jumping spider's body: the real posture raises the whole front of a large animal on its hind legs and shows the fangs; here the chelicerae stay where ROBLOX-81 put them and the prosoma rears. A real tarantula lays silk by drawing its spinnerets over the ground; here the spinnerets sweep on the spot and no silk is laid (the dragline and the silk the game has are the motor's, and a mat would be new game state, which a pose is not). The turn about is the brief's, kept: it is the part that shows the spinnerets.

What deliberately does not change: the difficulty, the motor, the collider, the speed, the jump and its math, the camera, the senses and the spider-eye view, the dragline, the wasp (a tarantula's threat display does not frighten it, and it does not change what the wasp or the rival does), the hunger and the growth, the saved record and the first three spiders (the peacock's dance and the ant mimic's antennae are as they were; a pose given to a rig whose appearance has no `threat` is ignored). Nothing is sold: both tarantulas are free (ROBLOX-66), `SpiderChoice.locked` still returns false. Nothing has run in Studio: how the rear reads from the default camera distance, whether 32 degrees is enough on a body this size, the legs' reach in the posture and the speed of the sweep are Nathan's to judge, and each is a dial in `Data/Tarantula` or in the profile's `threat`. Tests: `tests/tarantula.luau` (86 checks: the data, the loop's order and levels, interruption, slow motion, reduced motion, the words and the cycle), `tests/look.luau` (the drawn pose: the rear, legs I, the palps, the turn, the sweep, the planted feet and the root; 851 checks) and `tests/client_ant.luau` (the keys through the client loop).

### ROBLOX-83 - The notebook page for the tarantulas: "Velvet cousins (Theraphosidae)" (Nathan, Perplexity, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Decided. The pack's two spiders get one notebook page, `tarantula_cousins`, in the generator (`tools/gen_notebook_pages.py`, the `TARANTULA` entry beside `PEACOCK`, `ANTMIMIC` and `MATING`) and regenerated into `NotebookPages.luau`, never written by hand. Its trigger, `tarantula`, is the fourth the Roblox game added to Godot's list (after `dance`, `antennae` and `hatched`) and, like `dance` and `hatched`, is client-owned: `SpiderClient` fires `book:notice("tarantula")` when the player changes to either tarantula (`spiderPick.switch`) and when a tarantula's threat display starts (so a player who is given the spider by a saved choice, and not by changing, still finds the page when they rear up). `NotebookWatch` does not know it. The page is in the "things to try" list (`NotebookText.TRY`): "Change to a tarantula while resting, then rear up outside."

What it says. Tarantulas are large, heavy, long-lived spiders; they are not jumping spiders (their eyes sit close together on a small mound and are poor, and they feel their way with the hairs on their legs); their fangs strike downward, side by side; most New World tarantulas have urticating hairs on the abdomen and cast them at an attacker; a threatened tarantula rears up and lifts its front legs; and all spin silk from spinnerets at the rear of the abdomen (480 characters; the page limit is 520). "In this game": both are costumes on the first spider's body and the size is the first spider's (a real body is five or more centimetres), you still see, jump, spin a line and are hunted as the first spider, which no real tarantula could do; the rear-up and the turn with the spinnerets sweeping are drawn poses with invented angles and timing and no silk mat is laid; the colours are stylised, the redknee's rust patch is only a colour (no hairs are cast) and the greenbottle's bars soften at the last growth stage as a game choice. The page makes no claim about either species' hairs, colours or behaviour beyond what tarantulas do in general, because the sources do not.

Sources, and how far they were checked (docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md has the record). Foelix (2011), *Biology of Spiders*, 3rd edition: not consulted and no record of it could be checked; it stands for the textbook sentences and is the weakest-sourced part of the page. Bertani & Guadanucci (2013), *Zoologia* 30(4): 403-418: the bibliographic record was checked at Crossref and the abstract at OpenAlex, and it supports the urticating-hairs sentence; the paper was not opened. The DOI first written from memory was a different paper's, and was caught only because the record was looked up; the one in the file is the looked-up one. Both sources should be read before a public release, like the others.

What deliberately does not change: the difficulty, the senses, the physics, the wasp and the rival, the saved record (the notebook is the player's, outside the checkpoints), the first three spiders' pages and the 24 pages Godot's list opens in a long game (the watcher's test still opens 24; this page, like the dance and the hatching, is the client's). Nothing is sold: both tarantulas and the page are free (ROBLOX-66). Nothing ran in Studio: how the page reads in the panel and whether "Velvet cousins" is the title Nathan wants are his to judge; the sketch beside it is the default spider's (`NotebookArt` draws one only for the pages it has an extra for, and this page has none yet). Tests: `tests/notebook.luau` (the page, its trigger and its sources; 874 checks) and `tests/client_ant.luau` (choosing a tarantula opens it and shows the note).

Addendum (2026-10-19): ROBLOX-85 drew the redknee's rust patch as a cluster of eight short hairs, so the game note's "only a colour (no hairs are cast)" no longer matched the drawing. It now says the patch is "drawn as a small cluster of hairs (no hairs are cast)". The note makes no claim about the real animal's hairs, and the page's text is unchanged.

### ROBLOX-84 - Legs with segments, feet and fangs: the anatomy pass on the first spider and the two tarantulas (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Decided. Nathan looked at the first spider, the Mexican redknee and the Greenbottle blue and said what an eye would say: the first spider's pattern is strong, but its legs are plain tubes with ball joints, with no knee, no taper and no feet, and the tarantulas have the same legs (ROBLOX-81 drew them on the first spider's chain). This entry is only the shapes; the fur, the sheen and the palette are ROBLOX-85. It was judged from renders of the real rig code through `tools/preview` (Luau under the plain CLI, drawn in Godot), not in Studio: Roblox's materials and lighting differ, so the proportions, the placement and the silhouettes are what that looked at, and how any of it reads under the sill's light is Nathan's to judge.

What changed. The rig's three-link chain (femur, tibia, tarsus; hip, knee, ankle, foot) and the raycast placement are exactly as they were: `SpiderRig.update` still finds the same feet, the same knees and the same ankles, and each link is then drawn as the segments a spider has. A new optional appearance table, `legForm` (and `fang`, `palpForm`), says how, and an appearance without one is drawn as it always was. All numbers are ART and dials in `Data/Appearances`. The femur is two rods meeting end to end: a thick one from the hip to 62% of the link (58% for the tarantulas) and a thinner one, 0.74 of its diameter, on to the knee, so the pale band (which stays one `bandFraction` of the whole link, as pinned) sits wholly on the thick rod. The tibia of the first spider does the same (split 62%, tip 0.86 of its diameter). The knee, which was a ball 0.95 of the femur's diameter, is the patella: a short stout ellipsoid, 1.30 of the femur's diameter long and 1.10 (the tarantulas' 1.20) across, its long axis along the bend of the leg, in the knee's own colour, so the first spider's white knees and the redknee's flame-orange band (`#EE5E22`, exact) are on the patella. The third link (the rig's `Tarsus`, which a real leg splits into metatarsus and tarsus) has its break at 55% of the link, the metatarsus break, lifted 0.012 studs (the tarantulas 0) so the foot reads as two segments: a ball on the rod (`TarsalJoint`, 1.15 of the tarsus' diameter, the tarantulas 1.20, in the ankle's colour, so it is white inside the first spider's tarsus band) and, for the first spider only, a second, thinner rod (`TarsusTip`, 0.78) from the break to the foot. The pale tarsus band sits across the break, as it did across the middle of the tarsus. The foot ball (`Foot1..8`, full detail only, as before) is now a flattened ellipsoid lying on the surface under the foot, its long side the way the foot points, its centre a third of its height above the planted point: the first spider's is 1.7 tarsus diameters long, 1.0 high and 1.44 wide, with two tiny dark claws (`Claw`, 0.036 studs, 0.009 across) leaving its front, splayed; each tarantula's is a scopula, wider and rounder than the tarsus is thick (0.92 of its diameter either side of the centre, 0.58 high), with one short dark bristle (`FootBristle`, 0.04) swept forward out of its front. The fangs are new, `Anatomy.layout` places them (`Layout.fangs`, pure, so the test reads them) and the rig draws them as dark mahogany, slightly glossy Foil ellipsoids: the first spider's are two small curved segments each (0.040 and 0.032 studs, the second turned in toward the middle), hanging from the lower front of each chelicera, tucked; the tarantulas' are larger, one segment each (0.105 long, 0.019 thick), pointing forward and a little down under their chelicerae. The tarantulas' palps, drawn 1.45 times as long, taper the same way (`PalpStalkTip`).

Numbers, counted by `tests/look.luau` the way it already counts (every part of the model, the invisible root included). First spider: 676 parts at full detail (was 624: 475 hairs and 8 shells as before, 193 other, was 141; the 52 are 8 femur tips, 8 tibia tips, 8 tarsus tips, 8 tarsal joints, 16 claws and 4 fang segments; the patella and the pad replace parts) and 276 at low (was 266; 129 other, was 119: 8 femur tips and 2 fangs, one segment each; no tibia taper, metatarsus break, pad or claws at low). Mexican redknee: 116 at full (was 88: 8 femur tips, 8 tarsal joints, 8 foot bristles, 2 fangs and 2 palp tips) and 82 at low (was 78: the fangs and the palp tips). Greenbottle blue: 142 at full (was 124: the same 28 more, and its leg bristles cut from 34 to 24, one per segment, to make room for them until ROBLOX-85 redraws them) and 82 at low (was 78). The ceilings in `tests/look.luau` change deliberately: the first spider's 705 and 272 (the first coat's) become 700 and 290, its `other` ceiling 160 becomes 200, and the tarantulas' 128 and 80 become 150 and 90; nothing is near them but the first spider's low tier (276 of 290) and the greenbottle's full (142 of 150).

What deliberately does not change. The solver, the targets, the gait and the motor (`tests/look.luau` now puts the rival's rig, which has plain legs, beside the first spider's and requires the same planted feet, knees and ankles to a nanostud); the first spider's pattern (colours, the white bands, spots, band and speckles, the eye layout: every pin passes unchanged); the rival (it is a clone of the first spider's table and now sets `legForm`, `fang` and its own `detail` back to nothing and to what it was, so its parts, 624 at full and 266 at low, are as before), the peacock spider and the ant mimic (119 and 80, 79 and 69, as before); the mate and the growth stages (every new rod, pad and claw scales with `setScale` as the rest does, and `tests/look.luau` checks it at 1.7); the spider-eye view (legs I's lower segments, joint and claws stay; femurs, fangs and the other legs are hidden); the part names the tests and poses read (`Femur1`, `Tarsus1`, `Knee1`, `Foot1`: `Tarsus` is now the proximal rod of the break, so the threat pose's raised-leg checks pass as they were). The fur and the leg bristles are as ROBLOX-81 and ROBLOX-13 left them, apart from the greenbottle's count above. What has not been seen: any of this in Studio. How the tapered rods' steps, the patella's size, the break's bead, the flattened pads and the claws, which are 0.009 studs thick, and the fangs, which at the default camera are a few pixels, read in Roblox is unknown; each is a number in `legForm` or `fang`. Tests: `tests/look.luau` (the rods meet end to end to a millimetre, taper, the patella, the metatarsus break, the pads lying on the floor, the fangs' layout and direction, the budgets at both tiers, the eye view, `setScale`, the rival and the other spiders unchanged; 873 checks).

### ROBLOX-85 - Fur that lies, a sheen, and velvet: the coat, the sheen and the palette pass on the first spider and the two tarantulas (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Decided. ROBLOX-84 gave the three spiders their anatomy; this entry is the surface. The brief for this pass: the first spider's fur should lie along the body and not read as chips, the tarantulas should read as velvet and not smooth plastic, and the greenbottle's pure cobalt and turquoise should be deeper and more natural. It was judged from renders of the real rig code through `tools/preview` (Luau under the plain CLI, drawn in Godot), not in Studio: Roblox's materials and lighting differ, so layout, angles, counts and colours were what that looked at. The previewer's light brightens and cyan-shifts colours (the greenbottle's carapace reads more turquoise than its #1E8062) and a translucent shell shows a pale specular rim there that Roblox's Fabric will not; how any of it reads under the sill's light is Nathan's to judge. All numbers are ART and dials in `Data/Appearances`.

The first spider's coat. The old hairs were wide flat chips (0.018 across for 0.03 long, lying at 3 to 13 degrees). They are now fine and dense and lie along the body: every coat hair (`locks`, `pale`, `fringe`) sits 15 to 25 degrees off the surface (`pale` 14 to 22, `fringe` 20 to 30), 0.0075 wide (`pale` 0.0085, `fringe` 0.0065), 0.022 to 0.036 long, pointing backward and outward: toward the abdomen's tip, and away from the midline by up to 16 degrees (`out`; the fringe 24), so the flow fans out over the abdomen's curve; on a leg they lie 15 to 26 degrees off it, down toward the foot (`legLift`). Counts: locks 440 on the abdomen and 250 on the head (were 340 and 130), fringe 220 and 80 (were 170 and 60), white hairs 800 per square stud of marking (were 600), guard hairs cut to 24 and 12 (were 50 and 24) so only a few stand (42 to 56 degrees), legs {4, 4, 1} a leg (were {7, 6, 3}), the head's tufts 7 a side and 0.058 long (were 10, 0.078), the cheek ruff 4 (were 5), the palp fluff 5 a palp, 0.048 long (were 7, 0.055), all finer. Length and colour jitter: every hair's length is a hash of its index; 7% of the coat's (8% of the legs') carry a greyer, paler cast (`tip`, `grey`, #7F7F7A) over the black and dark ones, so a dark coat catches the light as a few pale tips; white is still only inside the white spots and the band. The hair-free zones (eyes, jaws) are as they were and pinned. Sheen: the carapace, face, pedicel and abdomen take Reflectance 0.04 (`gloss`, was 0; SmoothPlastic as before); the eyes keep their own Reflectance. The four fur shells are as they were.

The tarantulas' velvet. Each has a fur shell on the carapace (1.07 times its size) and one on the abdomen (1.075), Fabric, CastShadow off, partly transparent (0.45 redknee, 0.62 greenbottle), in the fur's colour, drawn at both tiers; the carapace's shell is a plain scaling of the dome and stays below the tubercle's eyes (a test requires that none of the eight beads is covered). Leg bristle fringes: each is a thin hair (0.080 studs long, 0.009 wide) growing from the side of the leg (50 to 115 degrees from the top, one side and the other in turn), leaning 32 to 48 degrees off the leg toward the foot, in the middle of the link (12 to 74%, clear of the patella and the ankle), at full detail only. The brief asked for about six a segment on the front pairs; that cannot fit the 150-part ceiling (a tarantula has 108 other parts), so the counts are per pair, femur, tibia, tarsus, front pair first: redknee {3, 2, 1}, {2, 2, 0}, {1, 1, 0}, {1, 1, 0} (28 bristles for the eight legs, ginger-brown #A8683C), greenbottle {4, 3, 0}, {3, 2, 1}, {2, 1, 0}, {1, 1, 0} (36, pale blue-white #C9DCF2). The old foot bristle (ROBLOX-84, a part per foot) and the strand on the scopula are gone; the foot is the pad alone. The redknee's urticating patch (#BA5028) is now a dense cluster standing out of it: 8 short ginger-rust hairs (0.045 to 0.065 long, 28 to 42 degrees off the back, combed toward the rear, a third of them paler, #D9803F), all inside the patch, at full detail only, and the patch itself stands proud of the velvet. The copper rim (#D67638) is raised: its ellipsoid is lifted 0.018 studs (the greenbottle's 0.010), so a ring of it stands about 0.011 studs proud of the carapace's edge. The orange patella bands (#EE5E22 knees, #E08642 tibia band) are exact and untouched.

The greenbottle's palette. Old to new: the carapace #24767C at Reflectance 0.08 to #1E8062 at 0.14 with a #2A9A7E rim (teal-green with a sheen); the legs #204AA8 at 0.06 to #1E3F8F at 0.10 (the joint rings #2E62C4 and the chelicerae #308E94 are as they were); the bristles #DAA86C (golden-tan) to #C9DCF2 (pale blue-white); the abdomen #E46226 to #C4551C (burnt orange); the bars #2A1C18 to a softer brown #5A2A14, and the dark midline #2A1C18 is now three raised ridges (thin ellipsoids, 0.0085 wide, 0.16 long at the back, middle and front, standing 0.002 studs above the abdomen's fur shell; the middle one alone at low detail), so it sits slightly proud of the velvet and not under it; the fur shells are light teal (#35A07F) on the carapace and burnt orange (#E0782C) on the abdomen.

Low detail. Shells only on the tarantulas (2 each, no bristles, no cluster, no ridges beyond the middle one, no fringe), and fewer lying hairs on the first spider: every 4th lock, every 3rd fringe hair, every 2nd white one and every 4th leg hair are kept (the first spider's low detail was every 3rd lock).

Numbers, counted by `tests/look.luau` the way it already counts (every part of the model, the invisible root included). First spider: 695 parts at full detail (was 676 after ROBLOX-84 and 624 before it: 494 hairs, 8 shells, 193 other, where the hairs were 475; the coat has more, thinner, hairs and fewer guard and tuft ones) and 267 at low (was 276 after ROBLOX-84: 136 hairs, 2 shells, 129 other). Mexican redknee: 146 at full (was 116: 36 hairs, 28 bristles and the 8 of the cluster, and 2 shells) and 84 at low (was 82: the second shell). Greenbottle blue: 148 at full (was 142: 36 bristles, was 24) and 84 at low (was 82). The ceilings from ROBLOX-84 stand (first spider 700 and 290, tarantulas 150 and 90); the nearest is the first spider's full tier, 695 of 700.

What deliberately does not change. The first spider's pattern (the colours, the white bands, spots, band and speckles, the eye layout: every pin passes unchanged) and its eyes; the solver, the targets, the gait and the motor; the rival (it clones the first spider's table, so it is given ROBLOX-13's coat and palp fluff back and no sheen, and is drawn as before: 624 parts at full detail, 266 at low); the peacock spider and the ant mimic; the mate and the growth stages (shells, hairs and ridges scale with `setScale`); the redknee's body, leg and band colours (it gains only the shells, the bristles, the cluster and the raised rim); every part name the tests read. Pinned test values changed deliberately and only for this entry: the coat's lift and tone pins (hairs lie at 15 to 25 degrees, not 3 to 13; a few are grey, none frosted), the greenbottle's colour and gloss pins (the palette above), the tarantulas' shell and hair pins (2 shells, 30 to 40 hairs at full and none at low), and the foot-bristle pins (removed, now pinned at 0). What has not been seen: any of this in Studio. How the Fabric shells blend over the sill's light, whether the 0.0075-wide hairs are visible at the default camera or flicker, whether a 0.04 Reflectance reads at all, the greenbottle's blues and teal, the ridges' height against the shell (0.002 studs) and the thin bristles are unknown; each is a number in `fuzz`, `velvet`, `cluster`, `gloss` or the palette. Tests: `tests/look.luau` (the flow, lift and tone of the coat, the sheen and the eyes, the shells' size, material and transparency, the bristles' counts, lean and sides, the cluster inside its patch and absent at low, the rim's height, the ridges above the shell, the softer bars, both tiers' budgets, the rival and the other spiders unchanged; 895 checks).

### ROBLOX-86 - The spider hangs properly on its dragline: a vertical head-down body on the line, folded legs, a damped sway and a slow turn, head-up on the way up (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Decided. Nathan: "work on making the spiders hang properly on their webs." ROBLOX-59 drew the suspended body by pitching it about the spinneret by at most 80 degrees and narrowing the legs, and that read as a spider lying on its side with its legs out like a table's: the pitch was clamped, the body swung about the tail so its middle hung 0.7 studs away from the collider, the strand ran from the anchor to a tail that was off the line, and the feet were still reaching for a floor that was not there. This entry redraws the hang. It was judged from renders of the real rig code through `tools/preview` (Luau under the plain CLI, drawn in Godot) with its new `--hang drop|climb` option (the collider two studs up, the line a little off the vertical, the rig drawn suspended, a thin strand drawn from the spinneret; views `hang_side`, `hang_front` (from behind the body's old front, which now shows its back), `hang_back` (the belly), `hang_three` and `hang_wide`), not in Studio: Roblox's materials and lighting differ, so the pose, the angles and the silhouette are what that looked at. All numbers are ART and dials in `Data/Appearances` (`hang`).

The body. The drawn body turns about its middle (the point halfway between the face's centre and the spinneret tip) by the shortest turn that puts its long axis, the face's centre to the spinneret tip, along the line; there is no angle limit (the 80 degree clamp and the roll fudge are gone), so a line that is vertical gives a body that hangs vertical, head down, and a line that leans gives a body that leans with it. The turn blends in at `rate` 3.5 a second (was 5, so about 0.29 s to hang), and landing still ends it at once. Turning about the middle and not the tail puts the spinneret on the line through the collider, so the silk leaves the spinneret tip, the strand from the anchor runs straight to it, and the body is drawn where the collider is (the camera follows the collider, so the spider no longer hangs off-centre in its own view). Then three small drawn motions about the spinneret, all scaled by how far the pose has blended in. A lean: the long axis stands 3.0 degrees off the line, so it is not a plumb bob. A sway: a damped pendulum, 4.5 degrees at the start, period 2.5 s, decaying with a time constant of 4.0 s (the swing about the body's own side axis; 0.45 of it swings sideways a quarter period out of phase, so the tail traces a thin ellipse), started by the drop (the clock restarts whenever the pose was fully off). A turn about the line: 8 degrees a second to start, decaying with a time constant of 7 s (at most 56 degrees in all), one way on one drop and the other way on the next. The long axis measured from the parts (the face to the spinneret) stays within 6.9 degrees of the line over ten seconds for the first spider, the peacock spider, the ant mimic and the rival, 5.9 for the tarantulas, against the brief's 10. Reduced Motion is a static pose: the lean stays (a posture), the sway, the turn and the feet's tremble do not run.

The legs. Each leg is now three links posed by direction, each link keeping the length it has standing (hip to knee, knee to ankle, ankle to foot, worked from the same rest pose the walking code uses), so the legs fold the way a spider's do and do not stretch. Directions in the body frame, x mirrored for the left, +Z toward the tail and the line, -Z toward the head, (femur, tibia, tarsus): legs I (0.65, -0.20, -0.25), (-0.10, -0.25, 0.90), (-0.45, -0.30, 0.50); legs II (0.65, -0.20, -0.05), (-0.05, -0.25, 0.90), (-0.50, -0.30, 0.50); legs III (0.60, -0.15, 0.55), (0.00, -0.20, 0.80), (-0.55, -0.30, -0.35); legs IV (0.40, -0.15, 0.85), (0.15, -0.20, 0.35), (-0.50, -0.30, -0.20), each normalised. So legs I and II go out and a little forward (toward the head, which is down), then curl back up toward the line, and the foot ends level with the thorax and the front of the abdomen; legs III and IV go out and back and fold along the body, the last pair trailing up past the abdomen toward the spinneret and the line. A fixed per-leg scatter (0.10 on each component, from a sine hash of the pair, the side and the link) keeps a pair from mirroring, and the feet tremble by 0.08 on the tarsus direction with a period of 2 s (not with Reduced Motion; the old 0.025 stud bob). The tests require that no foot is below the head along the line, none is planted, and none is more than 0.62 of the body's length off the line (the first spider's farthest is 0.55, the peacock's 0.46, the ant's 0.49, the tarantulas' 0.45 and 0.46). The palps are drawn in 30 degrees toward the jaws. The planted-foot solver, the targets and the gait are untouched: this is the not-grounded branch that was already drawn.

Going up the line. The rig can tell: the collider moves along the line toward its anchor, so a smoothed speed along the line (0.25 s) above 0.35 studs a second (the reel is 1.5) is a climb, and below 0.14 it is not (so lowering, which is slower and the other way, is not). The body then turns end over end about its middle to head up, blended over 0.5 s (a Reduced Motion spider takes it at once), and stands 0.8 of the carapace's half height off the line toward its back so the line runs along the belly, not through the body; the sway and the turn fade out as it goes; and the legs' directions along the body turn over (legs I and II reach up the line, III and IV go out to grip it: their Z components are scaled by one minus two (or 1.2) times the head-up blend). When it stops it turns back head down. The strand is still drawn from the spinneret, now at the lower end, so the line runs behind the body.

The other looks. The peacock spider, the ant mimic and the rival (a clone of the first spider's table) use the first spider's `hang` directly; the legs take their own lengths, so a small spider hangs as a small one. The two tarantulas use a copy with a lean of 3.0, a swing of 3.5 degrees (decay 3.0 s) and a turn of 5 degrees a second (decay 6 s): heavier. A tarantula on a line is a stretch biologically (a burrow dweller does not dangle on a dragline; it is silk it could fall on in this game), and the redknee was checked to read as a sane drawn animal: the orange-banded legs fold the same way, the spinnerets sit at the line, the fur shell hangs from the carapace without clipping. Both detail tiers hang through the same code: the low tier has the same pose, no more or fewer parts (the first spider 695 at full and 267 at low as before; every look's count is the same hanging as standing, pinned).

What deliberately does not change. The motor, the tether physics and the silk, the camera (the root part stays at the collider; a test pins it), the collider, the senses, the sim clock, the part budgets and the ceilings, every walking, standing, jump-preparation and display pose (the dance, the ant's antennae, the threat), and the client (nothing new is passed: `hangDirection` and the collider's movement are all the rig reads). The old 5 a second blend is now 3.5. Pinned values that changed: none of the earlier pins (the hanging tests of ROBLOX-59 pass as they were: the head hangs beneath the spinneret, the root is untouched, the Reduced Motion legs are still and unplanted, landing restores the surface frame at once). What has not been seen: any of this in Studio. How the folded legs read from the game's own camera (which looks from above and behind, so most of the time at the abdomen's top and the legs' knees), whether the sway and the turn read or look like a bug, the 0.35 studs a second that counts as climbing against the real reel, and the tail strand against the camera's own framing (ROBLOX-59's `LineFraming` turns the view to see the lip the line leaves) are unknown; each is a number in `hang`. Tests: `tests/look.luau` (the axis within 10 degrees and not a plumb bob, no foot below the head or beyond the sideways reach, the tail on the line, the head under the abdomen, the root untouched and the part counts unchanged for all six looks at both tiers; the swing's amplitude, its decay by exp(-t/4) a period on, its 2.5 s period, its death by ten seconds, the turn's limit and decay and its reversal on the second drop; Reduced Motion still; the left and right legs not mirrors; the head turning up over about half a second on the way up and down again on stopping, lowering staying head down; 1009 checks, 114 more than before this entry).

### ROBLOX-87 - The spider lives when nothing is asked of it: a breathing abdomen, grooming, attention, an alarm and a tarantula's stretch (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

What changed. The drawn spider (the full tier only: `cfg.idle` is what turns any of this on, and the low tier has none) now does the small things an animal does at rest, from a new pure stepper, `Rig/Living` (state, a seeded stream and a bout machine; it takes the clock, whether the spider stands, hangs or is posed, Reduced Motion, the focus point and the cues, and returns a pose the rig draws). Every number is in `look.idle` (`Appearances.luau`), none in code. Display only: the motor, the camera, the collider, the senses and the sim clock are not read or written by any of it.

One. The abdomen breathes. The breath is the old one (a sine with a slow wobble in its depth, now `Idle.breath`) at a period of 2.5 s (0.4 Hz, was 3.0 s, 0.33 Hz), and the abdomen's scale follows it by 2.5% (1.025 at the top of a breath, 0.975 at the bottom), as much as the old body lift and pitch (0.006 and 0.016 studs, 2.4 degrees, unchanged). While the spider walks, hangs or is posed it keeps 0.35 of that (`movingShare`), under Reduced Motion none. The scale moves every abdomen part's position about the abdomen's centre, so the hairs and the fur shell ride with it, but only the large masses (the abdomen, its shell and its patterns, a `big` flag set once when the rig is built) are resized, which keeps the per-frame cost where it was (a hair is 0.02 studs: a 2.5% change in it is not seen). The tarantulas breathe at 4.0 s (0.25 Hz) and 2%, with a lift of 0.004 studs and 1.6 degrees. The peacock spider's fan and the ant mimic have no `breathe.scale`, so they do not pulse (a fan that swelled would read as a display; the ant's gaster is a rigid thing); they keep their old breath.

Two. Grooming. After 4.0 s of standing (not walking, hanging, posed or alarmed) a bout starts 0.3 to 3.0 s later, and then every 8 to 20 s after the last one ended, from a seeded stream (a linear congruential generator from a hash of the look's name plus 7919 times the count of rigs built, so two spiders do not groom in step and a test can replay one). A bout is one of two acts, chosen by the stream: a palp drawn through the chelicerae, three strokes over 2.0 s (the palp turns 30 degrees toward the midline and 20 down; the tip is pulled to the jaws), or leg I wiped by the palps for 2.5 s (the foot goes to (0.12, -0.16, -0.64) in the body frame, studs, the knee bending out 0.22 and up 0.20, and sweeps 0.06 studs to and fro while both palps close 24 degrees). A bout fades in and out over 0.18 of its act and gives way in 0.25 s the moment the spider moves. After a meal, 1.5 s into the next stand, a longer cleaning: both palps (2.2 s), then leg I, then leg II (2.5 s each; the table has an entry for each of the four legs, III and IV the same as II, not used by the code today). A planted foot is never touched: the leg that grooms is drawn off its planted point only while the spider stands, and goes back as the bout fades; the foot override is the same drawn-only one the antennae use.

Three. Attention. The rig takes a `focusPoint` (a world position, or nil), which the client sets to the point the focus ray hits (the point the player looks at). The drawn body turns about its own up axis toward it, at most 20 degrees and eased over 0.4 s (an exponential approach, so a fast flick does not snap); it is applied as an accent to the body, head and abdomen bases and not to the root part, so the legs, the planted feet and the camera do not turn with it. The principal eyes shift 0.02 studs toward the focus, in full for any angle left within 28 degrees of the body's turn limit and fading to none beyond. With no focus the body eases back. Nothing in the senses reads this: the spider does not see what the player looks at, it only appears to. The tarantulas have no `attention` (a heavy, indifferent animal).

Four. Alarm. `rig:cue("alarm")` raises legs I to (0.20, 0.30, -0.52), the knee out 0.10 and up 0.22, in 0.15 s, holds them and freezes every other idle motion (breathing, tilt, grooming, glance) for 1.2 s, and lowers them in 0.35 s. The client cues it where the game already knows: the first warning of a wasp (the `visit_started` signal, so once a visit, not per pass) and the rival's windup (its `warning` branch). A cue during grooming cuts the bout. Under Reduced Motion the legs go up and down without the ease and the freeze is a pose held, not a motion. The tarantulas keep the alarm.

Five. The tarantula. A breath of 0.25 Hz and 2%; no grooming; and every 10 to 24 s (the first 2 to 6 s into a stand of 4 s) one leg stretches for 3.0 s: out 0.16 studs, up 0.05, along the body 0.16, the knee up 0.07, in and out on a smooth curve, the leg chosen by the stream.

Reduced Motion. The stepper returns a still pose: no breath, no scale, no groom, no stretch, no turn and no glance; the alarm is held (the legs up, as a pose) for its 1.2 s and then goes. Pinned.

What deliberately does not change. The motor, the tether physics, the camera (the root part stays at the collider), the collider, the senses, the sim clock, the planted feet and the gait, the part counts and budgets, the hang pose of ROBLOX-86 (the idle motion is off while it hangs, except the 0.35 breath), the dance, the threat and the ant's antennae, the peacock's fan, and the whole of the low tier, which still has `cfg.idle = nil` for all of it. The rival's rig is the low tier and has none of this: it neither breathes nor grooms. The mate's rig is the low tier too, and had none until ROBLOX-89 built it `living` for the mating scene (`SpiderRig.new(..., living)`: the same parts, with the breath and the scene's cues). Discrepancies against the brief, found when reading the code: the rig had no focus point, so `focusPoint` is a new field, set from the client's focus ray; and nothing followed the eyes (no glance, no catchlight follow existed), so the principal-eye shift is new, not a strengthening.

The client. It is at 199 top-level locals (the limit is 200); no new local was added. Three lines: `rig.focusPoint = focusPoint` after the focus ray, the two `rig:cue("alarm")` calls, and `rig:noteMeals(prey.feeding.mealsFinished)` before `setGrowthStage` (the rig counts meals itself, so the client passes a number and the rig cues the cleaning when it grows; it never reads the sim).

What has not been seen: any of this in Studio. How the 2.5% pulse reads from the game's camera (which sees the abdomen from above and behind, the one view in which it is most likely to be too small, or in which the hairs may seem to crawl), whether 20 degrees of turn toward the cursor reads as attention or as a slide, whether the leg wipe reads as grooming at the game's distance, and whether the alarm's 1.2 s is long enough to be seen when the wasp's warning and the camera's own cut come at once, are unknown; each is a number in `look.idle`. The previewer's images are the judge so far.

Tests: a new `tests/idle.luau` (the breath's period and amplitude, the scale within 2.5% and the hairs and shell moving with the abdomen, the movingShare; the bout times from the seed (none before 4 s, the first within 3.3 s of that, then 8 to 20 s apart), both acts' timing, the palp's three strokes, the leg's reach and sweep, the planted feet untouched, a bout giving way in 0.25 s; the meal cleaning's order (palps, then leg I, then leg II); the body turn within 20 degrees and eased in 0.4 s, the root and the camera untouched, the glance's sign and its fade; the alarm's raise, freeze and release times, the cut in a bout, Reduced Motion; the tarantula's breath, no groom, the stretch's 3 s; the low tier with none of it; the part counts unchanged; 5685 checks, about 77 s), `client_smoke` gains two asserts (the wasp's first warning cues the alarm; a finished meal cues the cleaning), and the look, peacock, antmimic, tarantula, client_ant, client_mating, client_rival and scenarios suites are unchanged and green.

### ROBLOX-88 - Pacing: five flies to a moult, a hungrier spider and a harder rival (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan, playing v28: "I feel I'm getting through too quickly, so maybe up the amount of food you have to consume, and the rival male should still be harder to take down", and "deplete the life bar quicker" (the hunger bar). Three dials, all in the game's own rules, none in a golden-pinned default.

Food to grow. Godot's moult asks for `Growth.READY_FOOD` = 0.9 of food, which ROBLOX-61 made three housefly-equivalents (a housefly is 0.3 in the units of `fedSinceMoult`, 0.9 / 3; a small prey is half). The game now asks for five (ten small prey). `Growth` gains `Growth.FLY_FOOD` = 0.3, `Growth.game = { moultFlies = 5 }` and `Growth:useGameRules()`, which sets the instance's own `readyFood` = 5 x 0.3 = 1.5 (and `gameRules` = true), in the way `NeedsModel.game` and `Data/Catch.game` are applied; `Growth:readyAt()` is the threshold in use and `Growth:mealGoal()` the flies it comes to (3 or 5). `Growth.eat` reads `readyAt()`, so a Growth never given the game's rules is Godot's bit for bit: `growth_reference.json` is untouched and its 438 checks still pass. The client turns the rules on in four places and adds no top-level local (it stays at 199): after `LifeLoop.new`, in a checkpoint restore, in `applyRules`, and in the tick when `life.growth.gameRules ~= true` (a family that carries on has a new Growth from `Mating.carryOn`; a record from before has none of the new fields). Its meal credit is unchanged, 0.9 / (3 x 0.35) of the energy swallowed, so a housefly still credits 0.3 and five make 1.5. The moult-progress words are the growth's own now: `LifeText.growth` takes its goal and threshold from the Growth when it can say them, so the line reads "Next moult: 1.5 of 5 meals · 2 small prey = 1 housefly" (the half-meal steps and the small-prey sentence are as ROBLOX-61 had them; the old `required` and `meal` arguments still serve a Growth that cannot say). Food already eaten is kept: a spider that had eaten two flies reads 2 of 5, and one already ready to moult stays ready (the threshold is a rule, not saved data; nothing about it is in `toRecord`, and the checkpoint records the two plain fields like any others). A fully grown spider needs 15 flies in all, not 9 (+67 percent); the rival (0.7, two flies) is 40 percent of a moult, not 67.

Hunger. `NeedsModel.game.drain` 1.55 to 1.9. The math is ROBLOX-78's: a day is about 11 waking hours at 0.021 and 13 asleep at 0.007, 0.322 at Godot's factor 1; at 1.9 that is 11 x 0.021 x 1.9 = 0.439 awake + 13 x 0.007 x 1.9 = 0.173 asleep = 0.612 (it was 0.499). A full reserve lasts 1.63 days (was 2.0); a housefly (0.35) is 0.57 of a day and a fruit fly 0.29; starving from full, with no food at all, is 1.63 days to empty and two more to die, 3.6 days in all (it was 4.0, the starving clock of 24 and 48 game hours being untouched). The balance, for Nathan: holding steady is about two flies a day (0.70 against 0.61, a surplus of 0.09; one fly a day is a slow decline of 0.26 a day), and growth asks for its five flies on top of that, so a spider that eats only the minimum does not grow at all and one that hunts well can. The daily rival (0.7) is now worth 1.14 days: it covers a day with 0.09 to spare and no more, so it helps but cannot carry a life. The gentle modes keep their half rate (a gentle day costs 0.306) and their floor. The dials, named: `NeedsModel.game.drain` (1.55 was 0.5 a day, 1.1 was 0.354, 1.0 is Godot's; each 0.1 moves a day by 0.032) and `Growth.game.moultFlies` (5; every fly is 0.3 and the reserve cap of 1.0 still wastes what overflows but growth credits it).

The rival, in three places. The contest of ROBLOX-73: caught / sprang clear / counter-bite 55 / 25 / 20 to 40 / 32 / 28 (`RivalRules.contestCaptured` 0.40, `contestSprang` 0.32, the rest 0.28); over 6000 strikes the rates are 0.400, 0.322 and 0.278. An unaware rival is still a free catch, as are a recovering one and a spared one. The counter-bite `RivalRules.counterCost` 0.25 to 0.30 (half a day's 0.61; the gentle modes are free as ever); the expected cost of one aware strike is 0.28 x 0.30 = 0.084 (it was 0.20 x 0.25 = 0.05). The grip on a caught rival (ROBLOX-77, `Data/Catch.rival`): `drainScale` 0.5 to 0.7 and `tire` 0.25 to 0.10, the rest as it was (start grip 0.6, a tap 0.085, jerks 0.8, no cap). The way these were chosen: a grid of (drain, tire) was run with `PreyRandom` seeded as the game seeds it, 60 rivals each, taps evenly spaced from 0.2 s, against Nathan's mark of about 5 taps a second to win in 7 to 10 s, 3.5 or fewer losing and no taps tearing free in about 1.6 s. d = 0.7 with tire 0.1 was the one that met two of the three and came a little under on the third (a win at 5 taps a second took 7.6 s on average, 5.6 to 9.8 s, so the quick end is below the 7 to 10 s mark, as the paragraph after the table says); d = 0.7 with tire 0.08 was a hair slower (mean 8.0, range 5.6 to 11.4, with 4.5 a second nearly a stalemate) and I kept the tighter one. The measured feel (60 rivals, standard rules, `tests/rival.luau`):

| taps a second | ROBLOX-77 (drain 0.5, tire 0.25) | now (drain 0.7, tire 0.10) |
|---|---|---|
| none | tears free in 2.06 s | tears free in 1.65 s |
| 2 | tears free (4.6 s) | tears free (2.8 s) |
| 3 | stalemate, never ends in 40 s | tears free (4.6 s) |
| 3.5 | wins, 13.3 s (9.1 to 20.2) | tears free (7.3 s) |
| 4 | wins, 7.2 s (5.7 to 8.4) | tears free (59 of 60 in 40 s, 22.8 s; one still holding on) |
| 4.5 | wins, 4.6 s (3.3 to 5.8) | the edge: wins, 19.8 s (9.5 to 32.6) |
| 5 | wins, 3.3 s (2.6 to 4.4) | wins every time, 7.6 s (5.6 to 9.8) |
| 5.5 | wins, 2.5 s | wins, 4.6 s (3.3 to 5.8) |
| 6 | wins, 2.1 s | wins, 3.2 s (2.5 to 3.7) |

So the threshold, about 3.9 taps a second before, is about 4.5 now, and a win at the 5 a second the brief named takes 7.6 s on average (all 60 between 5.6 and 9.8: a little under the 7 to 10 s mark at the quick end, which I did not tune away). A first tap as late as 0.5 s still wins at 5 a second; a one-second pause at 3 s costs 6.3 s more; a pause of three seconds loses it every time. Gentle (Easy, Free) is exactly the house fly's struggle as before: no taps tears free in 1.14 s, 3 a second wins every time and 4 a second in 5.5 s, and a counter-bite costs nothing. One consequence I did not plan for: Automatic grip, which adds a steady 0.4 a second, now takes 11.3 s to subdue a rival with no taps at all (3.8 s before), because the net gain over the drain is small until it tires; it still wins every time and nothing in it harms the player, but for the people the setting is for it is a long hold. I left it because the setting is the player's own choice and the rival is optional food (the dial is `Catch.rival.drainScale`, and a smaller `tire` makes it longer).

The notebook. The one line that described the hunger bar, "empties in about two days" in the game note of "Going without food", now says "a little over a day and a half" (`tools/gen_notebook_pages.py` and the generated `NotebookPages.luau`, CRLF, agree).

What did not change: Godot's `Growth`, `NeedsModel`, `Feeding`, `LifeClock` defaults and every golden fixture; the thresholds for hungry (0.25) and well fed (0.85); the meal values (housefly 0.35, small prey half, rival 0.7); the starving hours; the mating gate (energy at least 0.6 and full grown); the rival's patrol, windup, lunge, sight and daily arrival; the fly's struggle and the small prey's; the wasp. Tests, and the pins that moved on purpose: `tests/life.luau` (the hunger case now pins 1.9, a day of 0.612 with its 0.439 and 0.173 parts, the 1.63-day reserve, 1.9 times the drain in every mode and the two-flies arithmetic), `tests/rival.luau` (the contest numbers and rates 40 / 32 / 28, the counter-bite 0.30 in the two places it was 0.25, `Catch.rival` drain 0.7 and tire 0.1, and the whole feel block, now measured at 5 taps a second where it was 4, with new checks that 3.5 and 4 a second lose; its total went from 3619 to 2724 checks because each of 6000 strikes that ends in a catch runs a check and fewer end in one), `tests/client_rival.luau` (the real client's counter-bite costs 0.30), `tests/notebook.luau` (the real client now needs five flies, 1.5, to ready a moult and says "3 of 5 meals" at 0.9; +2 checks), `tests/client/SilkPlayback.luau` (the Studio playback credits 1.5) and `tests/growth.luau` (22 new checks for the game's rules: Godot's three still ready a default Growth; four flies do not ready a game Growth and the fifth does, once; nine small prey do not, ten do; the words at 0 of 5, 1.5 of 5, 4.5 of 5 and ready; food from a record kept; the life loop ready only at the fifth fly). Unmoved, which is the proof the goldens held: `tests/growth.luau`'s 438 original checks including `growth_reference.json`, `tests/life.luau`'s `life_reference.json` replay, `continuing`, `prey` (1646, `prey_reference.json`), `wasp`, `checkpoint` (225), `rivalnav` (293), `mating` (545), `hud` (86), and no `*_reference.json` or `*_reference.luau` file is in the commit. Not run in Studio or on a device: how a day at 0.61 and five flies a moult feel in play (the previous round's play suggested three flies and 0.5 were too quick; the test of the new numbers is Nathan's), whether 5 taps a second sustained for 7 to 10 s is comfortable under a thumb on touch (a long fight is about 40 taps; the gentle modes are the house fly's), and the Studio playback.

### ROBLOX-89 - The mating sequence: a fifteen-second scene with a two-shot camera, a touch, a settle and a parting (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan, playing v28: "there needs to be some kind of ending sequence when the spiders successfully mate." Until now the courtship meter filled, a five-second pause went by under one caption (`[a faint tremble in the silk]`, ROBLOX-80's `PAIR_CAPTION`; the "courted" notice, "The two spiders stay together for a moment.", is a different line and is said as before) and the mate walked off. That is now a scene of about fifteen seconds, in the sim-and-client style of the hatching: a pure plan, a pure pose, a render step that draws it, a skip, and nothing that traps the player past it. The sac, the guard days and the hatching are exactly what they were.

The clock. The scene's clock is the simulation's own, not the client's: `Predator/Mate` gains a `scene` flag (false in a new mate), `Mate.pairLength(m)`, `Mate.sceneTime(m)` and `Mate.skip(m, force)`. The scene begins at the tick the meter fills (`Mate.step` returns "courted" and sets `scene`), its seconds are the pair's `elapsed` while they are together and `pairLength + elapsed` while the mate walks off, so a wasp visit (which holds every timer of the mate) holds the scene, a menu holds it with everything else, and a scene seen again is the same scene. The pause itself grows from 5 s to `MatingRules.pairSeconds` 11.0 for a male mate, whose walk off the sill (up to 4 s) is the scene's last beat, and to `stayPairSeconds` 15.0 for a female mate, who stays (the player is the male; for him the departure is his, after the 15 s). `sceneSeconds` is 15.0 and `skipAfter` 4.0; `Mate.sceneTime` is nil when no scene is playing. `Mate.valid` checks `scene` is a boolean, true only in "pair" or in "leaving" for "mated"; `Mating.problemsWith` refuses a scene when the cycle is not courted or mated. A record from before has no `scene` field (nil), restores as a pair with no scene that ends on its own clock, as it always did; there is a test that strips the field from a real checkpoint and restores it.

Skipping. `Mating.skip(life, force)` is the player's cut: after `skipAfter` seconds of scene a jump or a cancel parts the pair at once (the same "mated" event and stage that Mating.tick gives when the pause ends by itself, so the sac chain cannot tell the difference), the mate walks off in play, and the scene is over; in the male mate's walk off a skip just ends the scene and lets him walk on. Before four seconds it is nothing. `force` is for a wasp: the client forces the skip in the tick in which a wasp visit is under way, because a player whose hands are the scene's cannot be left without them while a wasp hunts (this is a safety rule of my own, not in the brief; the other way it would have been a scene the player cannot leave at the worst moment). Reduced Motion changes nothing about the skip.

No rest inside it. `Checkpoint.boundaryProblem` returns "the two spiders are together" while `life.mate.scene` is true (the whole 15 s, the male's walk off included), like the struggle rule of ROBLOX-77 and the hatching's: a rest there keeps nothing and says so; a record can never be captured with the scene in it. The sim's own `s.resting` branch still finishes a pair that was already together (the mating is never cut short by a rest, as ROBLOX-79 had it); the refusal makes that case unreachable in play.

The plan (`Life/MatingScene`, new, pure, Shared). Beats by scene second: 0 to 3 "turn" (the female turns to face the male), 3 to 7 "touch", 7 to 11 "settle", 11 to 15 "depart". `plan(seed, mateNo, day, playerSex)` is seeded from `PreyRandom` by the world's 914, the mate's number and the day, and draws: which way round the camera sweeps (`dir`), which side it starts on (`side`), the sweep's centre off the side view (`jitterDeg`, -8 to 8), the camera's height (`elevationDeg`, 28 to 36 degrees above the pair's plane, so slightly above), a distance factor (0.95 to 1.05) and which way a female mate turns away (`turnSign`). Nothing is saved: it is a function of numbers that are. The shot is a point, a direction and a distance: the focus is the middle of the two spiders 0.5 stud up from the surface they stand on, the direction is the side of the pair (perpendicular to the line between them in the plane they stand on, so they are side by side and never one behind the other) turned by the orbit and lifted by the elevation, the distance `max(4.2, separation x 1.8 + 2.6) x factor` studs (6.2 at the 2.0 studs a mate stops from a spider; wider as a male walks off, and the camera's boom is cast against the sill like the orbit's, with a floor of 0.9). It works on a wall as on the floor (its "up" is the surface's). The orbit is a quarter-orbit: 90 degrees in all, a smoothstep of the 15 s centred on the side view (mean 6 degrees a second, about 9 at its quickest in the middle, almost still at both ends). The female mate's turn away (a male player only) is 110 degrees at 11 s over 1.5 s, smoothstepped, in the drawn body only: the simulation's heading is not touched. Reduced Motion: no orbit (the centre held), the reach and the settle held as poses, the turn away at once.
