The camera. `SpiderCamera:setShot(shot)` is given the plan's shot each frame; `_applyShot` runs at the end of `render`, after the orbit camera has been placed, and blends its position, look direction and up toward the shot by a smoothstep of `shotT`, which moves at 1/0.8 a second (0.8 s in; the same 0.8 s back out to the orbit camera, which has not moved) and the blend is `CFrame.lookAt`. The shot's boom is cast, so a wall or a leaf between the pair and the camera shortens it. While a shot is set the player's look input does nothing and the body is shown; the eye view is turned off when the scene begins. A frame's quickness does not change the ease (it is `frameDt` over 0.8).

The poses (`Rig/Living`, `Rig/SpiderRig`, `Data/Appearances`). Two more cues for the living motion, `rig:cue("meet", seconds?)` and `rig:cue("settle", seconds?)`, and `"release"`, which lets both go at once. A cue's seconds are its whole length, ease out included (it is let go `fallSeconds` before its end). Meet: legs I of both sides (indices 1 and 5) go to a reach in the body frame (x 0.14, y -0.02, z -0.74, knee out 0.08 and up 0.16, measured on the first spider: the front foot lifts 0.29 stud off the floor and comes 0.58 stud in toward the midline, ahead of the head), the palps lift 12 degrees and turn 8 toward the midline, rising in 0.8 s, held, falling over 0.8 s, 4 s in all. Settle: the drawn body sinks 0.035 stud toward the surface (measured 0.049 at the adult size; the planted feet stay where they are) and the abdomen's breath is 1.6 times as deep (measured 1.52 times in the pulse's size; the ramp is slower than the dial). Grooming and the accents give way to both (a bout in progress is cut short and none begins while they hold; the breath goes on; an alarm still wins the legs). The first spider's numbers are the look's; the peacock spider's are the same at its 0.47 size (reach x 0.066, z -0.35; lower 0.016), the tarantulas inherit the first spider's (their idle table is a clone), and the ant mimic has no Living at all, so the cues are no-ops for it, as every cue already was (this look has no pose of its own for them, and I did not draw one). The mate's rig is the low tier and had no living motion; `SpiderRig.new(..., living)` gives a low rig the same `_living` for the same parts (the part count is the plain low rig's, a test pins it) and `MateStage.drawMate` makes it so. The scene gives the player's rig `focusPoint` at the mate (the body turns a little toward it, ROBLOX-87's attention), and the mate's at the player's spider while the pair are together.

Who gets which cue: `MatingScene.cues(from, to)` gives the cues that fell due in (from, to], so a scene entered late gives the ones already passed. "meet" at 3 s, 4 s, to both; "settle" at 7 s for 4 s to the male rig (it rises at 11 s and goes) and 8 s to the female (she stays). The male rig is the player's when the player is the male, the mate's when not. A skip, or the end, sends "release" to both.

The controls. Locked from the first tick to the end: `tickInput` returns the idle input, and the handlers for feed, drop, display, change spider and eye are nothing; the dance and any aim are dropped at the start (`dropAll`, `ant.stopDisplay`). The jump and cancel buttons are the skip (nothing before 4 s; after, the skip), and a press of jump that began in the scene is swallowed to its end like a grip's press (`gripPress`), so releasing it afterwards cannot jump. Backspace (return to the last rest) and the menu are not locked: they are the escapes, and each ends the scene's presentation (`mateLegs.reset()`, `endLife` and a return all go through `mateLegs.coupleEnd(false)`, which gives the camera, the music and the rigs back at once, with no notebook line). A meal in the legs is held by an Automatic grip for the scene's length (`prey.feeding.auto` and the rival grip's `auto` are or-ed with the scene), so a fly caught when the pair came together is not lost to a locked hand; a grip that was not given is the player's again after. A pause menu or the notebook stops the scene with the sim.

The sound. `Soundscape:setSwell(on)`: the ambient music swells to 1.6 times itself over 1.0 s (0.18 in the mix to 0.288 at full volume; a slew of 0.6 a second) and returns the same way after; the wasp's duck still wins: the swell is not asked for while the duck is under way, and since a wasp ends the scene the music is simply ducked as before. The birds, the room and every other voice are untouched, and the volume settings multiply it as they multiply the duck. No new asset (the swell is a volume).

The words (`UI/MateText`). Captions by beat, gated by the Captions setting like the others, device-neutral (no button is named), and not shown while a wasp is: "She turns to face him." (to a male player: "She turns to face you."), "They touch, very lightly, with their front legs and palps.", "They settle together, still.", "He goes. The sill is yours again." (to a male player: "She stays. You go on."). The old one-caption `PAIR_CAPTION` is gone. The "mated" notice (`MateText.MATED`, "They have parted. ...") is held until the scene is over (for a male mate that is four seconds after the pause, so it is not read over the two-shot as a caption), the "courted" notice is said as before.

The notebook. The brief's "things you saw" did not exist in this port, so the lightest mechanism is a small one, not a page: `FieldNotebook:addEntry(id, day, text)` and `entriesFor(id)` (at most 6 lines to a page, the oldest dropped; the same line twice is once; a line for a page the book does not have is nothing; the lines show only once the page is found, and add nothing to what is found), `NotebookText.seenText` ("Things you saw:" and the lines) and the panel shows them under the page's "In this game" line. The scene adds one line to "Eggs, silk and wind" at its end: "Day 4: A male came to the sill. The two spiders stayed together a while, and then he went." (for the male player's female mate: "Day 4: A female was on the sill. The two spiders stayed together a while, and then you went on."). Two limits to know: the page opens at the hatching (its trigger is unchanged), so the line is first read there; and the lines belong to the session, not the record (the save has no field for them and I did not add one: `toRecord` and `load` are untouched, so a reload or a return to a rest loses them; the day and who came are still in the cycle's record, so a derived line is possible later). No new sourced page was added and the page's text and sources are untouched (`notebook_reference.json` agrees).

What did not change: the courtship (8 s meter, 0.25 a second decay, the face and stand-still conditions, the peacock's dance), the mate's arrival, three mornings to a mate, the sac made by the night's sleep, two guard days, the hatching scene and its card, Carry on and the generations, the wasp, the rival, any `*_reference` file, `Growth`, `NeedsModel`, `Feeding`, `LifeClock`, the notebook pages and the save format. The scene's flag is a new field of a record that old records lack and that every old record restores without.

Tests, and the pins that moved on purpose. `tests/mating.luau` (545 to 643): the pause is eleven seconds with a male mate (it was five, in the two places the whole-cycle tests said so, and the walk-and-court test now waits twelve seconds); new cases for the plan (beats at each edge, the lock, the skip, the cues' (from, to] rule, the seeded plan's determinism and ranges over 60 draws, all four of its dials varying, a monotone slow-at-the-ends quarter-orbit of exactly 90 degrees, the reduced orbit as the centre held, the turn away's timing, the shot's direction, elevation, distance, side-on at the sweep's middle, wall and coincident cases, Reduced Motion static), the scene in the simulation (its clock, the wasp hold, both pair lengths, the walk off as the last beat, who stays, the sac chain after it), the skip (before four seconds nothing, after, forced, in the walk off, for a female mate, with nothing to skip), the record (validity, an old record) and the checkpoint refusal (both states, a real captured record with the field stripped restoring and its pause ending by its own clock). `tests/client_mating.luau` (12 to 17): the words (every caption at every edge, both sexes, device-neutral and child-safe wording, the entry text); the courting test's pause now eleven seconds and the notice held to the scene's end; and four scene cases through the real client: the camera easing in over 0.8 s (half of it in 0.4 s), the orbit going round by 25 to 80 degrees between 4.6 s and 9 s, looking at the pair from above at 3.5 to 14 studs, the controls locked (walk, feed, drop, eye, dance, jump and cancel before four seconds do nothing), the cues reaching both rigs (the mate's `_living` included) with the male's four seconds and the female's eight, the breath deeper, the music at 1.6 and back to 1.0, a refused rest in the pause and in the walk off, the skip by jump and by cancel after four seconds, the camera back over 0.8 s, the departure, the notebook's line; a played-out scene with the notice held; Reduced Motion (static camera, held poses); a wasp (the controls the player's at once, the music ducked and not swelled); the male player's mirror (the peacock spider, the female mate, "She turns to face you.", the turn away of 110 degrees, "She stays. You go on.", the 15 s pause); and a return to the last rest mid-scene leaving nothing of it. `tests/idle.luau` (5685 to 5719): the knobs, the cues' timing, the poses, release, Reduced Motion, a look without the cue, the rig's reach, sink, breath and part counts; two old checks moved on purpose (the peacock spider now has living motion, for the scene's two cues only: its fan still does not pulse and it still neither grooms nor raises an alarm). `tests/settings.luau` (1082 to 1094): the swell, its return, the multiplied volumes, the duck winning. `tests/notebook.luau` (876 to 893): the lines. The counts that did not move: `growth` 460, `life` 20882, `prey` 1646, `rival` 2724, `rivalnav` 293, `hud` 86, `checkpoint` 225, `client_rival` 5, `client_smoke` 24, `continuing` 87, `wasp` 16884, `look` 1009, `peacock` 129, `tarantula` 86, `antmimic` 88, `client_ant` 3, `eyes` 231, and no golden file is in the commit.

Judgment calls. The pause is 11 s for a male mate and 15 s for a female one, not 15 for both, because the brief's last beat has the male walk off in 11 to 15 and the sim's pause is what the pair wait for; a female mate has no walk off, so her scene is all pause. The client forces a skip for a wasp. A held meal is kept by the Automatic grip for the scene. The "mated" notice waits. The notebook line is session-only. The ant mimic gets no reach (it has no Living). Not run in Studio or on a device (nothing has): how the camera, the ease, the quarter-orbit, the reach and the sink look; whether 0.049 stud of sink and a 1.5 times breath read as "settle" at the camera's distance of 6 to 9 studs; whether the touch reads as a touch with a 0.29 stud lift and no contact between the legs (the front feet end 0.28 stud apart across the midline, not touching: it is a gesture, not a contact); whether the camera's boom is cut by the sill's pieces at the crack's mouth or at a wall; the music swell on a real mix; whether eleven seconds feels long when it is the third time (the skip is there from four); and the touch-screen's Jump and Cancel as the skip.

### ROBLOX-90 - Inspect mode: a slow, close look at the spider, from the Settings or by holding the choose key, with the game still running (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

Nathan, after the anatomy, fur and living-motion rounds (ROBLOX-84 to 87): the detail is there but the camera never gets close enough, long enough, to see it. So there is now a close-up that is a moment of the player's own choosing: the camera leaves the usual view and goes round the spider, close, while the spider goes on breathing, grooming and watching. It is only a camera. Nothing is drawn that was not drawn, and nothing in the simulation knows it is on.

Getting in. Two ways, both for a spider that is standing safely. The Settings has a new button under Spider, in Your spider: "Inspect your spider", with the word "Look closely" on it and the note "A slow, close look from every side. Only while you stand safely. Cancel leaves." It is a live row like the Spider's (`SettingsSpec.LIVE[2]`, id `Inspect`, kind `"action"`; a button, not a value: it is never saved, never read from a save, never reset and never in `ids()` or `defaults()`; `SettingsModel.step` answers true and `valueText` the word). Switching it (a tap on the button or the row, Enter, Space, E, a controller's A; Left and Right do nothing to it) tells the client once, which closes the panel into the look, or, if it will not now, answers a line the panel shows ("You cannot look closely now. Stand safely on a surface first") and says it as a toast too. Opened from the life menu it still closes the panel into the game, not back to the menu. The other way is a hold: the choose key (C, RB / R1 on a controller, the touch Change button) held for `InspectView.HOLD_SECONDS` = 0.6 s while resting awake in the crack. The crack's prompt gains "hold C: look closely" after the change-spider hint, for the device in use. The cost, which is a change to something that was settled: a tap of that key still changes spider, but now when it is let go, not when it goes down (a tap is anything shorter than 0.6 s), so the key can tell a tap from a hold. If something else takes the key away mid-hold (focus lost, a menu, the spider leaving the crack) the hold is dropped and nothing changes. On a controller RB is also the dance key outside the crack; there it is the dance as it was, at once.

The camera. `SpiderCamera:beginInspect()` takes the camera from the pose it has to an orbit round the drawn spider, and `endInspect()` gives it back. The ease is 0.8 s in and 0.8 s out (the mating two-shot's own, `InspectView.EASE_SECONDS`), a smoothstep blend of position, look direction and up from the pose the camera had when it began (or, going out, from the last pose it gave) to the pose it has; the orbit camera behind it has not moved, so out is simply back where it was. A leave that finds the ease half done turns back from there. Where the view is: `InspectView` keeps a yaw (0 in front of the spider, 90 on its right, 180 behind), an elevation and a distance in body-lengths, and the camera sits that far from a focus on the drawn body (the motor's position less the rig's drop, so it is the body and not the feet). The body-length is the drawn body's, `SpiderRig:bodyLength()` = 0.97 stud times the growth stage (1.455 as an adult, 0.455 and 0.68 for the peacock spider, 0.485 and 0.73 for the ant mimic), so a small spider is looked at as closely as a large one. It begins on the side of the spider the camera was on, 1.6 body-lengths away and 20 degrees above the surface's plane ("slightly above"). The distance is between 1.1 and 3.0 body-lengths; the elevation between 4 and 88 degrees. The boom is cast against the sill like the orbit's, so a wall or the crack's roof shortens it (in the crack the test sees it settle between 0.85 and 1.6 body-lengths), recovering at the camera's own speed, and it is never allowed inside 0.9 of a body-length of the focus. The body is always shown, even in the crack. The eye view, if it was on, is switched off for the look and given back on leaving, and `V` in the look cycles the framings and is not the eye view.

Controls. Orbit and elevation are the player's look, by mouse, right stick or a touch drag, through the same sensitivity and inversion settings as the game's camera (a look to the right moves the camera to the spider's right; a look down lifts it; a full push of the stick is about 150 degrees a second at the default speed). Zoom: the wheel (a notch is 12 percent of the distance, the third-person camera's own 0.88), W and S or the left stick (forward is closer, 1.0 body-length a second at a full push, so from 1.6 to the near end takes half a second and from the near end to the far about two), the touch arrows, and a pinch on a touch screen (`InspectView.pinch`: fingers spreading by a ratio is that much closer; the game's own camera has never taken a pinch and still does not). The Eye key (V, Y, the touch View) cycles three preset framings, with a toast ("Framing: Face"): the face (yaw 0, 10 degrees up, 1.25 body-lengths, the focus 0.3 body-length ahead of the body's middle, on the head), the side (on the right, 6 degrees up, 1.8) and from above (84 degrees up, 2.0, the head up the screen), and round to the face again. A framing is reached by an exponential approach, 95 percent of the way in 0.35 s (the shortest way round in yaw), and is held still; the player's own turn or zoom cancels a trip on its way. The slow orbit: 10 degrees a second, once the player has been idle 3 s (`AUTO_AFTER_SECONDS`): a new view counts as idle already, so it begins turning at once (a full turn takes 36 s). Any turn, zoom, wheel notch or pinch starts the 3 s again; a framing stops the orbit until the player next turns the view. The spider and the world: while it holds, the motor gets the idle input and the buttons that act are nothing (feed, drop, focus, gait, slow, recenter, the dance, a rest, the walk), so a key held into it is let go with no penalty (the same `clear` a menu uses) and nothing is left half pressed. The touch screen shows only the arrows, Back (the Cancel button) and View (the Eye button), and gets its buttons back on leaving. The screen shows the spider's name ("The first spider", the peacock spider and so on) and one line of controls, for the device in use, and none of the hunger, day, notices, status or captions; H still shows the controls card, which has a new line, "Look closely at your spider: Settings, or hold C in the crack. Mouse orbits, V frames it, Q leaves", in the words of the device (ControlText names; PlayStation names on a PlayStation controller).

Not paused. The game goes on. The simulation's clock and the world's run as always (a test lets 1.3 s go by and sees the prey's time and the day's hour move), and the living motion runs (the abdomen breathes by 1 to 6 percent in the test's 1.3 s; the grooming, the glance and the cues are the rig's own). The Roblox menu, the life menu, the notebook and the Settings pause the world as they always did, and so end the look: a paused world has no business holding the camera. What ends it, and how. Cancel (Q, B, the touch Back), a jump (Space, A: the press ends it and is nothing else; the rest of the press is swallowed, so letting go cannot hop), the choose key or RB again (it leaves and is not also a change of spider), and the life menu, the notebook, the Settings, the catch screen or Roblox's own menu opening (a cut back, with nothing said). Dangers end it at once, as a cut, with a toast, "The close look ends: something is coming": a wasp's warning (`visit_started`, and any visit under way) and the rival's windup or lunge, and the player's controls are the game's the same frame, so the player is never trapped in a view while something comes. Anything that stops the spider being safe to look at ends it the same way with "The close look ends": seized, winding up or in a jump, moulting, the fly struggle, the mating scene or the hatching, and sleep, all as a cut. Backspace, a new life and a return to the last rest end it too. Reduced Motion: no slow orbit, the 0.8 s eases are cuts (the view is in on the first frame, and leaving is a cut), and a framing is a cut; the player's own orbit and zoom work as they do. Nothing is saved: no attribute, no setting, no choice of spider.

Where it works. Standing on any surface (the sill, a leaf, a wall: the frame is the spider's own, its drawn up and its heading in the surface's plane), resting in the crack, and hanging on a locked dragline. The change of spider refuses a line (`SpiderChoice.safeToChange`); the close look is not that rule, so there is a new one beside it, `SpiderChoice.safeToInspect`: attached or on a locked line, and not winding up, in the air, seized, moulting or holding a struggling fly (a jump in flight has a safety line, which is why "on a line" is not enough: it must not be in flight). On the line the motor, the line and the sway go on and the controls are the look's (W zooms; it does not reel; a tap of Cancel leaves the look and does not let go of the line; what a Cancel held through the leaving does to the line is not tested), and when the look ends the line's own prompt is back. The low tier is allowed: it is a camera, the same on both, and the tier changes only what is drawn.

What deliberately does not change. The simulation (the clock, hunger, growth, the wasp, the rival, the mating, the saves, the checkpoints, the motor, the jump, the silk), the drawing (no new part, no new asset, no new sound; the hair, sheen and legs are what ROBLOX-84 to 87 made), the low tier's parts, the game's own orbit camera and its settings (field of view, distance, framing, eye level horizon, the inversion and the speeds), the eye view, the mating two-shot (the look cannot begin while it plays, and its shot is not given to the look), the save format, Godot's goldens and every `*_reference` file. The client is still at 199 top-level locals (the limit is 200): the state is in the existing `spiderPick` table (`inspecting`, `hold`, `jumpSwallow`, `touchOn`, the two modules, and the functions `refusal`, `begin`, `finish`, `press`, `release`, `update`, `touchFrame`, `safeLook`, `inspectFromSettings`), and `spiderPick` itself is handed to the Studio playback driver.

Numbers, as dials in `Shared/Movement/InspectView`: `HOLD_SECONDS` 0.6, `EASE_SECONDS` 0.8, `DISTANCE` 1.6, `ELEVATION_DEG` 20, `AUTO_DEG_S` 10, `AUTO_AFTER_SECONDS` 3, `ZOOM_MIN` 1.1, `ZOOM_MAX` 3.0, `ZOOM_PER_SECOND` 1.0, `WHEEL_STEP` 0.88, `ELEVATION_MIN_DEG` 4, `ELEVATION_MAX_DEG` 88, `PRESET_EASE_SECONDS` 0.35, and `PRESETS` (face, side, top). The words are `UI/InspectText` (the button, the touch Back and View, the refusal, the two endings, the framings, the controls line and the card's line).

Tests, and the pins that moved on purpose. New `tests/inspect.luau`, 36 checks: the pure view (the numbers; the yaw convention and a unit direction; the auto-orbit's 3 s idle, its 10 degrees a second at 30, 60 and 240 frames a second and its off in Reduced Motion; the wrap and the elevation's limits; the zoom, the wheel, the pinch and every bad ratio; the three framings, their 95-percent time, the shortest way round, a hold that never drifts and a turn that gives the orbit back; the 0.8 s ease in both directions, a reversal part-way, long and zero frames, 30 frames a second, the smoothstep; 400 seeded runs of mixed turns, zooms, pinches, framings and frames that never leave the limits; `safeToInspect` for every state), the words (every device, PlayStation names), the rig's body-length on both tiers, and the real client: a tap and a hold of the choose key (0.55 s a tap, 0.67 s a hold), the refusals (outside the crack, seized, preparing, the struggle, moulting, the mating and hatching scenes), the Settings row from the game and from the life menu and refused, the ease (half at 0.4 s, whole at 0.8 s), 1.6 body-lengths and 20 degrees, the body shown, the 30 degrees in 3 s, the stick, a touch drag, a wheel notch and a pinch (and a pinch the interface took), W and S between 1.1 and 3, the framings and their toasts, the controls that are nothing, the screen, the living breath and the clock, leaving by Cancel (half back at 0.4 s, exactly where it was) and by the choose key, the eye view given back, the hold in the crack, a controller, the touch screen, a hold lost mid-way, a jump, each menu, the line, a wasp, the rival's windup, each unsafe state, Backspace, Reduced Motion, and nothing kept. `tests/settings.luau` (1094 to 1151): the Inspect row (an action, in the table, nothing saved, in the nav after Spider, a 160-wide button, Left and Right nothing, Enter, Space, a tap on the button and the row, a refusal, Reset not pressing it, a readable size on a phone, in landscape and at large text), and in the real client the setting reaching the camera, a controller's A closing the panel into the look, the world running in it, and the Reduced Motion cut. `tests/client_ant.luau`: one tolerance, 0.03 to 0.045 (the tarantula's foreleg after the pose eases out), because the tap of the choose key now ends two frames later, which moves the breath's phase by about 0.01 in that leg; nothing else about the pose moved. `tests/studio/SettingsSpec.luau` (two live rows, 25 rows, the button a touch target, not run) and `tests/shim.luau` (the stub input service has a `TouchPinch` signal). The counts: `inspect` 36, `settings` 1151, `look` 1009, `idle` 5719, `client_ant` 3, `client_mating` 17, `client_rival` 5, `client_smoke` 24, all unmoved but the two named; `peacock` 129, `eyes` 231, `gaze` 74 and `growth` 460 also run and unchanged. Type check clean, all 136 modules compile, every new module `--!strict`.

Judgment calls. The ease is 0.8 s, not the 0.6 an earlier cut of this work used, to match the two-shot. The slow orbit is "when the player is idle" (3 s after the last input, at once on entry) rather than a single orbit that the first touch ends for good, so a player who puts the pad down gets the spider turned for them again; the 3 s and the 10 degrees are guesses. A framing holds still rather than drifting. A jump press leaves the look and is nothing else. A menu leaves it as a cut with no words (they would be read over the menu). The hold key works in the crack, and the Settings button anywhere, the line included. The tap of the choose key moves to its release. The refusal is one line for every reason. The pinch is mine: the brief named it and the camera had none. Not run in Studio or on a device (nothing here has): how any of it looks or feels; whether 1.1 body-lengths shows the face and the eyes as closely as it should, or the far end of 3.0 is far enough for the whole body at a wide field of view; whether the crack's walls leave a framing worth having; whether the hanging body on the line is centred (the focus is the drawn body less the rig's drop, checked only in the box world's sill and a leaf, where it was 0.5 to 3 body-lengths from the abdomen and in the middle of the picture); whether the real `TouchPinch` event order is what the handler expects (Begin, then Change with a cumulative scale) and whether a two-finger pinch also reads as a one-finger drag and turns the view; the touch Back and View buttons' layout on a phone; whether 0.6 s is a comfortable hold on a controller; and the line of controls at large text on a narrow screen.

### ROBLOX-91 - The audit, part one: the sim (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

An audit of the sim after ROBLOX-88 to -90 found sixteen things: four bugs, nine risks and three improvements. Every one is fixed here, in the game's own rules or in the glue around them: Godot's defaults (`NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `RivalSpider` with no rules given) are untouched, so every golden fixture holds (`life_reference`, `growth_reference`, `rival` and `wasp` goldens: none moved). Seeded draws still come from the absolute day and the seed. Every new record field is optional and an older record restores (checkpoint validation tolerates the lack of it).

The bugs.

1. The rival and the mating scene. The rival walked into courtship and could lunge at a pair in the locked scene. `Rival.step` now takes `scene` (`Mate.sceneActive`: the scene flag, or the pair state) and `courting` (`Mate.courting`: a courtable mate whose meter is above 0 or which is within `MatingRules.nearWu` of the spider) and, like the wasp's visit, pauses with `phase(r,"recover"); r.notice = 0`. A dormant rival does not arrive during either. The client orders the tick as rival step, then `Mating.tick`; if the rival is already winding up or lunging as the meter fills (`ms.rivalThreat`), `Mating.tick` turns the "courted" event into "courted" then "mated" by `Mate.skip(mate, true)`: the scene is skipped and the pair part, rather than a locked player being lunged at.
2. The tear-free re-catch. A rival that tore free of the grip was in `recover`, which is not aware, so the next strike was a free catch and the contest of ROBLOX-73 could be skipped for ever (tear free, strike, tear free). `tearFree` now sets `r.shaken = true`; `Rival.aware` is true for `recover` while shaken, so a strike is the contest again (caught 40 / sprang 32 / counter-bite 28, Gentle 55 / 25 / 20). `phase()` clears `shaken` on a real change of state (a pause that re-enters recover keeps it). `Rival.valid` accepts an optional boolean `shaken`; old records have none and restore.
3. Gentle (Easy and Free play) had ROBLOX-88's pacing. That change was meant for the standard rules but reached the gentle ones through `NeedsModel.game.drain` (1.9, so the gentle half-rate day cost 0.306), `Growth.game.moultFlies` (five) and `RivalRules.contest*` (40 / 32 / 28). Now: `NeedsModel.game.gentleDrain` = 1.1 (ROBLOX-61's factor; with the gentle half rate 11 x 0.021 x 1.1 x 0.5 + 13 x 0.007 x 1.1 x 0.5 = 0.127 + 0.050 = 0.177 a day), `Growth.game.gentleMoultFlies` = 3 (`Growth:useGameRules(gentle)` sets the threshold to 3 x 0.3 = 0.9; the HUD says "of 3 meals" in Easy and Free play and "of 5 meals" otherwise), `RivalRules.contestGentle` = 55 / 25 / 20 (`Rival.contest(seed, day, n, gentle)`; the step reads `s.gentle`). The counter-bite costs nothing there as before; the gentle grip (`Catch.forRival(true)`) was never changed. The client threads the flag at startup, in the tick (a family that carries on has a new growth), in a return to the last rest and in `applyRules`. All three are pinned per mode in `tests/life.luau`, `tests/growth.luau` and `tests/rival.luau`.
4. The starvation streak reset on any meal. One tiny catch a day at 0% energy cleared the 24 and 48 hour clocks, so a spider on crumbs could never starve. In the game's rules (`NeedsModel.game.streakDecay`) a meal takes `amount / rateAwake` hours off the streak (the awake rate is 0.021 x 1.9 = 0.0399 an hour, so a catch worth 0.04 is one hour) and ends it altogether only when the reserve is above `streakEndsAbove` = 0.1. Godot's full reset stays the default (the golden life_reference pins it), and both are tested.

The risks.

5. The checkpoint carried two live profiling windows: the prey director's `cpu` (the last 1200 ticks' microseconds) and the sensor's cost `_ring`. `Checkpoint.capture` now swaps both for empty tables around the capture and `restore` leaves them empty, so a record is smaller (17786 characters against 21799 in the test world) and two captures at the same moment are identical. `Sensor.costPercentile` tolerates an empty ring.
6. A rest at a full-grown spider that was too hungry for a visitor said nothing. `Mating.tooHungry(life)` (adult, no cycle begun, no morning set, energy under 0.6) adds `MateText.TOO_HUNGRY`, "Too hungry for a visitor to come. Eat well, then rest in the crack again.", to the rest's message.
7. The first day and the first meal protect each generation. The rival and the wasp counted the sill's raw day and the total meals of every family, so a spiderling had no grace. A carried-on life records `life.born` (the day before it began) and `life.mealsBase` (the meals finished so far); `Mating.grace(life, meals)` gives the generation's own day base and meals, which the rival (`s.born`, `s.meals`) and the wasp (`born`, `meals`) read. `Mating.carryOn` sets them. Both are validated (whole numbers 0 to 1e9); an older record has none and means the first generation. `WaspDirector.MAX_SLOTS` is `#Data.game.windows` (two), not a literal.
8. The guard-day death rule, written down. Dying on a guard day gives two choices. "Back to your last rest" restores the last rest as it was: the sac and the generation stay. "Continue as another spider" is a new life (the same call as the menu's new spider): the sac is lost and the generation is 0. The CaughtPanel says so while a sac exists: "Continue as another spider (a new life: the brood is lost)" (`MateText.another(hasSac)`); without a sac it is the plain "Continue as another spider".
9. Easy and standard shared one rest slot, so a new Easy life replaced a standard life's rest. The slots are now main, free and easy, keyed by the rules (`SaveRules.slotOf(mode, rules)`): the server's keys `R_<uid>_main`, `_free` and the new `_easy`. Existing standard saves keep their key; an Easy rest left in the main slot by an earlier build is still accepted there (`SaveRest.check("main")` takes any main-mode record) and still loads. The menu offers "Continue the Easy life from its last rest". Tested in `state` (the server) and `state_client` (the real client: a new Easy life leaves the standard rest byte for byte, and its rest goes up as `easy` and passes `SaveRest.check("easy")`).
10. `SaveService.shutdown` counted a player still loading as departing, and the loader then counted it again, so `departing` could go below zero. A `counted` flag on the entry makes each departure count once; tested.
11. `feeding:useGameRules()` is now called on every restore (a record from before, or one made while a rival was held, may hold Godot's struggle or the rival's), not only at start.
12. `rival_carried_off` is acted on only when `life.rival.state == "taken"`: a late word for a rival that was renewed in the meantime no longer consumes the new one.
13. `Rival.renew` is relative to the morning, not the raw day: `Rival.morning(day, hour)` is the day if the hour is 07:00 or later and the day before otherwise. A rival eaten at 06:00 on day 5 belongs to the morning of day 4 and the next comes at 07:00 on day 5; one eaten at 20:00 on day 4 also has the morning of day 4. The record stamps `hour` (optional, 0 to under 24; an older record means the arrival hour).

The improvements.

14. Stale text: "no Gentle mode", "no Easy mode yet" and "starvation not ported" in the comments of `SpiderClient`, `NeedsModel`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`, `WaspText` and `Data/Catch` (the rival's comment now has ROBLOX-88's figures); `MateText.SAC` now ends "Rest in the crack to pass the nights."; `tools/gen_notebook_pages.py` says "In Easy and Free play the wasp only touches and leaves.", "four growth stages (three moults)" and, of going without food, "On Easy and in free play it empties much more slowly and cannot starve." `NotebookPages.luau` was regenerated (three lines), the notebook reference is unchanged.
15. One credit formula. `Growth.rivalCredit(energy)` (energy x `READY_FOOD` / (3 x the fly's nutrition)) replaces the two copies in the client; `Growth.mealsOf(energy)` is the housefly meals it comes to, and the status line "Eating rival — stay still (2 meals)" is derived from it (`RivalText.eating`).
16. The rival's grip jerks came from a seed of (seed, day, contests), so a rival that tore free and was caught again, with no contest between, jerked at exactly the same moments. `RivalGrip` now adds a per-grip counter, `grips` in the rival's record (validated, a whole number 0 to 1e9; an older record has none), to the seed. The first grip (grips 0) is the seed it always was, so no feel pin moved.

The gentle modes against the standard rules.

| | standard | Easy and Free play |
|---|---|---|
| drain factor | 1.9 | 1.1 (and the gentle half rate) |
| energy a day | 0.612 | 0.177 (was 0.306 since ROBLOX-88) |
| a full reserve lasts | 1.63 days | 5.6 days (cannot starve) |
| flies to a moult | 5 (1.5) | 3 (0.9) |
| contest, caught / sprang / counter-bite | 40 / 32 / 28 | 55 / 25 / 20 |
| a counter-bite costs | 0.30 | nothing |
| the rival's grip | `Catch.rival` | the house fly's, unchanged |
| the starving streak | decays by meals (reset above 0.1) | not counted |

Pins that moved on purpose. `tests/life.luau`: the gentle ratio is 1.1 and the standard 1.9 pins stay, plus the gentle day and the streak decay (20882 to 20902). `tests/growth.luau`: gentle needs three flies, "of 3 meals", `rivalCredit`, `mealsOf` and the derived "(2 meals)" (460 to 477). `tests/rival.luau`: the gentle counter-bite test uses gentle days against standard days; "a recovering rival can be caught again" became the shaken contest; new blocks for the scene and courting pause, the renewal by morning, `grips` (2724 to 2830). `tests/mating.luau`: `tooHungry`, `sceneActive` and `courting`, the threat skip, the generation grace, the validation of `born` and `mealsBase` (643 to 715). `tests/checkpoint.luau`: the profiling windows, and the gentle contest outcome (225 to 239). `tests/state.luau`: the Easy slot and the shutdown accounting (457 to 484). `tests/state_client.luau`: the Easy slot through the real client, the HUD's "of 3 meals" and "of 5 meals", and `feeding:useGameRules` on restore (104 to 123). `tests/client_rival.luau`: the gentle counter-bite draws with the gentle odds, the "(2 meals)" status, and the guard on a late "carried off" word (5 to 6). `tests/client_mating.luau`: the rival keeps away from the pair and the scene, the too-hungry notice, the end card's brood-lost choice (17 to 20). The wasp, notebook, hud, rivalnav and state_offline suites run and are unchanged (16884, 893, 86, 293, 8).

Not run in Studio or on a server: the real DataStore key for the `easy` slot and a player leaving while loading; how the lower gentle pacing feels over a real Easy life (the numbers are ROBLOX-61's, from before ROBLOX-88); whether three flies a moult and 55 / 25 / 20 are the right ease now that the standard rules are harder. Not touched: the standard pacing of ROBLOX-88.

### ROBLOX-92 - The audit, part two: the client (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

An audit of the client after ROBLOX-89 to -91 found seventeen things: seven bugs, eight risks and two groups of improvements. Every one is fixed here, in the client and the words it shows; the sim's rules, Godot's defaults and every golden fixture are untouched. Player words live in `Shared/UI/*Text` with the device's names from `ControlText`.

The bugs.

1. A failing save stopped the frame. `updateSaves` ran bare in the render step, so an error in the notebook's record or the sender ended every frame after it. It now runs inside `saveState.frame()` in a `pcall`; on failure `saveState.broken` is set, one `warn` is logged, a toast and the status line say "Saving is off" (`SaveText.BROKEN`), the menu's line says why (`SaveText.brokenLine`), the Settings panel's status reads "session", and nothing more is sent or rested up (`life.save` guards its `sendRest` the same way). `state_client`: a throwing notebook leaves the frame running, the world ticking, one warning, no more messages.
2. The mating scene never showed, and the controls were locked for the scene's 15 seconds, when a close look had begun from the eye view: the look ended, gave the eye view back over the two-shot, and the camera drew the eye view. `mateLegs.takeView()` (the scene's start and the hatching's) calls `cam:cancelEyeRestore()` and ends the look first; `SpiderCamera.setShot` also clears the restore. The eye view is off for the scene as it always was and is not given back afterwards: it is the player's to turn on again. `client_mating`: inspect from the eye view, then the scene (the two-shot plays, a skip frees the controls, the eye view stays off), and the same for the hatching.
3. The Spider settings row was allowed in the mating and hatching scenes. `spiderPick.occupied()` (a scene, the hatching, sleep, a wasp's visit, the rival's windup) is now the gate of both the close look (`refusal`) and the row (`safe`), and the row says its own `PeacockText.NOT_NOW`. Choosing a spider before a life has begun still works (the start menu): only the in-world conditions are shared.
4. A Cancel held after leaving the close look let go of the line (the fresh press counted for the release clock). `spiderPick.cancelSwallow` swallows the rest of that press until its release, as `jumpSwallow` does for Jump. `inspect`: hanging on the line, Q held 1.5 s after it left the look; the line holds.
5. A pinch in the close look also orbited on touch: each finger's `TouchMoved` turned the view. `SpiderInput` now counts the fingers that are down and not on a button, and the pinch under way, and gives `touchLook` only for one free finger outside a pinch. `inspect`: a pinch does not orbit, two fingers down after the pinch do not, one finger does.
6. `SpiderCamera:snap()` left `shotT`, `lastShot`, `inspectT` and `inspectLast`, so a return to a rest (or a respawn) during an ease blended a stale pose into the snapped view. It zeroes them. `client_mating` snaps during the two-shot's ease out.
7. The controls card broke the ten-line rule and PromptSpec measured the wrong string. The one string the HUD shows is now `PeacockText.card(device, display)` (the base card, the crack's line, the Settings pointer and the display key's line); the Studio PromptSpec measures it for every device, every display key and both pad styles. The card was cut: "(five spiders)" is gone, the close look's own line is folded into the crack's (`PeacockText.crackLine`: "In the crack: C change spider (hold C: look closely)"), the keyboard's long crack line is two short ones, and the device names come from `ControlText` (the touch card said "Change change spider"; it says "tap Change"). Every action is still on it. `tests/peacock.luau` pins the greedy wrap at 61 characters (516 px at 15 px GothamMedium) for the 24 combinations.

Lines on the card, the worst of every display key and pad style (wrapped at 61 characters):

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| keyboard | 13 | 9 |
| gamepad | 14 | 10 |
| touch | 11 | 10 |

The risks.

8. The scene's skip was unannounced. From 4 seconds (`MatingScene.canSkip`) the status shows "{jump} or {cancel}: part" (`MateText.skipLine`: "Space or Q: part", "A or B: part", "Skip or Cancel: part"), and on touch the Jump button reads "Skip" (`MateText.TOUCH_SKIP`), as it reads "Grip" in a struggle. It is a status line, not a sound caption, so the Captions setting does not hide it.
9. The tarantula notebook page hardcoded "(X)"; it says `{display}`, filled by `NotebookText.fill` with the device's display key (on touch the tarantulas' own button, "Rear up", not the peacock's "Dance"). `tools/gen_notebook_pages.py` carries it and `NotebookPages.luau` is regenerated. The page said "no silk mat is laid" while the card said "lay a silk mat": the card now says "rear up and turn" (and the ant mimic's "wave antennae higher"), with the same shape on every device.
10. The Menu has a "Controls" entry (in a life that has begun) that closes the menu and puts the card up until H puts it away; the card was keyboard-only after its auto-hide. The close look's hint is phrased per device in the crack's line.
11. The mate's "arrived", "noticed" and "gone" notices and its courting line were told wherever the spider was. They now follow the rival's rule (`UI/Heard.within`: within 18 studs by ear, or in clear sight within 24; `WaspText.heardRival` uses it too). An arrival too far to hear is held (`mateLegs.pending`) and told when the visitor first comes within earshot or sight; a leaving too far to hear is not told.
12. Reduced Motion now reaches the mating two-shot: it cuts in and out (`SpiderCamera._applyShot`), where it eased over 0.8 s. The close look's ease was cut already.
13. Touch layout. A short screen (`HudLayout.isShort`: under 450 tall) takes the compact three rows whatever its width, where it was 568 wide and more only. A 480 x 320 phone then fits the top strip only with narrower Menu and Notebook buttons (`HudLayout.TOP_SHORT`: 64 and 92 px wide, still 48 tall, used only when the usual pair would not leave a 300 px column). The 13-size layout test is 15 sizes (480 x 320 and 640 x 360 added), the prompt test 11. The Studio PromptSpec measures the relabels (Rear up, Grip, Back, View, Leave, Sleep, Wake, Skip, Eat, Rest, Stop) at the default and the compact sizes, and the Studio SettingsSpec includes the two live rows (the Spider row with its longest name, the Inspect button) in its fit check.
14. The HUD has one toast label, so a plainer toast replaced a warning of the moment. `Hud.toast` has priorities (information, warning, danger): a lower one waits out the higher and is dropped, an equal or higher one replaces it. The close look's "something is coming" is a danger and stays its 2.6 seconds.
15. A rest that was refused gave an engineer's sentence ("the two spiders are together"). `LifeText.restRefusal` maps each reason of `Checkpoint.boundaryProblem` to the game's own words ("Not now — the two of you are together", "Not while a fight is on", "Not while the brood is hatching", "Not now — keep still first", and so on; a plain fallback for a reason nobody wrote words for, and `checkpoint` tests that every reason is known). `life.save` runs `boundaryProblem` before `Mating.atRest`, so a refused rest no longer sets the mate's morning. "Checkpoint" is gone from every player-facing word: "Last rest kept.", "Returned to your last rest", "No last rest yet", the controls card's "Backspace: last rest".

The improvements.

16. Locals headroom in `SpiderClient`. The script compiled at -O1 but not at -O0 (a function holds 200 registers, and -O0 folds nothing). The modules used only inside functions are one table `M`, and the save state (`prefTouched`, `applyingSaved`, `savedStatus`, `savedReason`, `lastSig`, `lastEye`, `lastEyeCheck`) is `saveState`. No behaviour change. Top-level local names: 202 before, 181 after; the script now compiles at -O0 (`luau-compile -O0`) with room for about 17 more locals, and at -O1 with room for about 58.
17. Small ones. The rival's grip also takes the Eat key (E, L3), and the touch Feed button reads "Grip" with Jump during a struggle; the mate's snapshot of the spider is one table (`mateLegs.state`) filled again each tick, not a clone of the rival's; `Soundscape.playing` leaves a recording alone until it has loaded; the wasp's hum is re-parented only when it changes; a touch Change button hidden under a finger takes the press away (`SpiderInput` passes `cancelled`), so nothing is changed and nothing is said; and a saved spider is put on only when the spider stands safely (`saveState.settleLook`), and not at all if the player has chosen one since.

Notes. The Studio spec measures the card at 516 px (568 less 32 and 20), the width the review used; the stacked HUD column the card sits in is narrower (300 px at the stacked sizes), so whether the card is really ten lines on a phone is for the Studio checklist to say, not the CLI. Pins that moved on purpose: `peacock` (129 to 265: the card, the crack's line, the wrap pins), `hud` (86 to 96: the toast priorities), `tarantula` (85 to 86, the card says "rear up and turn"), `inspect` (36 to 37 checks; the card's line is the crack's; the danger toast lasts), `state_client` (123 to 140), `checkpoint` (239 to 251: "Last rest kept.", the reasons), `notebook` (893 to 900), `client_rival` (6 to 7), `client_mating` (20 to 27: seven new checks), `life` ("a return to the rest"), `client_smoke` (24: the last rest's words, and the checks that read a toast wait out the warning before them), `run` (the layout sizes, 196). Unchanged: settings 1151, antmimic 88, client_ant 3, mating 715, growth 477, wasp 16884.

Not run in Studio: the card's real wrap on a phone (the review's rule is ten lines and the CLI proves it at 61 characters), the 480 x 320 and 640 x 360 layouts, the width of "Skip" and "Rear up" in their buttons (the Studio specs measure them), and whether a visitor heard at 18 studs and seen at 24 is the right reach for a mate.

### ROBLOX-93 - The audit, part three: the rigs and the frame (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-18)

An audit of the drawn rigs and of what a frame asks of the engine, after ROBLOX-85 to -92, found sixteen things: three bugs, ten risks and three improvements. Every one is fixed here, in `SpiderRig`, `Setae`, `Anatomy`, `Idle`, `Living`, the looks' data, `MateStage` and the two calls the client makes for the rival and the mate; the sim and every golden fixture are untouched. The cost was measured, not guessed: the test shim now counts what the engine would be asked (`shim.counters`: Size writes, `BulkMoveTo` calls and the parts they move), and a golden dump of every part's CFrame and Size (six looks, both tiers, 12 sampled frames of the stand, Reduced Motion, walk, condition, growth, hang, jump, cue, dance, ant and threat scenarios) was taken from the HEAD tree and from this one (scratch tools, not kept). The CLI counts calls and parts; it cannot say what a frame costs in Roblox.

The bugs.

1. The mate was rescaled every frame. `MateStage.drawMate` called `setGrowthStage` and `setScale` each frame and each wrote every part's Size: 697 Size writes a frame for a mate standing still. `MateStage` keeps the factor last applied (`scaleFor`, 0 for a new rig) and sets the scale when the rig is made and when the factor changes. `SpiderRig.setGrowthStage` writes its attribute and the juvenile bars only when the stage changes (`_stage`), and `setScale` returns at once for the scale it has (else it counts a scale epoch, `_epoch`, and `refreshScale` works out again what the scale fixes: 7 and 8). A mate standing in Reduced Motion: 697 to 0 Size writes a frame and 267 to 131 parts moved (breathing: 5.75 writes, 250.9 of 265 parts). `client_mating` ("the mate's drawing") and `look` (the same stage and scale again write nothing, a new scale does).
2. The spiderlings' heads floated. A head was 0.6 BODY wide, its centre 0.9 BODY in front of the abdomen's: the radii (0.5 and 0.3) add to 0.8, so the balls were 0.1 BODY (0.014 studs) apart. `MateStage.BODY_AT`, `HEAD_AT`, `HEAD` = -0.35, 0.42, 0.7: the centres are 0.77 apart and the radii add to 0.85, so the head is 0.08 BODY (0.011 studs) into the abdomen. `client_mating` measures every drawn spiderling's overlap in the stage's four beats and in the real hatching (over a hundred sightings): 0.008 to 0.015 studs, never a gap and never swallowed.
3. Hairs through eyes. Sampled root to tip against every eye's ellipsoid on the HEAD tree, the first spider had one body hair through an eye (a tuft hair, skimming it: lowest ellipsoid radius 0.98) and the rival two (tuft hairs, 0.63). `Anatomy.touchesEye(eye, a, b, pad)` is an exact test (the closest approach of a segment to an ellipsoid, found in the ellipsoid's own scaled space, not a sampling); `Setae.body` asks it of every hair root to tip against every eye (`offEyes`, `EYE_PAD` 0.004 studs, a little over half the widest hair) for the tufts and the ruff (`plume` leaves out a hair that touches, and the fans being mirror images, 9, on both sides alike) and, as `coat`'s new `clearSeg`, for the coats, in addition to the root, middle and tip tests they already had. After: no hair through any eye in either look (lowest ellipsoid radius 1.31), and each look loses two hairs at the full tier, a tuft hair a side (the coats lose none): first 695 to 693 parts, rival 624 to 622. `look` samples every hair of both looks at 41 points against every eye by a test that shares nothing with `touchesEye`, shows that the sampler does see a made-up hair through an eye, and aims the tufts at the front eyes (they keep only the hairs that clear, the same number on both sides). Close-ups of both faces rendered before and after.

The risks.

4. A leg's pale bands were given a Size every frame (24 writes a frame on the first spider, 8 legs of 3, standing still). `drawSegment` keeps each link's band length (`Seg.bandX`) and writes the Size only when it is more than `BAND_STEP` (1e-3 studs, the rods' own tolerance) off: a band can be a thousandth of a stud, about a hundredth of its length, off its link (the golden dump's largest size difference, 0.00099).
5. The peacock spider's fan sized each disc, figure and flap twice a frame (its own size, then that times the condition). `drawFan` (a function of the module now, not a method that made three closures a frame) works each part's size out once from `fan.sizes` (what each part holds: nothing is read from the part) and `fanSize` writes it when it is more than 1e-5 off. With 4, the peacock's Size writes a frame: held (Reduced Motion, paused, hanging) 56 to 0, standing 57.9 to 1.9 (the breath), walking 78.9 to 36.1 (what is left is the links' lengths, which do change as a leg steps).
6. The abdomen group worked its places and sizes out again every frame and compared each size with the part's property. A `Fixed` part now carries its place's two halves (`pos`, `rot`), its offset at the rig's scale (`scaled`), its size at scale 1 (`size0`) and the size it holds (`size`). The abdomen's parts and hairs keep the last condition and breath (`Seen.sizeKey`, `Seen.breath`) and are sized and placed again only when either has moved by more than `SCALE_STEP` (1e-4); the big masses, which breathe, follow the breath. A change of scale makes all of it fresh (the epoch).
7. Drawing only what moved. The rig makes two `BulkMoveTo` batches a frame: the body (the root and every part that is not a hair), always, as before, and the hairs, only for the kinds whose frame has moved. The hairs follow one frame each (the head's, the body's, each palp's pivot, the abdomen's, each leg link's); a kind is placed again when its frame has moved by more than `MOVE_STUDS` (1e-4 studs) or `MOVE_RADIANS` (1e-3, tested by the trace of the relative rotation, so no angle is worked out), or the abdomen's scale by `SCALE_STEP`; and the movement is measured from where the hairs were last placed (`Seen`), not from the last frame, so a slow drift is placed once it has added up. `look` runs a rig that places every hair every frame (`_exact`, for tests) beside the real one over 520 frames of standing, walking, growing and hanging: the hairs placed by tolerance stay within 6.7e-4 studs and 1.0e-3 radians of the exact ones (first spider, full), 6.7e-4 and 9.6e-4 (low, drawn as the mate is), 1.8e-4 and 9.9e-4 (redknee), none in size; the test fails with the constants ten times looser. The measure is in the table below. What it shows plainly: a spider standing at the full tier still moves 93% of its parts, because it breathes (the body lifts, the abdomen swells) and tilts its head on nearly every frame, and the body's, head's and abdomen's hairs, which are most of them, follow; the second batch saves a held spider (29%), the low tier standing (60%, it neither breathes nor tilts) and the planted legs' hairs, and walking is every part in two calls instead of one.
8. Per-frame closures and tables, hoisted (a pure refactor): `put`, `scaledFrame` and the leg-segment closure (made once a leg a frame, eight a frame) are module functions (`put`, `scaled`, `drawSegment`); the palps' pivots table and the `palpAngle` tables (made again by each cue that touched them) are numbers (`Idle.palp(t, drum, palp, k)`, `Idle.palps` kept for the tests and the docs); `Idle.pose` fills the rig's own `_pose`; `Idle.snap`'s `pulse` and `Living.stepScene`'s `ease` are functions of their modules; `Living.setLeg` takes numbers (grooming made a five-field table a leg a step; `setLegAt` for a look's spec); `_hangLeg` made three tables and six `math.sin` of a fixed scatter a leg a frame and now keeps the scatter in `leg.noise` and the lengths and joints in locals; `restLocal` and the hip of each leg per leg per frame are `leg.rest` and `leg.hip`, worked out when the scale changes (`refreshScale`); the foothold reaches are `REACHES`; the batches are tables the rig keeps and clears in place. Proof: `look` (1417) and `idle` (5719) pass, and the golden dump compared with HEAD's: peacock and ant mimic, 342 sampled frames and 29694 parts, largest position difference 0, rotation 2e-19, size 0.00099 (the bands, 4); redknee, every part but the setae, 16684 parts, 0, 0, 0.00099; the first spider and the rival, every part but the setae, 0 in position and rotation in every scenario except the mating cues, where the meet pose moved on purpose (11; at most 0.038 studs). Luau time of an update under the CLI (indicative only): first spider full standing 8.5 to 7.7 ms, walking 8.8 to 7.6.
9. Symmetric thinning. The low tier kept one hair in `every` of a kind counted over both sides, so an odd number to a side kept different hairs, and a different number, on the two; and the two sides were not mirror images to begin with. `Setae.thin(hairs, every)` (it was `keep` inside `SpiderRig.new`) counts each side of a mirrored kind (tuft, ruff, palp fluff) by itself; the left tuft's, ruff's and palp's hairs are the right's with every angle turned the other way and the same length and tone (`plume`, `Setae.palp`); and legs 5 to 8 are the mirror images of legs 1 to 4 (the same hairs, from the pair's own index, with every angle round the leg negated, because the rod frames of the two sides face opposite ways). Low-tier counts: first 267 to 265 parts (hairs 136 to 134), rival 266 to 262 (145 to 141). `look` pins mirror images hair for hair (including drawn, on the floor, mirrored across the body to 1e-5 studs) and the same number of tuft, ruff, palp and twin-leg hairs on both sides at both tiers.
10. Legs I and II start at the face, where the anterior lateral eyes are, and a walking leg I swung hairs through the eye (the smallest clearance of any leg hair from an anterior lateral eye, over standing, walking, an alarm and a groom: first spider -0.035 studs, rival -0.049 on HEAD). `look.fuzz.hipBare = { 0.6, 0.6, 0, 0 }`, for the first spider and the rival: the first 60% of the femur of legs I and II has no hair, and the femur's hairs are spread over the rest (`Setae.leg`). Clearance now +0.014 and +0.011; `look` keeps it at 0.004 or more for both looks and both tiers standing, walking, alarmed, grooming and reaching to meet (the legs of the cue are the ones nearest the face).
11. The meet pose's legs I: `kneeOut` 0.08 to 0.12 (the first spider and the rival; 0.04 to 0.056 for the peacock spider at its size, 0.47 of the first's). Measured on the rig through the cue: the knee's clearance from the principal eye, -0.043 to -0.007 studs (a cautious measure: the leg's axis to the eye's middle, less the rod and the eye's smallest radius), the tibia's 0.010 to 0.037; the knee from the nearest of the other eyes 0.038 to 0.049; nothing near the palps, jaws or fangs at either value (the least, 0.027, is the femur at the hip, unchanged). 0.14 would take the knee clear of the principal eye (+0.011); 0.12 is the audit's number and was kept. Rendered with `--cue meet`, before and after.
12. Every eye of every look sits well inside the surface it is placed on. `Anatomy.Eye` has `kind` and `margin` (1 minus the squares of its share of the radii across and along: 0 at the rim, where the place is clamped, 1 in the middle); the tightest of the 48 eyes of the six looks is the peacock spider's anterior lateral eye at 0.152 (the first spider's and the rival's, 0.177). `look` keeps every margin at 0.02 or more and shows that an eye moved to the rim fails.
13. A rival or a mate far from the camera was drawn sixty times a second. `SpiderRig.drawStep(collider, dt, grounded, moving, distance)` draws at once the first time and whenever `distance` (studs from the camera) is within `FAR_STUDS` (25), and otherwise adds to the time owed and draws every `FAR_SECONDS` (0.1) with all of it (a millionth of a second of slack, so six frames of 1/60 count): ten drawings a second at 60 Hz. The simulation still steps both every tick; only the drawing is throttled, and the time is not lost (`update` is given what has passed). The client gives distance 0 (full rate) to a rival that is winding up, lunging or taken, and `MateStage.drawMate` to a mate in the mating scene (it has a `focus`), coming to court (`attend`), paired, or with no camera to measure from. `client_rival`: near it every frame is drawn, beyond 25 studs 19 to 21 of 120 frames while the sim moves it nearly every tick, a windup and a lunge every frame; `client_mating` and `look` the same for the mate and for the rig itself.

The improvements.

14. The greenbottle blue's legs I are a tenth heavier again: `frontLegScale` 1.1 to 1.1 x 1.1 (the tarantula factory takes it as an option; the redknee's stays 1.1). Drawn, the first femur is 0.1105 to 0.1216 studs across (1.32 to 1.45 times the second's), the first tibia 0.0907 to 0.0997, still slimmer than the redknee's 0.1297; `look` pins the factor, the ratio and that both tarantulas stay within 150 parts (148 and 146). Rendered before and after.
15. `mate.scale` is read once, in `MateStage.drawMate`, and clamped to 0.5 to 2 there (what `Mate.valid` allows); a missing or not-a-number factor is 1. `client_mating` checks 3, 0.1, +inf, -inf, NaN, nil and a string.
16. The translucent grey disc behind the peacock fan in `sheet_dance_6.2.png` is not a transparency fault: no fan part has any. It is the fan's back repeat (`fan.back`: the cream fringe, the red-orange arch and the blue field, three discs under the abdomen's belly so that the raised fan is a fan from behind). With the abdomen pitched up in the dance they stand behind the front fan, the cream fringe disc round its rim and, in a three-quarter view, as a flat disc behind it; the previewer's lighting gives that face a grey cast. Taking the back off (`detail.full.fanBack = false`) takes the disc away; nothing was changed. If it is unwelcome from the front, the cheap changes are to leave the fringe out of the back (`back.layers = { 2, 3 }`) or to draw the back a tenth smaller.

The cost of a frame, before to after (the shim's counts; a frame is 1/60 s; "hang" is swaying on the line; Reduced Motion is the same for paused and for hanging held):

| look, tier | parts | standing | walking | hang | Reduced Motion | Size writes standing, walking |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| first spider, full | 695 to 693 | 695 to 646.9 (93%) | 695 to 693, 2 calls | 695 to 657.6 (95%) | 695 to 201 (29%) | 31.8 to 8.3, 73.0 to 69.3 |
| first spider, low | 267 to 265 | 267 to 159.6 (60%) | 267 to 265, 2 calls | 267 to 246.9 (93%) | 267 to 131 (49%) | 26.1 to 2.4, 54.6 to 50.8 |
| rival, low | 266 to 262 | 266 to 150.9 (58%) | 266 to 262, 2 calls | 266 to 245.1 (94%) | 266 to 121 (46%) | 26.0 to 2.3, 47.4 to 43.6 |
| mate, low, living | 267 to 265 | 267 to 253.7 (96%) | 267 to 265, 2 calls | 267 to 254.9 (96%) | 267 to 131 (49%) | 28.4 to 4.8, 56.5 to 52.8 |
| peacock, full | 119 | 119 (no hairs) | 119 | 119 | 119 | 57.9 to 1.9, 78.9 to 36.1 |
| ant mimic, full | 79 | 79 | 79 | 79 | 79 | 1.6 to 1.6, 22.5 to 22.5 |
| redknee, full | 146 | 146 to 129.0 (88%) | 146, 2 calls | 146 to 141.0 (97%) | 146 to 110 (75%) | 12.2 to 4.4, 40.0 to 39.0 |
| redknee, low | 84 | 84 | 84 | 84 | 84 | 9.8 to 1.9, 30.7 to 29.7 |
| greenbottle, full | 148 | 148 to 126.6 (86%) | 148, 2 calls | 148 to 142.5 (96%) | 148 to 112 (76%) | 4.1 to 4.1, 32.0 to 32.0 |

Calls a frame: one when no hair moved and two when any did (standing 1.22 to 2.0, walking always 2). The rival and the mate beyond 25 studs: a sixth of these figures (ten drawings in sixty frames). The mate's row counts the rig alone (low, living, as the client makes it); `MateStage.drawMate` added the 697 Size writes a frame of item 1 on top of its before.

Parts per look and tier after (hairs, shells, other), with HEAD's counts where they moved:

| look | full | low |
|---|---|---|
| first spider | 693 (492 hairs, 8 shells, 193 other; was 695, 494) | 265 (134, 2, 129; was 267, 136) |
| rival | 622 (473, 8, 141; was 624, 475) | 262 (141, 2, 119; was 266, 145) |
| peacock spider | 119 | 80 |
| ant mimic | 79 | 69 |
| redknee | 146 (36, 2, 108) | 84 (0, 2, 82) |

Pins that moved on purpose. `tests/look.luau` (1009 to 1417): the tuft pin is now one hair of the seven a side left out, the same on both sides (it was 14); the tier's kept-hair count is per side for the mirrored kinds; and `auditThreeChecks` (the eyes, the mirror images, the anterior lateral eyes, what a frame writes, the tolerance, the throttle, the greenbottle's legs) is new. `tests/client_rival.luau` (7 to 8): the throttle. `tests/client_mating.luau` (27 to 28): the mate's drawing, and the heads' overlap asserted inside the stage check and the hatching. `tests/shim.luau`: `Size` is kept and every write counted; `BulkMoveTo` counts its calls and parts. Unchanged: idle 5719, peacock 265, antmimic 88, tarantula 86, scenarios 17, client_ant 3, client_smoke 24.

Not seen in Studio: the two `BulkMoveTo` calls a frame and the 10 Hz drawing of a far rival or mate (the CLI counts calls and parts, not what the engine spends; whether a far rival's legs look steppy at ten a second, and the frame time, are Chet's checklist); the hairs placed up to 1e-4 studs or 1e-3 radians late (not visible in the previewer; look at a close face and a walking leg under Studio's shadows); the bare hips of legs I and II (60% of the femur) and the one tuft hair a side left out; the meet pose's wider knees, which still just touch the principal eye by the cautious measure; the greenbottle's heavier first legs; the spiderlings' heads, now 0.011 studs into their bodies (the picture of a head floating in front is gone in the stage's numbers, not in a render of the scene); the fan's back repeat from the front. The previewer shades and shadows as Godot does, not Roblox, so every render here is evidence of shape and position, not of how it looks in the game.

### ROBLOX-94 - The world core: the sill as region 0 of one continuous garden (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

Nathan: "I want to begin developing a huge open world sandbox for this game." `docs/OPEN_WORLD_DESIGN.md` is the architecture; this is its first round, W1, the world core, and its rule is that the game plays identically afterwards: the sill becomes region 0 of a world map, the server builds every region under one folder, the world streams around the player, the save records where in the world the rest was kept, and the day's pace becomes a dial per region, while nothing a player can see or do has changed. Godot's defaults are untouched (`Species`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates), every golden fixture holds, every save from before restores, and `WindowsillLayout` is still the sill's data, byte for byte.

The modules.

1. `Shared/World/WorldMap`: pure data and pure questions about a point. A region is `id`, `kind`, `origin`, `bounds` ({ min, max }), `seed`, `neighbors` (the seams, none yet), `retreats` (box volumes with ids), `spawn` (support point and heading) and `clockScale`. One region so far, `sill`, kind `sill`, at the origin, with the layout's own play bounds, the crevice (`sill_crevice`, the layout's retreat box), the layout's spawn and heading, and `clockScale` 1.0. `WorldMap.SEED` = 914 (the seed the prey, the wasp and the rival already draw from), `WorldMap.REVISION` = "world-1" (changes whenever a region's geometry moves in a way a saved position could no longer be trusted; adding a region somewhere new does not need it), `WorldMap.HOME` = "sill". The questions: `regions(worldSeed?)`, `region(id, worldSeed?)`, `at(pos)` (the first region listed whose bounds hold the point, edges included), `inBounds(pos, regionId?)`, `bounds()` (the union box, the motor's fall-off box), `inRetreat(pos, regionId?)` (the retreat's id or nil), `retreat(id)`, `unionOf(regions)`. `WorldMap.seed(worldSeed, id)` mixes the world's seed with the region's id into 1 .. 2^31 - 2 (every product stays below 2^53, so it is exact on every machine; the sill's is 1883132339, pinned); nothing in a region but its seed depends on the world's seed, and nothing here draws from `math.random`. The regions list and every region are frozen.
2. `Shared/World/Region`: the generators, pure and deterministic from the region. `pieces(region)` and `decor(region)` return `WindowsillLayout.PIECES` and `DECOR` themselves for the sill (the tables, not copies: `tests/worldmap.luau` pins byte for byte); `keepOut(region)` the boxes the rival's route keeps out of (the sill's: `RivalRules.keepOutMin` to `keepOutMax`, one per retreat); `folderName(region)` ("Windowsill" for the sill, the region's id for any other); `budget(kind, tier)` the most drawn parts a kind may carry (the sill: 1156 full, 292 low, what its decor builds now, held exactly by `tests/world.luau`). A kind with no generator is an error, not an empty region. `Region.WORLD_FOLDER` = "World".
3. `Shared/World/WorldBuild`: `world(parent, worldSeed?, replaced?)` builds a `World` folder with a folder for every region and parents it once it is whole; `region(region, parent, replaced?)` builds one. The sill is built by `Scenery.sill(replaced, pieces)`, so its folder is exactly the one `Server/Main` used to parent to the workspace: the same name, the same 28 parts, the same `ContentRevision` attribute, the same hidden-but-solid pieces where the decor drew its own; only its parent changed. `Scenery.parts(parent, pieces, hide)` is the part-making split out of `Scenery.sill` so that any later kind gets the same anchored, queryable parts with `SurfaceId` and `ProfileId` from its own generator. `tests/world.luau` dumps every part of the sill built by `WorldBuild` and by `Scenery.sill` (class, name, size, position, anchoring, collision, query, touch, transparency, shadow, material, colour, reflectance, attributes) and holds the two equal, with the decor's own replaced list, with none, and under another world seed; `tests/world_client.luau` casts 400 seeded rays against the sill in the workspace and against it inside `World` and holds every hit, position and normal equal.
4. `Server/WorldFocus`: with instance streaming on the server sends each client what is near that client's replication focus, and a player here has no character (`CharacterAutoLoads` is off; the spider is the client's own rig). `park(player, parent, at)` makes the player a small invisible anchored part (`CanCollide`, `CanQuery`, `CanTouch` and `CastShadow` off, transparency 1, 0.2 studs, named `ReplicationFocus_<UserId>` under `Workspace.ReplicationFoci`) and sets `player.ReplicationFocus` to it; `move`, `release`, and `watch(players, parent, at)` (every player already in the game, then `PlayerAdded`, and the part destroyed on `PlayerRemoving`). The focus is parked at the home spawn, 0.31 above it, for as long as the world is the sill: the round that lets the spider leave moves it with the spider. The part is not under `World` and cannot be queried, so no cast of the sim (an Include filter on `World`) and no cast at the whole workspace meets it; `tests/world.luau` casts down through it onto the sill.
5. `Client/WorldClient`: the client's side, one instance kept in `SpiderClient`'s module table `M.World` (the client is at Luau's limit of 200 locals: 180 top-level locals now, 181 before, since the layout's local went; it compiles at -O0, -O1 and -O2). `new(ws, player, map?)` finds the boxes: a `Windowsill` straight in the workspace (the box worlds of the tests, the old places) is both the root of every ray and the sill; otherwise `World` is the root and `World.Windowsill` the sill. With `Workspace.StreamingEnabled` true it waits for every box of the sill (`PIECES_WAIT`, 30 s in all, each one warned about by name if it never comes) and for the `Decor` (`DECOR_WAIT`, 10 s) before the sim begins, and without streaming nothing is waited for. It offers `root`, `sill`, `decor`, `home`, `region`, `boundsMin`, `boundsMax`, `spawnPos`, `spawnHeading`; `inRetreat(pos)` (any region); `regionAt(pos)`; `isActive(regionId)` (whether what belongs to a region is in play: the creatures are regional now, nothing is put to sleep yet because there is nowhere else to go); `focus(part)`, `refocus()` and `update(pos, root)`, called each frame before the sim's ticks: the region the spider is in (kept when it is momentarily in none), `LifeClock.game.clockScale` set from it, and the player's own `ReplicationFocus` pointed at the drawn spider's root when it is not yet (again after a respawn, a return to a rest, a new life and a new rig). That client-side write is belt and braces, not the mechanism: the server's parked part is what streaming follows, a client's write of the property is not expected to reach the server, and a write that raises is warned about once and the game goes on (`tests/world_client.luau` refuses it and counts one warning).

What the client wires through it. The motor's `world`, `spawn`, `heading`, `boundsMin` and `boundsMax`; the raycast params (`Include`, `{ M.World.root }`), so the motor, the senses, the flies' perches, the rig's feet, the wasp's casts and the rival's and the mate's motors all see `World` and only `World`; `Breakaway` and the perception stage take the sill's folder; the wasp's `inRetreat` callback, the rival's `safe`, the life loop's `inRetreat` and the mate's region all ask the map; `backToStart`, `returnToCheckpoint`, `fresh` and `carryOn` refocus. The predators take a region: `RivalSpider.valid(r, region?)`, `RivalSpider.target(r, region?)`, `Mate.valid(m, region?)`, `RivalDrive.new(motor, blocked, region?)` and `MateDrive.new(motor, blocked, region?)` default to the sill, so a record validated or a drive made without one is exactly what it was; `RivalRoute` builds its graph from `Region.pieces(sill)` and its keep-out from `Region.keepOut(sill)` (the same tables, so `rivalnav` is unchanged at 293); `MateStage` takes the crack's floor from `WorldMap.retreat("sill_crevice")`. `SpiderMotor.respawn(spawn?, heading?)` takes a support point and heading (the config's own when none is given).

Streaming, and the numbers. `Workspace.StreamingEnabled` true, `StreamingMinRadius` 96, `StreamingTargetRadius` 160, `StreamingIntegrityMode` Disabled, authored in `default.project.json` (runtime scripts cannot set them). The reasoning: everything the client's sim can raycast at must be present before it is asked for, so the whole active world must lie inside the radius the server always sends, measured from the focus; the sill's farthest corner is 68.6 studs from the parked focus and its farthest box corner 57.8 (`tests/worldmap.luau` prints both and fails if a corner passes 96; a region farther out, when one comes, moves the focus with the spider first). 160 is a region further for the camera; the sill has nothing beyond it. Integrity mode is Disabled because its job is to pause a character outside the streamed region, and there is no character. The drawn sill became a Model with `ModelStreamingMode` Persistent: a Folder's parts would stream in and out one by one around the focus, and the view out of the window is hundreds of studs away (the hills at 320 beyond the glass, a 2048-stud ground plane), past both radii, so with a Folder the view would never arrive; as a persistent model the whole drawn sill is sent as one unit and never streamed out, and the client's one-time reads of it (`Breakaway`'s Pilea, `WorldDetail`, `DayLight`, the eye view's UV layer) meet it whole. `tests.project.json` carries the same four properties: the tests place is the evidence for the live place, and the one thing this round cannot prove in the CLI is streaming; the engine checks run on the server, which has every instance whatever streaming does, and the live-client checks run where streaming acts, which is the point of running them. Nothing else assumed the Decor was a Folder (every consumer takes an `Instance`); `GraphicsSpec` finds the sill at `World.Windowsill` and the Decor as a Model.

The clock. `LifeClock.game.clockScale` (1.0) multiplies every advance of game hours, awake, asleep and moulting alike, and nothing else; `LifeClock.currentScale()` treats anything that is not a positive finite number as 1.0, so the dial can never stop or poison the clock. It is a dial on the module and not a field of a clock because it is never saved (hours are state, the rate is not) and a return to a rest builds a new clock that must run at the pace of where the spider now is. At 1.0 the hours are Godot's to the last bit (`tests/life.luau`: a minute awake is exactly 60 times the rate; every life golden runs at 1.0 and none moved); at 0.25 a night's sleep reaches the same morning after the same hours in four times the ticks, and an hour of play drains a quarter of the sill's hunger. The sill is 1.0; no region but the sill exists yet (the garden's own pace is W4's, 0.25 in the architecture or 0.33 in Nathan's rules).

The save. A rest records `world_map = { seed, revision, region, retreat, diffs }` beside the object graph (`world` is still the graph, so the name is `world_map`); the world is never saved, only the seed and revision it was made from and what changed in it, and `diffs` is an empty list until something in the world can change (a record with any entry in it is refused: "changes this game does not know"). `player.retreat_id` is now whatever retreat of the map the spider is in (the crevice, or ""), written from the same lookup as `world_map.retreat`; a record where the two disagree is refused, as is a region that is not on the map, a retreat that is not in that region, a seed that is not a whole number in 0 .. 2^32 - 1, a revision that is not a name of 1 to 64 characters, or a `world_map` that is not a record. A record without one (every rest before this round) means the sill of seed 914 with no changes, and restores exactly as it did: `tests/checkpoint.luau` strips the field from a fresh record and restores it at home in the crack, and a record away from the crack to its spot; the old bounds refusal ("the spider is outside the windowsill") and the unknown-retreat refusal still read as they did. The revision gate: a rest kept under another `WorldMap.REVISION` or another seed (`Checkpoint.worldMoved`) is a good record whose position is not tested and not used; it restores at its region's spawn by `SpiderMotor.respawn(spawn.pos, spawn.heading)`, with the life, the reserves, the flies and the clock as the record has them, and the notice "Returned to your last rest, but the world has changed since you rested, so you stand where a life begins in it. Everything else is as you left it." (a rest that was going on ends at the next tick, since the spawn is outside the crack). `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 3 and `CONTENT_REVISION` stays "windowsill-roblox-1": an optional field that an old record lacks needs neither, and the sill's geometry has not moved. No restore rerolls a seeded draw: the graph is put back as it was, as before. The server's rest check (`SaveRest.check`, which runs `Checkpoint.check` on a stand-in world) refuses and accepts the same records (`tests/state.luau`).

What stays byte-identical, and what the tests pin. `WindowsillLayout` is untouched and `Region.pieces(sill)` is its `PIECES` table itself; the sill's folder, part for part; the rays through it; `Layout.inRetreat` against `WorldMap.inRetreat` over 10,000 seeded points and all eight corners of the crack; `Layout`'s bounds against `WorldMap.at` and `inBounds` over 10,000 points; the keep-out box against `RivalRules`; the decor's budgets (1156 and 292) against `Region.budget`. Goldens unchanged: `life_reference` (20,902 of life's checks are as they were; the 20 new are the dial), `rival` 2830, `rivalnav` 293, `mating` 715, `client_rival` 8, `client_mating` 28, `wasp` 16884, `growth` 477, `continuing` 87.

Pins that moved on purpose.
