New suites: `tests/worldmap.luau` (87: the one region and its numbers, the frozen map, another world seed, the seed's stability, spread and range over 656 (world, region) pairs, `at`/`inBounds`/`bounds`, `unionOf`, `inRetreat`, the sill's generator byte for byte, the keep-out, the budgets, every box inside its region and every corner inside the streaming radius) and `tests/world_client.luau` (17: the flat sill and the server's `World` as roots, the equal rays, the region and the dial with an invented garden region at 0.25, the focus and its refusal, the waits under streaming and none without, then the real client on the folders the server builds: it stands, walks, raycasts `World` only, its flies perch, the focus follows the drawn spider through Backspace and a new life, the dial through the real loop, a rest from another revision put back at the spawn with the notice, the rival's legs bound to the sill and out of the crack). `tests/checkpoint.luau` 251 to 375 (the world record, a record from before, the refusals, the revision gate, `respawn`). `tests/life.luau` 20902 to 20922 (the dial). `tests/world.luau` 275 to 294 (the budgets, the persistent Decor, the `World` folder against `Scenery.sill`, `Scenery.parts` for an invented region, `WorldFocus`). `tests/state.luau` 484 to 491 (the server's rest check against the world record). `tests/run.py` bundles `WorldMap`, `Region`, `WorldBuild`, `WorldFocus` and `WorldClient` (`RivalRules` moved before `Region`, which reads it). All 37 suites: 61,070 checks, nothing failed (60,796 in 35 before). Strict analyze clean; 142 of 142 modules compile.

Not run in Studio: streaming on a real client and on the PlayStation (whether the 28 boxes and the persistent Decor arrive before the client's waits run out, and how long a 1,156-part persistent model takes to arrive at join); Rojo applying the Workspace `$properties` on a sync into the live place (the handoff's first checklist item is `print(workspace.StreamingEnabled)` in the command bar; the built places carry them); the Decor model's persistence (that nothing of the view out of the window streams out); the parked focus part (that the server streams around it with no character, and that `Player.ReplicationFocus` set on the server to an anchored part under a Folder is honoured as the docs say); the client-side focus write (expected to be ignored, guarded if it raises). The places in the repository are the audit round's and need `tools/build.py` (no Rojo in the VM). `docs/HANDOFF-roblox-world.md` is Chet's checklist; W2 to W5 are the next rounds of `docs/OPEN_WORLD_DESIGN.md`.

### ROBLOX-99 - The hang goes slower: letting down on a line at half Godot's reel speed (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

Nathan, playing v30 on the PlayStation: "make the hang just go slower by default and the mechanic would work perfect". Godot's silk reels at `reel_speed_wu_s` = 1.5 studs a second either way (`Species.phidippus_audax_prototype.silk`), and the let-down on the line (the stick back, or the drop key held) ran at that: a 10 cm drop in under seven seconds, too quick to place a stop or to watch the hang of ROBLOX-86.

The rule is the game's, not Godot's: `SilkController.game = { lowerScale = 0.5 }`, applied by `Silk:useGameRules()` (`lowerSpeed` = `speed` x 0.5 = 0.75 studs a second), which `Motor:useGameRules()` hands on and the client calls once at startup beside `needs:useGameRules()`. `Silk.step` reels with `lowerSpeed` when the intent is below zero and with `speed` when it is above: reeling in keeps Godot's 1.5, so the climb back is as brisk as it was and the rig's head-up climb (`look.hang.climb.speed`, 0.35 on, 0.14 off) trips as before. The initial slack of a drop (`TetherMath.DROP_SLACK`), the maximum line, the release hold and everything else on the line are untouched. A controller never given the rule replays Godot: the tether goldens (`tests/run.luau`), the recovery scenarios and Chet's silk spec run on Godot's numbers and are unchanged. The rival's and the mate's motors are never given it (they do not let themselves down).

Pins that moved on purpose. `tests/scenarios.luau` 17 to 18: the leaf drop of the silk spec, a hang that settles, a second of letting down (1.5 studs for Godot, 0.75 for the game), half a second of reeling in (0.75 either way), and a motor's line taking the rule only when given it. `tests/client_smoke.luau` 24 to 25: the real client's motor lets down at 0.75 and reels at 1.5.

Not run in Studio: how 0.75 studs a second feels on the pad (the dial is one number; Nathan's "slower by default" may want 0.4 or 0.6), and the drop key held against the stick (the drop key is the full let-down, the stick is proportional, as before).

### ROBLOX-100 - The music plays again: no gate on IsLoaded, and a load report in Output (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

Nathan on the PlayStation, v30: "also i cant hear the music". The music's voice (ROBLOX-76, wired to its asset in v28) is a 64 s loop in a Sound under the `WindowsillAudio` folder, played by `Soundscape.update` through the same `playing()` helper as the room. The audit (ROBLOX-92, v30) had put a gate in that helper: a Sound whose `IsLoaded` is false was not asked to play, on the reasoning that asking every frame restarts the request. The reasoning was wrong and the gate was harmful. `Play` and `Resume` set `Playing` at once, loaded or not, so the helper's `if not s.Playing` already asks only once and the engine plays the sound when it has loaded; and the engine is known to leave `IsLoaded` false for audio that has loaded and plays (devforum thread 2654049, a bug report of January 2024 still open as far as we know, affecting random clients for days at a time). On such a client the gate kept the music, the room and the hum from ever being asked. The v28 and v29 helper had no gate; v30 had it; v30 is the silent one. Nothing else about the music changed between them (the mix 0.18, the duck, the swell, the slider's default 1.0, the asset's privacy and its grant to the experience were all checked again, and the asset is still on the dashboard).

So: the gate is gone and the helper is v29's again (`Resume`, then `Play` if that did not set `Playing`), for every voice. And, for whoever can see Output (Studio, the PC's F9 console; nothing on screen), two reports: at construction, `ContentProvider:PreloadAsync` on the music with a callback that warns if the `AssetFetchStatus` is not `Success` ("did not load (<status>); check the asset's moderation and that it is granted to this experience"); and in `update`, once, if the music has been asked to play for `Soundscape.MUSIC_LOAD_WARN_S` = 20 s of audible time and `IsLoaded` is still false ("it plays when it loads, if it loads"). Both are wrapped so that a stub without the service or the method (the CLI shim) is simply silent.

Pins that moved on purpose. `tests/client_smoke.luau`: the audit's "an unloaded hum is left alone" is now "an unloaded hum is asked to play, and plays when it loads", and a hum already asked is not asked again (the shim's `Sound.Play`/`Resume` count their asks in `plays`); the count stays 25 with ROBLOX-99's check. `tests/settings.luau` 1151 unchanged (the music's voice exists, the slider trims it).

Not run in Studio or on the PlayStation: whether this is the whole of the silence. If the asset itself does not load on the console the Output report will say so on the PC, and the next step is the asset (a re-upload, or the moderation queue), not the code. The mix level (0.18 under birds at 0.16) was not changed; if the music plays but is too quiet on a television, that is `MIX.music` and a word from Nathan.


### ROBLOX-95 - Beyond the glass: the raised sash, the outer sill, the brick wall and the bed under the window (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

W2 of `docs/OPEN_WORLD_DESIGN.md`. The window opens and the world outside it exists and can be walked: out through the raised sash onto the stone outer sill, over its front edge, down 90 cm of brick to the bed under the window, and back. Nothing lives out there yet (W3 brings the garden's prey and bugs); the rival and the mate stay on the sill. Everything on the sill plays as it did except where the opening is. Godot's defaults are untouched (`Species`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, perception) and every golden holds.

The raised sash. `WindowsillLayout`'s `back_frame` (x -31..31, y 0..14, z -13.5..-12.5) is now four boxes round an opening x -8..8, y 0.7..6: `back_frame_west` (x -31..-8), `back_frame_east` (x 8..31), `back_frame_head` (x -8..8, y 6..14) and `back_frame_rail` (x -8..8, y 0..0.7, the sill rail the spider steps over), the same profile and colour, in the old box's place in the list; every other piece is byte for byte what it was. `WindowsillLayout.DECOR` is unchanged: its `window_glass` and mullions are never drawn (they are in `Graphics.window.replaces`, Scenery draws the window itself), so they stay the layout's record and the drawn window is `Scenery.window`'s. `Graphics.window.hidden` is the four ids and gains `opening` (the layout's gap), `sashRail` 0.8, `sashDepth` 0.45, `sashSetback` 0.12 and `jambWidth` 0.5. `Scenery.window` cuts the wall under the window at the opening (`ApronWest`, `ApronEast`), lines the cut with jambs, draws the sill rail as trim a hair under the rail's walkable top, the raised sash's bottom rail across the top of the opening set back in the frame, the side panes as they were (`GlassWest`, `GlassEast`) and the raised sash's pane above its rail (`Glass`); nothing is drawn in the opening. `Scenery.wallBox()` is the union the drawn wall stands in for. `Patina.glass` puts a condensation drop that would have hung in the gap on the raised pane instead, by the same draws. Through the opening the view is the stone and the garden beyond, and two things in the drawn view could not stay as they were now that the outside can be walked to: the window box (Data/Flora's garden box) stood across the whole window, sunk to y -3.2 in what is now the stone, right where the spider steps out, and its walls would have been walked through; it is two halves on the stone (x -24.5..-9.5 and 9.5..24.5, bottom y 0, top 2.8 as before), solid (World/Garden's `window_box_west` and `window_box_east`, hidden once the sill's Decor draws them), with the three middle flowers moved into them (same draws, same parts). The lawn of the view was a 2048-stud plate at y -46, a ceiling over the bed; it is at y -90.4 now, 0.4 under the bed's soil where the wall meets it, and the trunks and one house were lengthened to stand on it (`trunk_a`, `trunk_b`, `house_5`). The flowering shrubs were balls floating at sill height (invisible from the room, plain from the garden) and three hung over the bed: those three stand further out where they look the same through the glass (depths 36, 52, 32 to 80, 80, 82; the second narrowed from 8.5 to 6.5 so it fits between the bed and the houses), and every shrub stands on a woody stem to the lawn (a standard, a crown on a stem, `Flora.garden.shrub.body`). The sill's Decor is 1,178 parts at full and 314 at low (1,156 and 292 before: the wall's 8 new pieces, the window box's 6 and the shrubs' 8 stems).

The regions (`Shared/World/WorldMap`, in priority order; a point in two regions' bounds is the first one's):

| Region | Kind | Bounds (min..max) | Pieces | Look (full / low) | Clock | Retreat | Spawn | Seed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sill` | sill | (-33, -5, -16)..(33, 35, 16), the layout's | 31 | 1,178 / 314 (Decor) | 1.0 | `sill_crevice` | the layout's | 1883132339 |
| `outer_sill` | outer_sill | (-33, -3, -25.5)..(33, 35, -13.5) | 3 | 94 / 36 | 1.0 | none | (0, 0, -18) facing out | 1665906148 |
| `house_wall` | house_wall | (-42, -89, -30)..(42, 35, -13.5) | 3 | 93 / 73 | 1.0 | none | (35, 0, -18.75), the east shoulder | 781001660 |
| `bed_a` | bed | (-42, -93, -76)..(42, 35, -24) | 21 | 364 / 107 | 0.33 | `bed_a_stone` (8.6, -90, -52.6)..(13.4, -88.6, -47.4) | (11, -90, -43.5) facing the stone | 276332508 |

The union, `WorldMap.bounds()`, is (-42, -93, -76)..(42, 35, 16). The seams, each listed by both regions: the sill and the outer sill meet along the opening's rail line, (-8, 0, -13.5) to (8, 0, -13.5), where the rail's outer face meets the stone; the outer sill and the wall along (-30, -3, -24) to (30, -3, -24), where the stone's front face goes on down as brick (the slab is 3 cm thick, y -3..0, so the front edge at y 0 is the stone's own and the regions meet 3 cm below it, on one plane); the wall and the bed along the wall's foot, (-25, -90, -24) to (25, -90, -24). The sill keeps the layout's bounds, so its margin (to z -16) is the first 2.5 studs of the stone; the wall's region is its face and six studs of air in front of it down to y -89, so a spider standing on the soil at the foot is in the bed; the bed's is its soil and the air over it, past the soil's edges to x ±42, so a spider that jumps out from the wall falls through the bed's air. The bed runs at a third of the sill's pace (Nathan's 0.33; W4 tunes it), the outer sill and the wall at the sill's.

The generators (`Shared/World/Garden`, which `Region` asks for every kind but the sill). Pure and seeded from the region's seed, a Park-Miller stream of its own for each kind of thing (so a drawn thing the low tier drops never moves one it keeps), cached per region. The outer sill: the slab x -30..30, y -3..0, z -24..-13.5 (painted stone, `Concrete`) and the window box's halves; its look is the paint worn through to the stone in patches (worst along the front edge), rain streaks down the front face a hair proud of it, moss in six corners (the slab's four, the window box's inner ends) and grit. The wall: one box x -40..40, y -90.4..-3, z -24..-13.5 under the slab and two shoulders x ±(30..40), y -3..0 beside it, all three invisible once drawn (still solid): stretcher-bond courses of 21.5 x 6.5 bricks with 1 cm joints, a hair behind the walkable face, over mortar recessed 0.28, a coping on the shoulders, and the house going on above the outer sill round the window to y 30 (drawn only, a plaster skin on the room's side), with spalled faces, a little salt bloom and algae along the foot at the full tier. The bed: the soil x -25..25, y -92..-90, z -74..-24 (`Ground`); the stone over the retreat, a slab on two rests whose gap is the map's `bed_a_stone` (`Slate`); then, each placed where it overlaps nothing placed before it, off a 5-stud landing strip at the wall's foot and clear of the spawn: a dock (one stem 18 tall, three leaves growing from its sides at 6, 11 and 15, each leaf's underside on the stem so a spider climbing it walks out under the leaf and over its edge), a dandelion (a rosette of four leaves on the soil round a 12-stud flower stalk with a drawn head), two more stones and six soil mounds. Its look: the plants drawn the Pilea's way (a blade with rounded ends whose top is the box's top at both tiers, a darker rim and a lighter underside and a midrib at full), the stalk and stem as rods, lichen and moss on and round the stones, a heap round each mound so it reads as a heap (its walkable top is the box's), damp patches, sixteen grass tufts, seventy fallen leaves (a third kept at low), twigs and pebbles. No Terrain, no meshes, no uploads. A look is records (`Garden.Drawn`: size, centre, turn, shape, material, colour, `full`), one part each, built by `Scenery.look`; the budgets (`Garden.BUDGETS`, reported by `Region.budget`) are the counts above, the placer stops at them keeping what it places first (`Garden.decorWith` under half the budget places at most half), and `tests/world.luau` builds the looks and holds both tiers to them exactly. `Server/Main` builds the looks into `Workspace.WorldDecor`, a persistent Model per region as the sill's Decor is (so the low tier can thin each once, `Client/WorldDetail`, which now also waits for `WorldDecor`), best effort like the sill's (a look that fails is warned about, left out, and its pieces stay visible); the pieces a look draws instead turn invisible, still solid.

The revision and the saves. `WorldMap.REVISION` is `"world-2"`: the sill's back frame moved, so a rest kept under world-1 (v31) is put back at its region's spawn with the existing notice ("the world has changed since you rested"), and so is a record without `world_map` (v30 and before), which is now read as `WorldMap.FIRST_REVISION`, `"world-1"`: W1 restored those unchanged because nothing had moved, and now something has. Its position is then not tested, so an old record outside the sill is no longer refused for it (it is not used). `Checkpoint.CONTENT_REVISION` and `SCHEMA_VERSION` are unchanged: a new content revision would refuse old records instead of putting them back. A rest under the bed's stone goes through the existing rest flow (the life loop's `inRetreat` is any region's), records `world_map.region = "bed_a"`, `retreat = "bed_a_stone"` (and the player block's `retreat_id`), is checked against the bed's bounds and retreats, and comes back there; a bed rest from another revision comes back at the bed's spawn. The mate and the rival still arrive at the sill and the sac and eggs are the sill's. The server's rest check (`SaveRest.check`, `tests/state.luau`) accepts a bed rest and refuses one outside the bed, one naming the crack, and one under the stone that says the sill.

The world's floor. `SpiderMotor`'s config takes an optional `inBounds`; the client hands it `WorldClient.inBounds`, so the player is out of the world only where no region holds it. A fall from the sill's room side ends at y -5 as it always did; a fall through the opening or off the outer sill goes on down through the wall's and the bed's air and lands on the bed (a 1 cm spider takes no harm from 90 cm; nothing here hurts it); beside the garden or under the soil (y -93) it is out of the world and goes back to its last support. Without `inBounds` the motor is Godot's box. The rival's and the mate's motors keep the sill's bounds (`WorldClient.homeMin`, `homeMax`).

Streaming follows the spider. The bed is about 115 studs from the parked focus, past `StreamingMinRadius` (96). The server makes a `RemoteEvent` at startup, `ReplicatedStorage.WorldFocus` (`WorldFocus.listen`); the client fires it with the spider's place whenever the spider is more than 8 studs from its last report or 2 seconds have passed (`WorldClient.report`, from `update`); the server takes a report only if it is a finite `Vector3` inside `WorldMap.bounds()` (a table sent as a point is not one) and at least 0.09 s after that player's last one it took (about ten a second), and moves the player's focus part there (`WorldFocus.report`, `move`); anything else is ignored. The client keeps its own pcall-guarded `ReplicationFocus` write. The readiness guard: `WorldClient:regionReady(id)` is whether the region's folder holds every piece of `Region.pieces(region)`, and `update` warns once per region in Output (`[Windowsill] world: the spider is in bed_a, which has not arrived whole ...`) if the spider is in a region that is not whole, since a missing box is a fall; a region found whole is remembered. The numbers (`tests/worldmap.luau` prints and checks them, measuring a box by the farther of its nearest point and its centre, with the focus up to 8 studs behind the spider): the sill and the outer sill are whole from the parked focus before any report (farthest box corners 57.8 and 55.5); from anywhere in a region its farthest box is 62.5 (sill), 44.1 (outer sill), 59.1 (bed) and 112.7 (the wall, whose far shoulder is that far from the wall's foot at the other end: inside the target radius, 160, not the 96 always sent, so the wall being whole leans on the target radius; everything within 20 studs of the spider is always inside 96); within 10 studs of a seam, the boxes on the far side that meet the seam are at most 61.7 away (the rest of that region inside the target radius); over the bed, the soil under a spider anywhere on the wall's face is at most 81.9 down (89.9 from the lagging focus), a drop from the stone's front edge lands on soil 90.7 from its focus 8 studs back over the stone, and the whole bed is inside the 96 everywhere below y -17.8 on the wall, a fifth of the way down: by the time a descent on the line (0.75 studs a second, two minutes for the 90 cm, ROBLOX-99) is a quarter done, the bed is there whole. How the engine streams a box much larger than its grid cells (the 80 x 87 wall, the 50 x 50 soil) is not something the CLI can see.

Navigation per region. `RivalRoute.graph(id)` is a region's graph, built when first asked from that region's pieces (faces, stand points and seams exactly as the sill's always were) with its neighbours' boxes as obstacles and its keep-outs (`Region.keepOut`): the bed's stone gap grown by half a stud; for the sill the crack (`RivalRules`' box, first, as `aroundKeepOut` uses it) and now also the open sash (`Region.SASH_MIN`..`SASH_MAX`, the opening grown by 0.3 and none of the floor in front of it), so no place to patrol to, no crossing and no corner the rival or the mate uses is in the opening: they are bound to the sill. Seam edges (`graph:seamsOf(face)`, `graph:allSeams()`) join faces of two neighbouring graphs that meet along a declared seam's line, across a corner or flat across one plane, with the places a body can cross (tested against the real boxes of both regions, not pruned by keep-outs: they are the map's topology); the sill to the outer sill across the corner where the rail's outer face meets the stone (and a second corner under the frame, where the stone's back face meets the rail's underside in the slot that has always been there under the back frame), the outer sill to the wall flat across z -24, the wall to the bed across the foot. Nothing plans across a seam this round. The module's functions (`faces`, `faceAt`, `adjacent`, `plan`, `aroundKeepOut`, `inKeepOut`, `waypoints`, `walkBlocked`) are the sill's graph's, so the rival and the mate call what they always called; the sill's graph has 186 faces and 110 places to patrol to (168 and 101 before: the back frame's three new boxes). The bed's graph (126 faces, 81 places) is built and planned over in the tests and nothing walks it yet; the outer sill's has 18 places, the wall's 23. Prey perches stay the sill's authored list.

Camera, HUD, words: nothing added. `LineFraming`, the boom cast, the eye view, Inspect and the dragline are unchanged and raycast `World`, which now holds every region.

Pins that moved on purpose. `tests/worldmap.luau` 87 to 191: the four regions and their numbers, the seams both ways, `at`'s priority where bounds meet, the union, the bed's retreat, the garden's generators (deterministic, inside their regions, no box outside the window overlapping another, the stone round its gap, the dock's leaves on its stem, nothing in front of the opening), the keep-outs, the budgets, and the streaming numbers above (the W1 check that every corner of the map is inside 96 of the parked focus could not hold once the bed exists and is replaced by them). `tests/world.luau` 294 to 328: the layout is 31 boxes with checksum 25125.25 (28 and 25445.9 before), 15 hidden sill pieces (12), the decor reports the window box's halves too, a ray at y 3 goes out through the opening and one at 0.35 meets the rail, the drawn wall covers the back wall but the opening and the opening is clear, the panes, rails and jambs, the window box's halves on the stone, the drops never in the gap, the sill's Decor 1,178 / 314 (1,156 / 292), and a new block for the looks (persistent models, both tiers exactly at their budgets, nothing collidable), the regions' parts, a failed look, and the focus report (validation, the rate, the remote). `tests/rivalnav.luau` 293 to 355: the back frame's names, no place to patrol to in the sash, a graph per region, the bed's plans (up the dock onto its leaves, onto the stone and the dandelion's leaves), the seams both ways and on their lines, and the rival walking along the back wall's foot past the opening without entering it; the sill's behaviour checks are as they were. `tests/checkpoint.luau` 375 to 386: a record without `world_map` is now world-1's and comes back at the spawn with the notice (W1's "restores unchanged" checks are replaced), a rest under the bed's stone, the bed's refusals, a moved bed rest at the bed's spawn. `tests/state.luau` 491 to 495: the server's check of bed rests. `tests/world_client.luau` 17 to 23: rays that leave the sill may meet the garden first (every ray that ends on the sill ends where it did), `regionReady`, the once-per-region warning (and none without `warn`), the report's timing and its absence without the remote, the world's floor, and the real client walking from the sill floor out through the opening, over the stone, over its edge and down the brick to the soil (regions sill, outer_sill, house_wall, bed_a in that order; the dial 1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 0.33; reports every 8 studs of it), then a rest under the bed's stone kept as bed_a / bed_a_stone and returned to. `tests/shim.luau` gains `OnServerEvent` and a `FireServer` that keeps what is fired. `tests/scenarios.luau` (18, the recovery scenarios) passes on the changed sill unchanged. `tests/run.py` bundles `World/Garden` (and `Region` after `Flora`, which Garden reads). The Studio specs that named `back_frame` (`GraphicsSpec`, `RivalSpec`) name its pieces now, and `GraphicsSpec` checks that a low ray goes out through the opening. All 37 suites: 61,293 checks, nothing failed (61,072 at v31). Strict analyze clean; 143 of 143 modules compile (the client at -O0, -O1 and -O2).

The previews (`tools/preview/scene.py`, which gains `--world` to build the regions outside and three spiders out there, and the views `sash`, `outside`, `wall`, `descent`, `bed`, `stone` and `world`). Looked at: `window` (the sash rail across the middle pane, the opening below it with the stone and the garden through it), `sash` (from the floor in front of the opening: the rail, the stone with its moss and grit, the window box's halves either side, the shrubs beyond), `outside` (from the outer sill: the opening framed by its jambs, rails and the raised pane, the sill inside through it, the window box's east half with its flowers), `wall` and `descent` (the brick courses, a spider on a mortar joint part way down), `bed` and `stone` (the soil with its mounds, stones, litter and grass, the dock and the dandelion, the stone on its rests with the gap under it), `world` (the wall, the bed at its foot, the shrubs on their stems on the lawn), `overview`, `game` and `retreat` (the sill as it was). They caught the shrubs: as columns of foliage down to the lawn they walled the garden in, so they became standards on stems. They show shape and placement only, not Roblox's materials or light.

Not run in Studio: the opening and the view through it (whether the jambs, rails and panes read as a raised sash, whether the window box on the stone reads right from the room), walking out over the 0.7 rail and the 3 cm stone edge on a pad, the descent on the line down 90 cm of brick (the hang and the 0.75 let-down, two minutes) and how the camera's boom behaves against the wall, streaming while descending (whether the focus report reaches the server and the part moves, whether `[Windowsill] world:` warnings appear, how a 50 x 50 soil box and an 80 x 87 wall box stream), the focus report on a real server (`ReplicatedStorage.WorldFocus` made at startup, the rate limit against a real client), the persistent looks at join (about 550 more parts arriving with the Decor's 1,178), part budgets and frame time on a phone and the PlayStation, the fall from the sill to the bed and the reset beside the garden, a rest under the stone and a return to it, an old (v30 or v31) save put back at the spawn with the notice, and the PBR look of concrete, brick, ground and slate at 1 stud = 1 cm. Out of scope and not looked at: the wasp is handed the spider's position wherever the spider is, as it always was, and what it does while the spider is in the garden was not checked (the garden's dangers are W3 and W4); the flies' perches are the sill's.

### ROBLOX-96 - Garden life: prey and bugs in the bed, the stone as a home, the descent dressed, the ground-life page, saves per region (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

W3 of `docs/OPEN_WORLD_DESIGN.md`: "prey in the bed (gnats and aphids join the flies), woodlice as the first ambient bug, the stone retreat, the descent set piece, the page, saves across regions." The garden under the window gets its first life and the sill plays exactly as it does today: its twelve authored perches, its two vents, its house flies with every third a fruit fly, its seed 914, are untouched and pinned (PREY 1646 of 1656 and FLIES 229 of 271 are the old checks unchanged, the sill's checkpoint record is byte for byte what it was but for the timing float the fingerprints already strip, every golden holds). Godot's defaults are untouched: `Species.fly_like_prototype`, `Catch`'s base numbers, `Fly`, `FlyFlight`, `FlySenses`, `PreyPopulation` and `PerchGraph`'s logic, the perception modules, `TetherMath`, `SilkController`'s defaults; the new prey are derived profiles the way the fruit fly is, and the new rules are opt-in game values.

**Prey per region** (`Shared/Prey/PreyWorld`, kept in the client as `M.World.prey` so the client gains no local). A `PreyDirector` is one region's prey. Without a spec it is the sill's, exactly as it was; with a spec (`World/Garden.prey(region)`) its perches and vents are the spec's, a perch marked `walker` is a plant perch only a walker is spawned on (and needs no flight to be valid), a vent marked `walker` is where an aphid appears on a free plant perch within the vent's reach, out of the spider's sight by the population's own check (`PreyPopulation` unchanged: the same delays, the same twice-unseen rule), with no flight. The region's flies carry its name (`bed_a_fly_1`...), so no two flies of the world share an id. `D.after` is split into `strike`, the feeding step, the held fly and `tidy`, in the order it always ran; `afterShared` is the strike and the tidying alone, for a director whose jaws are another's. `PreyWorld` holds the sill's director as `home` and makes the bed's from the spec with the region's own seed (`WorldMap.seed(914, "bed_a")` = 276332508) the first time the bed is whole (`WorldClient:regionReady`) and the spider is in it or beside it; the bed's director shares the home's `Feeding` (one jaws, one reserve: the feeding step runs once a tick, in the home's `after`, whether or not the sill's prey is active) and the home's casts (`PreyDirector.casts`: one probe, one set of closures, so a checkpoint names them once). Which regions run: the spider's own, and each neighbour with prey whose farthest perch or vent is within `ACTIVE_RADIUS` = 96 studs (the streaming radius always sent) of the spider: on the sill and the outer sill the sill's; on the wall nothing until about a third of the way down, where the bed's farthest perch comes inside 96; in the bed the bed's. A director that is not active is not stepped: its clock stops, its flies stay where they are, nothing is rerolled by a seam; a fly that tears free is sent back to the director that owns it (`PreyWorld.escape`). The drawn flies, the sound's view and the notebook's watcher read the active regions' flies (`PreyWorld.flies()`, `view`); a region the spider has left is not drawn (its flies are 100 studs off behind the wall). The gate: no director is made and none steps while a box of its region may be missing, since every perch and every flight is a raycast; the readiness warning of ROBLOX-95 stays.

**The bed's perches and vents** (`Garden.prey`, from the bed's own boxes and seed, cached): eight seeded free spots on the soil at least three studs apart (the first two the gnats' starts), the top of each mound (6) and of each stone (3), under each of the dock's three leaves for the gnats (the first a start; shelter 0.6, food 0.5) and on each for the aphids (walker perches), the dock's stem's east side (a walker perch, a start) and the dandelion's four leaves (walker perches, the first a start): 28 perches, 8 of them walkers, six starts. Shelter and food as the sill's (soil 0.2/0.1, mound 0.15/0.1, stone 0.1/0.05, under a leaf 0.6/0.5, a leaf 0.3/0.5, the stem 0.35/0.5): the plants are where the food is, as the sill's rim is, which is the gradient the fly model moves on (with equal food everywhere a gnat never moved: the move score never cleared the rest score plus the hysteresis). Four vents: two gnats' vents on the soil, each within an entry flight (4 to 9 studs) of a soil perch, and the aphids' at the dock's foot (reach 8) and the dandelion's stalk (reach 6), listed gnat, aphid, gnat, aphid so neither kind of entry always comes first. The bed holds six prey (`target`): three gnats and three aphids to begin with. `tests/worldmap.luau` holds every perch inside the bed and on a face of a real box (a top, a leaf's underside or the stem's side), `tests/garden_life.luau` casts for each from the box world.

**The two profiles** (`Species`, derived by copy from `fly_like_prototype` like the fruit fly; game values, no measured behaviour claimed). The fungus gnat (`fungus_gnat_prototype`, `prey_kind` "fungus_gnat"): nutrition 0.35/3 (a third of a housefly meal), visual_scale 0.45, radius 0.12, capture radius 0.40; sight 9 and startle range 5 (the fly's 14 and 7); take-off 40, flee 7, cruise 3.5, landing 1.2 (80, 15, 7, 2), clearance 0.10, lift 0.5, approach 0.5, min hop 1.2, max flight 10 (26), safe distance 6, perch avoid 2.5, open escape 3; repositions up to its whole flight (10), restless twice as soon (0.06 a second) with a hysteresis of 0.1 and `move_restless` 1.0 (the three numbers that make a restless gnat on open soil flit to the next perch while one under a leaf stays), walks of 0.2 to 0.8 at 0.4 to 0.9, a wander radius of 1.5, wander flights at 0.3; vents 8 from the spider, entry flights of 4. The aphid (`aphid_prototype`, "aphid", `body.walker` = true): nutrition 0.35/6 (a sixth), visual_scale 0.4, radius 0.1, capture radius 0.38, feed rate 0.06; sight 4, startle 3; walks of 0.1 to 0.4 at 0.15 to 0.35 every 4 to 12 s, a wander radius of 0.8, fidgets of 0.04 to 0.1. The walker rule in `Fly`: `launch` refuses every flight of a walker (so `FlyFlight` is never begun for it: the choice is one leaf and a shuffle, not a perch-to-perch walk, because the fly model's rest already walks, fidgets, grooms and glances within the perch's wander radius, which is an aphid's day, while a walk along the plant's boxes would need a surface path planner the gnats and flies do not have and this round did not need) and, startled, a walker freezes instead of turning to face the threat; a torn-free walker is put back on its perch if the perch is free, alert, and is gone if not (`D.escape`). Godot's profiles have no `walker` field, so nothing of theirs is touched. `Catch.factorFor`, read from the meal as before: the gnat 0.6, the aphid 0.5 (the floor, `Catch.size.min`): a steady two taps a second holds either every time (and loses a house fly every time); untouched, a gnat lasts longer in the jaws than a fruit fly and an aphid longer still, under three seconds. `Feeding` needs no word of it: the meal is the nutrition, so a gnat credits a third of a fly's growth by the rule that already is.

**Drawn** (`Data/FlyLook.small`, `Client/FlyBodies.buildSmall`): a template per prey kind, built when first asked for, in the fly's body frame at the profile's visual scale. The gnat: thorax, abdomen, head, two eyes (ellipsoids), six two-part legs (12 rods, tucked in flight), two antennae, one pair of plain glass wings folded flat (a fan in flight, as the fly's without the vein and the film) and the shadow: 22 parts, dark grey. The aphid: a pear-shaped green abdomen, a head, two eyes, two antennae, two cornicles, six one-part legs and the shadow: 15 parts, no wings (a captured aphid is drawn still). Both under the fly's 40, all anchored, never solid, never seen by a ray, no shadow cast. A fly whose kind has no sheet (the house fly, the fruit fly) is drawn as before; a rig whose kind changes under the same id is rebuilt.

**Woodlice** (`Shared/Prey/Woodlouse`, `Client/BugBodies`): the first ambient bug, not prey. Four in the bed, from `Garden.bugs(bed_a)` (seeded free spots on the soil, a stud from any box and five from each other), each with its own walk speed (0.35 to 0.65 studs a second) and heading. Pure, seeded (a `PreyRandom` stream from the region's seed), no raycast: the soil is flat, the standing boxes (the mounds, the stones, the plants) are footprints it steers round (a step into one, or within a stud of the soil's edge, turns it 90 to 180 degrees), it turns a little every 1 to 3 s and pauses now and then for 1 to 4 s. Within `HIDE_WU` = 4 studs of the spider (and within 6 vertically) a louse makes for the nearest hide spot at `FLEE` = 1.2 studs a second and goes under (`hidden`); the spots are the mouth and the back of the gap under the big stone, the foot of each other stone and the first three of the fallen leaves the low tier keeps (so a hiding place is always drawn), seven in all. It stays under while the spider is within `OUT_WU` = 6 and comes out 8 to 15 s (seeded) after it has gone, turned away. Not prey: `PreyWorld` never sweeps them and nothing can hold one (`tests/garden_life.luau` drives a strike through one). To the senses they are movement and nothing finer (`percepts`: `maxCategory` "movement", as the wasp's, the rival's and the mate's, radius 0.35; a hidden louse is not reported): the perception modules are untouched. Why the spider leaves them alone: their tegumental glands give a secretion that repels spiders (Fischer et al. 2025, a modern test with *Porcellio scaber* and a comb-footed spider; the classic account is Gorvett 1956, which could not be fetched and is not on the page); `docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md` says so. Drawn as a grey segmented body a centimetre long: seven overlapping plates arched over the back, a head, two antennae and seven pairs of short legs, `BugBodies.PARTS` = 24 parts each (96 for the four), anchored and never solid, placed in one `BulkMoveTo`; a hidden louse is drawn a stud and a half under the soil, inside the solid ground.

**The stone as a home.** `LifeText.HOMES` names each retreat of the map: the crack ("the silk-lined crack", "The silk-lined crack", "Home: the silk-lined crack", the way in as it was, "Last rest kept.") and the stone ("under the stone", "Under the stone", "Home: under the stone", "Walk into the gap under the big stone in the bed, then stop to rest.", "Last rest kept, under the stone."). `LifeText.prompt` takes the retreat's id (nil: the crack, every word as it was): "Asleep under the stone", "Resting under the stone"; `LifeText.homeLine(retreat, inside, device)` is the line near home; `LifeText.restKept(retreat, message)` is the kept-rest notice (the store's own words for the crack). The client asks `WorldClient:homeRetreat(pos)`: the retreat the spider is in, else the first retreat of the region it is in, else the last one it was in or beside (the crack to begin with); the line near home shows within 7 studs of the retreat's `door`, a point `WorldMap`'s retreats now carry (the crack's (-24, 1, 6), the very point the client always measured from; the stone's (11, -89.5, -46.5), in front of the gap's mouth). Nothing else in the HUD changes (W5 decides the region's name). The meal line: `LifeText.growth` takes the kind of the prey in the jaws and says its rule, "3 gnats = 1 housefly" or "6 aphids = 1 housefly", counting in tenths (so a gnat's third shows), and "2 small prey = 1 housefly" in halves as always when nothing or a sill fly is held.

**The descent.** The wall's look gains the set piece's dressing within its budget, all seeded from the wall's seed: three cobweb strands in one corner under the outer sill (which corner the seed decides; the two long ones at both tiers, since the spider passes them on its way down; thin Glass rods at 0.45 transparency), eight lichen patches on the brick between y -72 and -16 (pale grey-green ellipses, full tier) and one weathered course between the sixth and the tenth below the slab whose faces have eroded (six darker chips, full tier). `Garden.BUDGETS.house_wall` is 110 full / 75 low (93/73; the three looks together 568/218, 551/216 before), the placer exactly at it, `tests/world.luau` holding both tiers exactly. Nothing moved: no box of any region changed, so `WorldMap.REVISION` stays `world-2` and every v32 rest comes back where it was (adding lichen to a look is not geometry a saved position depends on). The let-down itself needed one game rule: Godot's `max_line_length_wu` is 20 studs, never reached on the sill, and the 90 cm from the outer sill's front edge to the bed is 90 studs, so `SilkController.game.maxLine` = 100 (applied by `useGameRules` beside the lowering speed; a controller never given the rule keeps Godot's 20, which the tether goldens and the box-world scenarios run on). A game value: jumping spiders do descend on draglines far farther than their body length, but no measured length is claimed. `LineFraming`, the boom cast, the hang, the slack and the release are unchanged. `tests/world_client.luau` stands the real client's spider at the slab's front edge, presses F (the edge walk, the drop) and holds it: the line pays out at 0.75 studs a second (measured on the way, between 0.6 and 0.9), the spider lands on the soil after about two minutes, the line is released, the region is `bed_a` at its pace, the focus report is within 8 studs, no world warning on the way down, and the bed's prey runs.

**The page.** `tools/gen_notebook_pages.py` gains `ROBLOX_PAGES` (the peacock, the ant mimic, eggs and wind, the velvet cousins, and now "Life on the ground", id `ground_life`, trigger `garden_bed`), regenerates `NotebookPages.luau` (29 pages; `tests/notebook_reference.json` is still the byte copy of Godot's file and the Godot pages still match word for word), and the trigger list gains `garden_bed`. `NotebookWatch.step`'s view carries the kind of the region the spider is in; the first tick with a region of kind "bed" opens the page, once (the client passes `M.World.region.kind`). The text (497 characters): fungus gnats are small dark flies whose larvae live in damp soil and eat fungi and rotting matter, the adults fly weakly; aphids are soft, pear-shaped insects that feed in groups, sucking sap from leaves and stems; a jumping spider hunts small insects of many kinds, the related *Phidippus johnsoni* takes aphids; woodlice are crustaceans, not insects, with seven pairs of legs, that keep to damp places under stones and litter, and glands in their armour give off a secretion that puts spiders off. Five sources, each in the notebook's style: Cranshaw & Cloyd 2009 (Colorado State Extension 5.584), Warren & Schalau 2014 (Arizona Extension AZ1635), Jackson 1977 (*J. Arachnol.* 5: 145-149), Hopkin 1991 (the FSC key to the woodlice), Fischer et al. 2025 (*J. R. Soc. Interface* 22: 20250260). None could be opened from the build environment (the hosts refused); every sentence rests on a search snippet or an indexed abstract, as `docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md` records sentence by sentence, with what is not claimed (no species, size, lifespan, colour or wing; not that *P. johnsoni* eats gnats; not that a jumping spider in particular rejects a woodlouse: the game note calls the woodlice's immunity the game's way of saying the spider leaves them alone). The "In this game" line says the gnats and the aphids are the house fly's model made smaller and slower with no measured numbers, a third and a sixth of a meal, the aphids only walk, the woodlice hide and cannot be caught.

**Saves across regions** (`Persistence/Checkpoint`). The `prey` root is still the sill's director, byte for byte; the regions outside are an optional `garden` root, `PreyWorld`'s own table `{ [regionId] = { prey = a PreyDirector?, bugs = a Woodlice? } }` (`Woodlice` joins `CLASSES`; the gnat's and the aphid's profiles join the refs as `gnatProfile` and `aphidProfile`), recorded when the world has one, its directors' `cpu` windows left out like the sill's. The checks are the sill's for each director (unique ids within it, a known state, a finite place, a meal no bigger than the profile's own: the fruit fly's special case at 0.18 is gone, generalised to `profile.body.nutrition`, the same answer for every fly there was; perches held and reserved only by flies that exist), plus: every region named is on the map and has generated prey, every director eats with the sill's jaws (the same `Feeding`), every colony is a `Woodlice` whose lice are in known states inside the region's bounds and hide in spots it has. The held fly is any director's, found by identity. A record without the root (every rest before this round) restores unchanged and leaves the live garden exactly as it is (nothing rerolls); a garden record into a world with no garden is "the live world is missing a part"; a rest under the stone restores with the bed's gnats, aphids and woodlice where they were and runs on from there identically in two worlds (`tests/checkpoint.luau`). After a return to a rest the bed's director is a fresh object whose `feeding` is the record's shared one; `PreyWorld.director` re-points every garden director at the home's jaws each tick, so a bed director that outlived the sill's restore (an old record) eats with the new reserve. The server's rest check (`SaveRest`) accepts a garden record and refuses a garden naming a region the map lacks, the sill (no generated prey), a region that is not a record, or a bed whose prey is not a director (`tests/state.luau`). `CONTENT_REVISION` and `SCHEMA_VERSION` do not change: an optional root an old record lacks needs neither.

**Streaming and the client.** `WorldClient` keeps `prey`, `bugs` and `lastRetreat`, and answers `homeRetreat` and `nearRetreat`; `SpiderClient` wires the world's prey through it (percepts, `before`, `after`, the drawn flies and bugs, the sound's view, the watcher's view with the region's kind and the world's captures, the checkpoint world's `garden`), and the HUD's home line and meal line through `LifeText`; no new top-level local (the two modules are `M.PreyWorld` and `M.BugBodies`). The previewer (`tools/preview/scene.py --world`) now draws the bed's six prey on their start perches and the four woodlice where the seed puts them, with the views `bed_life` (the dock with an aphid on its leaf and a gnat under it) and `corner_web` (the corner under the outer sill).

Pins that moved on purpose. New suite `tests/garden_life.luau` (72,546: the profiles and their factors, the bed's perches and vents against the real boxes, the director's six prey and their determinism, three minutes of gnats flying short and aphids never flying, the startled aphid and the torn-free one, the vents' replacements by flight and by appearance, the woodlice's placement, wandering, hiding and return, their percepts and problems, the world's prey per region with its gate and its seam, the strike that takes a gnat and never a woodlouse and the torn-free gnat sent home, the drawn gnat, aphid and woodlouse under their caps; most of its count is a check every tick that nothing leaves the bed). `tests/prey.luau` 1646 to 1656 (the gnat and the aphid through the struggle), `tests/flies.luau` 229 to 271 (the two bodies), `tests/worldmap.luau` 191 to 308 (the perch list on real faces, the vents, the hide spots, the doors, the wall's budget), `tests/world.luau` 328 unchanged (the wall's look holds its new budget exactly), `tests/checkpoint.luau` 386 to 422 (the garden round trip, the old record, the refusals), `tests/state.luau` 495 to 500 (the server's check of garden roots), `tests/notebook.luau` 941 to 970 (the page, the trigger, the watcher's moment; the Godot pages still match), `tests/world_client.luau` 23 to 28 (prey only in the spider's region and only when whole, the seam, the descent, the stone's words, the return to a rest under the stone with the bed's prey where it was). `tests/run.py` bundles `Woodlouse`, `PreyWorld` and `BugBodies` and lists `garden_life` among the client suites. All 38 suites: 134,083 checks, nothing failed (61,293 in 37 at v32). Strict analyze clean; 146 of 146 modules compile.

The previews (`--world --views bed,stone,descent,wall,bed_life,corner_web`). Looked at: `bed` (the soil with its mounds, stones, litter and grass, the dock and the dandelion, a woodlouse at a stone's foot), `stone` (the stone on its rests, the gap, the spider in front), `bed_life` (the dock's lowest leaf with an aphid on it and a gnat's dark dot under it, aphids on the dandelion's rosette; they are 3 mm things and read as dots at this distance, which is right), `descent` and `wall` (the lichen as pale ellipses over the brick, the eroded course's chips, the spider on a mortar joint), `corner_web` (the three strands as fine white lines running from the slab's underside across the brick: the first camera looked at the wrong corner, the seed chose the east one). They show shape and placement only, not Roblox's materials, light or the part budgets on a device.

Not run in Studio: the garden's prey on a real client (whether six prey and four woodlice at 3 mm read at all on a television, and whether the gate ever holds them back long after the bed arrives), the descent on the line against streaming (the bed must be whole before the spider is a third of the way down; the line's 100 studs under the real boom cast and `LineFraming` beside a brick face), the stone's words at the real HUD, the page's trigger on a real step, the part budgets on a phone (about 200 parts for the bed's life at full), the woodlice as movement on the perception HUD (W5's caption rule may want them quieter), and whether a gnat is visible enough to hunt. Out of scope and not looked at: the wasp and the rival in the bed (W4), the aphids walking the plant, prey for the outer sill and the wall (none), several rivals.

### ROBLOX-101 - The garden's bestiary, part A: springtails, leafhoppers, small moths and crane flies (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

Part A of the bestiary round W3b (`docs/GARDEN_BESTIARY.md`, Nathan: "More more more bugs and types of bugs and information on them"): the four prey of its suggested first set, built on W3's pattern exactly (ROBLOX-96: a profile derived from the house fly's, a body under the fly's part cap, a catch factor and a meal, seeded perches per region, saves per region, a generated page with sources that were opened). The sill is untouched and pinned: its director, its twelve perches, its two vents, its seed 914 and every sill golden are as they were, and while the spider is on the sill no prey outside it runs (the outer sill, the sill's one neighbour, has prey now, so `PreyWorld.activeAt` returns the sill alone from the sill: its play, its percepts and its drawing are byte for byte W3's). Godot's defaults are untouched (`Species.fly_like_prototype`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`'s base numbers and `Feeding`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, the perception modules); W3's gnats, aphids and woodlice are as they were (`Garden.prey` gives the same 28 perches and four vents, the bed's director the same six prey from the same streams).

**Where the new behaviours live.** `Shared/Prey/PreyKinds` (new), called from `Prey/Fly` only for a profile whose body asks: `body.hop` (an escape hop on the reaction model: a parabola from where the prey stands to a free perch of its kind, or on level ground to a spot on the same surface, between the hop's `min` and `max`, away from the danger and swept for clearance in six chords before it leaves; state `HOP`, stepped by `PreyKinds.step` instead of `FlyFlight`; it lands perched, alert, and with `fly_after` may fly on), `body.flightless` (never begins a flight), `body.sidestep` (alert and watched from its own side, it goes round its stem or leaf to the perch behind, passing the perch's `round` point, in `time` seconds, not again for `rest`), `body.dusk`/`body.day` (the same animal's profile for the other part of the day: `PreyKinds.light` swaps a director's profile and every fly's, keeping the restlessness each drew and restarting its clock, so they do not all lift at once), `body.slip_chance` and `body.shed_chance`/`shed_after` (`PreyKinds.grip`, run by `PreyWorld.after` on the jaws before their step: the first grip on a moth may fail, once; a crane fly may leave a leg that many seconds into the struggle, once; either way the prey is out of the jaws and escapes as a torn-free fly does, and cannot be struck again for `PreyWorld.FREE_S` = 0.5 s, since it leaves from the spider's mouth while the strike that took it may still be open), `flight.wander_local` (a moth's or crane fly's wander loop keeps near the height it flew from: `Fly.launch`'s 0.8-to-10 band is the sill's, and the wall and the bed are far below it; the stream draws are unchanged) and `flight.home_in` (a flier left in the air with no perch within a flight, one that slipped the jaws far from its perches, flies a leg of open flight toward the nearest free perch and looks again; without it a crane fly carried to the middle of the outer sill hovered for ever). Two small fixes beside them: a fly standing with its heading along its surface's normal (spawned on the wall, or landed square on to it) has the heading laid on the surface, head up the wall (`PreyKinds.upright`, from `Fly.step`; never true on the sill's or the bed's level perches, nor on the dock stem's side where W3's aphid stands), and a fly caught mid-hop or mid-sidestep frees the perch it had reserved (`PreyDirector.strike`). `Data/Catch.factorFor` reads an optional `body.catch_factor` before the meal (none of Godot's profiles or W3's has one).

**The four** (`Data/Species`, each a copy of `fly_like_prototype` like the fruit fly and W3's two; game values, no measured behaviour claimed; all drawn at their true size, visual_scale 1, with the fly's hover).
- **Springtail** (`springtail_prototype`): an eighth of a housefly meal (0.35/8; the brief's "a sixth or less"), catch factor 0.5 (the floor: easy once held), radius 0.08, capture radius 0.34; flightless; hop 1.5 to 4 studs, 0.16 s in the air at most, 0.7 high; sight 3, startle 2.5, startle delays 0.01 to 0.09 s (it reacts fast); walks of 0.05 to 0.3 at 0.1 to 0.3 within 0.6 of its spot. Torn free, it flicks off from the jaws, or with nowhere to land is put back as an aphid is.
- **Leafhopper** (`leafhopper_prototype`): half a housefly meal, catch factor 1.0 (`catch_factor`: it kicks as hard as a house fly; by its meal it would be 0.7), radius 0.15; hop 3 to 10 studs, 0.3 s, 1.8 high, flying on after 35% of landings; sidestep 0.5 s, not again for 1.5 s; sight 8, startle 3.5; short flights (cruise 4, flee 8, at most 8 studs), restless rarely (0.01 a second).
- **Small moth** (`moth_prototype`, by day, and `moth_dusk_prototype`): a housefly meal, catch factor 1.0, radius 0.3, capture radius 0.5; slip 35% on the first grip; a slow flier (cruise 3, flee 6, take-off 30, turn 400 degrees a second, flights of 2 to 14) with wander loops on 80% of moves; restless 0.002 a second by day (still), 0.1 from dusk; replacements every 40 to 80 s.
- **Crane fly** (`crane_fly_prototype`, `crane_fly_dusk_prototype`): two housefly meals, catch factor 1.25 (`catch_factor`: by its meal it would be 1.6, the ceiling, and no tapping wins that; it is big but weak, so a little harder than a house fly: 33 of 60 seeded struggles won at four taps a second, all 60 at six, where the house fly is won 60 of 60 at four), radius 0.5, capture radius 0.6; sheds a leg 40% of the time, 0.6 to 1.6 s into the struggle, once; the slowest flier (cruise 1.8, flee 3.5, take-off 15, turn 240, flights of 2.5 to 18), wander loops on 70%; restless 0.001 by day, 0.07 from dusk; replacements every 60 to 120 s. The one prey that outsizes the spider, budgeted alone.

**Dusk.** `Garden.BESTIARY.dusk` = 18:00 to 6:00 by the life clock (the evening from `LifeClock.EVENING_H`, through the night to `DAWN_H`): the moths and crane flies are their dusk profiles then and their day ones otherwise. The hour reaches the sim as `PreyWorld.before`'s new last argument (the client passes `life.clock.hour`; given none, the last one holds); the profile a director and its flies have is part of the rest's record (both profiles are kept by name), so a rest at dusk comes back at dusk. Over two minutes by day no moth or crane fly leaves its perch; from dusk they fly and resettle (`tests/garden_life.luau`).

**Where they live and how many** (`World/Garden.bestiary(region)`: one spec per kind, each with its own director, its own seed `(region.seed + salt x 104729) mod 2^31-1`, its own prefix `bed_a_springtail_fly_`..., targets at the full and the low tier; every vent an `appear` vent, where a prey of the director's kind comes on a free perch within reach, out of sight, with no flight, W3's walker vents' rule without the aphid).
- The bed: **springtails** on 12 seeded spots of soil 0.7 to 2.2 studs from the woodlice's hide spots (the big stone's mouth and back, the other stones' feet, the litter), a stud apart, clear of every standing box and of the retreat, two vents at the stone's mouth and a stone's foot (reach 6); 4 at full, 2 at low. **Leafhoppers** on 12 perches in six pairs, each the other's far side: the dock stem low (east and west) and high (north and south), the dandelion's stalk (east and west), each dock leaf's top and underside two thirds of the way to its tip; two vents at the plants' feet (reach 14); 2 at full, 1 at low. With W3's six the bed holds **12 prey at full** (the brief's ceiling was 14) and 9 at low.
- The house wall: **moths** on 8 seeded spots of the brick face (x -30..30, y -45..-6, five apart), **a crane fly** on 6 (x -20..20, y -86..-40, six apart); vents two studs off the face; 2 and 1 at full, 1 and 1 at low.
- The outer sill: **a moth** on 6 perches by the window (the slab's ends against the frame, the window box's two fronts, the slab's front edge either side of the way down), **a crane fly** on 4 (the slab's front corners and the box fronts beside them); 1 and 1 at full, 1 and 0 at low. The glass is the sill's region and its prey is pinned, so no crane fly rests on the glass this round.
- The tier: `PreyWorld.tier` ("full" or "low", the client sets it from its detail tier) is read when a director is made. The bodies: the springtail 15 parts at full and 11 at low, the leafhopper 25 and 13, the moth 20 and 9, the crane fly 31 and 11 (28 and 10 with a leg gone); at full the bed's living prey is about 221 parts (W3's 111, 60 for the springtails, 50 for the leafhoppers) beside the woodlice's 96, the wall's 71 and the outer sill's 51.

**Drawn** (`Data/FlyLook.small`, `Client/FlyBodies`). The sheets gain three words: `full` (drawn only at the full tier), `low` (only at the low tier: the crane fly's legs there are one rod each, hip to foot), `wings` (pairs of several kinds, each with its own colour and material; `hide`: drawn at rest only, the moth's dark bands) and `shed` (the parts whose names begin with it are not drawn once the fly has `legsLost`). `FlyBodies.new` takes the detail tier; a template is built per kind, tier and shed leg. The springtail: a grey-blue thorax, abdomen and head, eyes, antennae, six legs and the furcula folded forward under the abdomen, swung down and back in the leap. The leafhopper: a broad head, eyes, pronotum, scutellum, the abdomen under two opaque green forewings held like a roof (a fan in flight), six two-part legs, pale spine rows along the hind tibiae, short antennae. The moth: head, eyes, a furry thorax, the abdomen, four wings lying flat and spread (the forewings out to the sides with a dark band each, the hindwings behind and under them; fans in flight), antennae, six short legs. The crane fly: head, snout, eyes, thorax, a long abdomen, two smoky glass wings held out in a V, halteres, antennae and six legs of three rods each, splayed at rest about four studs long and dangling in flight. All anchored, never solid, never seen by a ray, no shadow cast, as the fly's.

**The words.** The meal line (`LifeText.MEALS`): "8 springtails = 1 housefly" (in tenths), "2 leafhoppers = 1 housefly", "1 moth = 1 housefly", "1 crane fly = 2 houseflies" (in halves). A slip or a shed leg is a note for four seconds (`UI/PreyText`): "The moth slipped free, leaving scales in your fangs." and "The crane fly left a leg in your fangs and got away." (the client shows `PreyWorld.event` and clears it; no new local, `M.PreyText`).

**The pages** (`tools/gen_notebook_pages.py` ROBLOX_PAGES; 33 pages): "Springtails and the spring" (`springtails`, 462 characters), "Leafhoppers: the sidestep and the jump" (`leafhoppers`, 467), "Moths at the window" (`small_moths`, 488), "Crane flies: legs to spare" (`crane_flies`, 480), triggered by the first close look: `NotebookWatch.step` opens a kind's page the first tick one of the view's flies of that kind is within `NotebookPages.lookWu` = 4 studs of the spider (by distance; W3's watcher had no finer moment to offer, and the sensor's identification would need the perception modules to tell the kinds apart, which they do not). Every sentence rests on a source opened this round with WebFetch, recorded with the quotation it rests on in `docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md` (13 sources on the pages: university extension sheets from Florida, Kentucky, Illinois, Nebraska, Utah, Maine and Mississippi, the Missouri Department of Conservation's field guide twice, Burrows & Sutton 2008 *J. Exp. Biol.*, Fabian et al. 2024 *Nat. Commun.*, Alicea-Serrano et al. 2021 *Integr. Comp. Biol.* for Eisner et al. 1964, and Jackson 1977 *J. Arachnol.*, whose prey table lists a crane fly and a moth; two more were opened in support and are named only in the sources file). Left out for want of an opened source: that jumping spiders eat springtails in particular, why a leafhopper sits in line with its stem, how geometrid moths rest, any measured jump or leg length. W3's five sources were tried again: Cranshaw & Cloyd, Warren & Schalau and Jackson 1977 were opened, Hopkin 1991 only through the Field Studies Council's page and Fischer et al. 2025 only through the University of Greifswald's release; "feed in groups" became "live in colonies", "glands in their armour" became "glands on the sides of the body", the gnats' sheet is of 2003 (reviewed 2025), not 2009, and the woodlice's sentence gains Kentucky's ENTFACT-439 (the page now has six sources).

**Saves per region.** `PreyWorld` keeps each region's bestiary directors in its entry of the garden as `kinds` ({ [kind] = director }), beside W3's `prey` and `bugs`; the checkpoint records them in the same `garden` root and checks them as W3's director is (a kind the region has, a director, the sill's jaws, the flies' states and meals and perches; `HOP` is a known state); the wall and the outer sill may now be named in the root. A W3 record (the bed's gnats, aphids and woodlice alone) restores unchanged: the record's garden replaces the live one, so the kinds it lacks are made afresh from their seeds when their region is next whole, exactly as on a first arrival, and nothing the record had is rerolled. A moth's spent slip, a crane fly's lost leg, a springtail in the air and dusk on the wall come back as they were, and two worlds restored from one rest run on identically (`tests/checkpoint.luau`). Following W3's precedent (an optional part an old record lacks needs no version), `CONTENT_REVISION` stays `windowsill-roblox-1`, `SCHEMA_VERSION` 3 and `WorldMap.REVISION` `world-2`: no box of any region moved.

**Pins that moved with counts.** `tests/garden_life.luau` 72,546 to 108,273: eleven new cases (the profiles and their numbers, the bestiary's perches on real faces with the budgets at both tiers, the directors at both tiers and their determinism, the springtail's flick and the torn-free one, the leafhopper's sidestep and jump, the moth's and the crane fly's day and dusk and the world's hour, the moth's seeded slip, the crane fly's seeded shed leg and its drawn body a leg short, the world's prey with the bestiary, the bodies at both tiers), most of the count a check every tick that nothing leaves its region; and W3's world-prey case, whose numbers moved because the wall and the outer sill have prey now: high on the wall the wall's prey runs and the outer sill's beside it (W3: nothing ran there), at the wall's foot those and the bed's, 7 directors made, not 1, and the view 17 flies, not 6. `tests/prey.luau` 1656 to 1668 (the four through the struggle: their factors, a springtail held every time at two taps a second, a leafhopper fighting exactly as a house fly, a crane fly won 33 of 60 at four taps a second and 60 at six, Godot's controller untouched). `tests/checkpoint.luau` 422 to 451 (the bestiary's round trip, refusals, and the W3-shaped record). `tests/notebook.luau` 970 to 1082 (four pages, their triggers and the watcher's close look; the ground-life page's corrections and six sources; the long game opens 29 pages, not 25). `tests/world_client.luau` 28 to 29 (the bestiary through the real client: made and drawn at the client's tier, the wall's moths at dusk by the life clock's hour, the sill's prey alone on the sill; and W3's checks that held nothing ran on the wall now hold that the bed's prey does not run there, and count the bed's flies by name). All 38 suites: 169,964 checks, nothing failed (134,083 at v33); every golden and every other suite unchanged. Strict analyze clean; 148 of 148 modules compile. `tests/run.py` bundles `PreyKinds` and `PreyText`.

**Previewed** (`tools/preview/scene.py --world`, new views `spec_springtail`, `spec_springtail_air`, `spec_leafhopper`, `spec_moth`, `spec_moth_air`, `spec_crane_fly`, `spec_crane_fly_shed`, `spec_crane_fly_air` on a specimen tray at x 200, and `bestiary_stone`, `bestiary_wall` in place). Shape and placement only, not Roblox's materials or light.

Not run in Studio: whether a 1.5 mm springtail can be seen, approached and struck at all on a television or a phone, whether the hop reads as a flick or a teleport at 60 frames, the leafhopper going round its stem in front of the real camera, the moths and the crane fly on the brick at dusk under the game's light, the slip and shed notes on the real HUD, the crane fly's legs in the jaws at the real camera's distance, the part budgets on a phone (about 340 parts of living prey in the bed at full with the woodlice), and the pages' close-look trigger on a real step. Out of scope: part B of W3b (the ladybird, millipede, snail and harvestman as ambient bugs; the centipede at the stone with W4's rules), the thrips, midges, hoverflies, lacewings, caterpillars and whiteflies of the bestiary's table, the glass as a perch.

### ROBLOX-102 - Nathan's v33 notes, the quick ones: the room tone silenced, the music louder, hunger faster (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

Nathan, playing v33: "I hear the music but it is quiet and the hum should just be eliminated for now", and "still more difficult with faster life depletion". Three numbers, no new mechanics. The room tone (`Soundscape` MIX.room, a constant low hum under everything) goes 0.09 to 0: the voice is kept, silent, so the menu and mute wiring and their tests stay as they were; the wasp's hum is a warning, not ambience, and is untouched (if Nathan meant the wasp, that is a different decision). The music goes 0.18 to 0.32 (above the birds, under the wasp's hum 0.42; the duck to 40% and the 1.6 swell multiply it as before). The standard hunger factor `NeedsModel.game.drain` goes 1.9 to 2.4: a day costs 0.773 of the reserve (11 x 0.021 x 2.4 + 13 x 0.007 x 2.4), a full reserve lasts about 1.29 days, two houseflies no longer hold a day steady (three do), and the daily rival alone leaves a small deficit. Easy and Free keep `gentleDrain` 1.1. Godot's defaults are untouched.

Pins that moved on purpose: `tests/life.luau` (the factor, the day, the reserve's length, the meals that hold a day; count unchanged at 20,922), `tests/settings.luau` (the room at 0, the music at 0.32 through the duck, the swell and the slider; 1,151 unchanged). Not run in Studio: how 0.32 sounds on a television, and whether 2.4 is the hard-but-fair Nathan wants (the garden's slower clock, 0.33 in the bed, softens it there).

### ROBLOX-103 - The look: insects that walk and fly, bushes to the ground, a single-story house, more detail (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

W3c of `docs/WORLD_MAP_PLAN.md` ("The look"), from Nathan's notes on v33 played on a television: "better animations for the new insects"; "no holes, shrubs should be bushes and the house a single story. more realistic. still more detail. More beauty. Lighting is beautiful". The lighting is exactly as it was: `Data/Graphics`' lighting, sun, day and night, atmosphere, bloom, grade and shafts are untouched, and nothing added casts a shadow (the view's parts and every look's parts never have, the new region's walkable boxes are invisible under their look, and the tests hold it), so the light through the window falls as it did. Godot's defaults and every golden hold (`Species`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, perception); the sill's pieces in `WindowsillLayout` are as they were; no prey, no rule and no save field changed.

**The bodies move, pose only** (`Client/FlyBodies`, `Client/BugBodies`, numbers in `Data/FlyLook`'s new `motion` tables). No part is added: each part's frame in the body is turned before the one `BulkMoveTo` the flies and the woodlice already had, so every body keeps its count (the house fly 39 parts, the fungus gnat 22, the aphid 15, the springtail 15 and 11 at low, the leafhopper 25 and 13, the moth 20 and 9, the crane fly 31 and 11, 28 and 10 a leg short, the woodlouse 24; the fly's cap is 40). What moves:
- **Legs that step.** A leg segment turns about its leg's hip: a swing forward and back (`gait.swing`) and a lift of the foot on the forward stroke (`gait.lift`), in two tripods (front and hind of one side with the middle of the other) for the six-legged, and a metachronal wave down the woodlouse's seven pairs (each pair 0.9 rad behind the one in front, the sides in opposite phase). The phase advances by the ground the body covers on its surface since the last drawing, a cycle per `gait.stride` (the gnat 0.42 of its body units, the aphid 0.3, the springtail 0.07, the leafhopper 0.14, the moth 0.28, the crane fly 1.3, the house fly 0.36, the woodlouse 0.4 studs), so an aphid's shuffle, a leafhopper's sidestep round its stem, a fidget, a moth resettling and a woodlouse scurrying for cover each step at their own pace, and a body that stops stops stepping; the stepping fades in and out over a few frames (`settle`) and a move longer than `jump` between drawings (a hop's landing, a teleport) is not a step. In the jaws the legs kick (`kick`, 4 to 8 Hz). A fly in the air keeps its flight pose's tucked legs.
- **Wings.** The house fly's, the gnat's, the leafhopper's and the crane fly's flight fans are the soft blur they were, trembling at the fly's pattern (`FlyLook.wing.fan.shimmer`, at most 0.1 rad, under 0.03 rad a frame: no strobing, the test that has always held it holds). The moth beats visibly: its fans swing 0.75 rad either side of their roots at 5 Hz (at most 0.5 rad a frame, a smooth flap); landed, its wings shiver for 0.9 s, dying away, and lie flat.
- **The crane fly** rocks in flight (roll 0.14 rad and pitch 0.08 at 2.1 Hz, two waves mixed so it wobbles) and its hanging legs swing fore and aft (0.18 rad at 1.7 Hz, each leg its own phase).
- **Antennae** sway a little about their bases (0.08 to 0.18 rad, faster while walking), every body its own seeded phase.
- **The springtail's spring and the leafhopper's crouch.** A hopper whose reaction is pending (startled, `fly.pending`) sinks and tips before it leaves: the leafhopper 0.03 down and its nose up 0.18 rad on its hind legs, the springtail 0.012 down; in the air the springtail turns one somersault over the hop's time while its furcula is swung down and back (its flight pose, as it was). The somersault and every number of the motion are the game's: no source was opened for them this round and no page says anything of them.
- **The woodlouse** the page names, *Porcellio scaber*, cannot roll into a ball (a pill-bug can), so it scurries for cover on its stepping legs and does not roll.
Reduced Motion is read as the spider's rig reads it (the player's setting, passed by the client): the steps stay, as the rig's legs keep walking, and everything else is still (no sway, no trembling or beating fan, no shiver, no rocking, no crouch, no somersault). The far rule is the rival's and the mate's (ROBLOX-93): a body farther than 25 studs from the camera (`FlyLook.far`) is placed once every 0.1 s with all the time since, so it moves ten times a second; nearer, every frame; the simulation steps every prey every tick regardless. Phases come only from the prey's clock the client hands the drawing (`M.World.prey.clock`) and the ground covered, so a paused game is a still one and nothing random is drawn. `FlyBodies.draw(flies, now, cameraAt?, reduced?)` and `BugBodies.draw(lice, now?, cameraAt?, reduced?)`; the client passes the camera's place and the setting on the two lines that already drew them (no new local in `SpiderClient`).

**Bushes to the ground** (`Data/Flora.garden.shrubs`, `Habitat/Plants`). The view's eight shrubs were standards, a ball on a stem, placed by where they appeared on the glass; they are ten flowering bushes standing where they really stand in the garden's own studs (`x`, `z`, height `h`, width `w`), 0.55 to 1.5 m tall, round the edges where the map plan puts them: a hedge line west of the bed (shrub_west and hedge_west's ground) and two on the east border, none over a walkable region, the house's wall or a tree's trunk. Each is a rounded core of leaves whose underside sits 6% of its height above the lawn, eight clusters of leaves over its top (three, sunlit) and down its sides (five, the lowest resting on the lawn), three woody stems from the lawn splaying up into the crown, and seven blossoms on the side facing the house, all in `LeafyGrass` (a PBR leaf at about a box leaf's size) but for the blossoms and the stems. The low tier keeps the core, the three top clusters, two stems and two blossoms. 190 parts at full and 80 at low (128 and 40 before). The world plan's `shrub_west` region (walkable twigs) was not begun: the round's time went to the house; it stays next.

**A single-story house.** The new region `wall_upper` (`WorldMap`, after the bed in priority order; kind `wall_upper` in `World/Garden`), the map plan's region 4: the brick above the window, face z -24 from the window's head (y 14) to the eaves at y 110 (1.1 m above the sill), x -40..40, standing on two jambs either side of the window (x 31..40, y 0..14, on the wall's shoulders, where the house_wall's look has always drawn brick that nothing held up); the eaves' box out to the fascia (z -54, y 110..120, x -42..42; its underside is the soffit, walkable upside down); the fascia board (z -55.5..-54, y 106..120); the gutter hung on it, a trough 10.5 wide with its floor's top at y 109 (1.09 m above the sill, the high perch), a lip 6 high (y 115) and two end caps; and the eave course of the roof over it (y 120..121.5, from z -62, over half the gutter, to the wall's back). Ten boxes, each well under the 60 a region may carry. Bounds (-42, 0, -70)..(42, 130, -13.5); spawn in the gutter (0, 109, -61) heading along it; the sill's pace (clock 1.0); no prey; no retreat: the gutter's corner was looked at for one and is not sound yet (it is open to the sky with nothing to rest under; a leaf mat with a gap under it and its words in `LifeText.HOMES` are the work, left for the round that brings the eaves' house spider). Its seam is with the wall: the east shoulder's face goes on up as the east jamb's, (31, 0, -24) to (40, 0, -24) (the west is the same; the map lists one line a pair, as it always has). The streaming numbers hold (`tests/worldmap.luau`): from anywhere on it its farthest box is 144.7 studs (152.7 with the focus 8 behind, inside the 160 target), and from within ten studs of the seam on the wall the rest of it is at most 150.5 (158.5); the eaves were set at 110 and not 120 for that margin. `WorldMap.bounds()` is now (-42, -93, -76)..(42, 130, 16). Its look (`Garden.decor`, 154 parts at full, 59 at low, the budget exactly): the brick courses on from where the wall's look stops (y 30) in the same bond and colours over a recessed mortar bed and the room's plaster behind, the soffit, fascia, gutter and eave course over the boxes they stand in for, the roof's slope at 35 degrees from the eaves over the whole house to a ridge 3.9 m back, and the rest of the house drawn round it as a backdrop until wall_west and wall_east are built: the back wall either side out to x ±500 (5 m) from the lawn to the eaves, meeting the walkable wall with no slit, its eaves, fascia and gutter, a downpipe from the gutter at x -50 to a shoe on the lawn; at the full tier the tile joints, the gutter's rolled lip and brackets, mortar lines along the long walls (toned toward the brick so they read as courses), another window like the kitchen's (the sill's own size: the kitchen window is 62 by 14) and the back door on its step, leaves, moss and a puddle in the gutter, dirt streaks under it, lichen and an old cobweb in the eaves' corner. The house is one storey under a pitched roof: nothing above the eaves but the roof.

The house_wall's spawn moved: it stood on the east shoulder's top (35, 0, -18.75), which the east jamb now covers, so it is on the shoulder's face (35, -1.5, -24) heading up. A spawn is only where a rest from another revision goes back to, and nothing can rest on the wall (no retreat), so no save is affected. **`WorldMap.REVISION` stays `world-2`** (W1's rule: adding a region somewhere new needs no revision): no box of any region moved; the new jambs stand in what was air over the shoulders (and inside the house_wall look's drawn brick), where no rest can be kept. `tests/world_client.luau`'s two placements of the client's spider on that shoulder's top now place it on the shoulder's face (the test driver's `place` takes an optional `up`).

**The houses across the way are bungalows** (`Graphics.view.across`, built by `Scenery.across`). The seven houses were tall narrow blocks (14 high on the glass against 5 to 6.6 wide, two or three storeys to the eye) standing 1.2 m beyond the glass, inside the garden once it could be walked; they are three bungalows in the garden's own studs beyond the back fence (fronts at z -1460, 14.6 m out): rendered walls 2.6 m tall and 5.2 to 7.6 m wide, a roof's front slope rising at 30 degrees from eaves overhanging 30 cm to a ridge over the middle, a ridge cap, one row of windows and a door, chimneys on two (both tiers: the walls and the roofs; 20 parts at full, 6 at low). The garden's fences: the back fence across the garden's end (z -1420, 10 m) and two side fences from the house to it (x ±500), 1.8 m of boards (`WoodPlanks`), the back one's posts at full. The trees moved back to be real trees in the neighbours' gardens beyond the side fences (7.4 and 9 m out, 5 and 3.5 m tall, on round trunks: a new view kind `trunk`); they had been lollipops a metre tall just past the bed, the very standards the bushes note was about.
