The far hills are flattened ellipsoids 27 m off (kind `hill`), their feet under the horizon (the balls stood inside the garden). Each moved item keeps its place on the glass from the reference eye, by construction (`Scenery.viewPosition`). The sill's Decor is 1,253 parts at full and 355 at low (1,178 and 314; `Region` records it and `tests/world.luau` holds it).

**Detail and realism on the four built regions** (each from a Park-Miller stream of its own placed after everything before it, so nothing placed before moves; the woodlice's litter hides, the springtails' spots and every perch are where they were). The outer sill (94/36 to 116/39): petals fallen from the window box's black-eyed Susans, some browning (three at both tiers), their seeds, two dry leaves, hairline cracks in the paint. The wall (110/75 to 134/75): drip streaks under the outer sill's front edge, mortar weathered darker and deeper in places along the joints, moss in the joints near the foot. The bed (364/107 to 466/113): four side veins on each of the seven leaves (the Pilea's rim, underside, midrib and veins standard), seed heads on stalks over half the tufts, six grass clumps creeping in at the edges (a blade of each kept at low) each with a seed head, fourteen more pieces of litter of four kinds (skeleton leaves gone to lace, dark rotting ones, small round yellow ones, husks, in `Fabric`) and an empty snail shell. The sill's view: the bushes, the bungalows, the fences, the trees and the hills above. No hole or seam: `tests/world.luau` holds that the view's lawn lies under every region's whole footprint at or below its lowest floor, that the bed's soil stands in the lawn with its top above it, that the house's back wall meets the walkable wall with no slit from the lawn to the eaves, that the roof covers the whole house, and that the wall above takes up the brick where the wall's drawing stops. The four looks together are 870 parts at full and 286 at low (568 and 218 before), beside the sill's Decor.

Pins that moved on purpose (the suite's totals are in the README). `tests/flies.luau` 271 to 286: the gnat steps while it walks and its legs are still and back in the standing pose when it stops, no foot goes through the surface, no part is added, the antennae sway and are still in Reduced Motion while the legs still step, the wing's phase runs in flight and holds at rest, the house fly walks on two tripods, the legs kick in the jaws, the blur is still in Reduced Motion, no NaN for any kind of the sill and the bed walking, flying or held on awkward headings, and the far rule (60 placings a second near, ten far). `tests/garden_life.luau` 108,273 to 108,327: every bestiary kind at both tiers steps while it walks and is still when it stands with its part count kept, the moth's visible smooth flap and its still blur in Reduced Motion, its wings shivering on landing and then still, the crane fly's rocking and dangling legs and their stillness in Reduced Motion, the leafhopper's crouch nose up before the jump (not in Reduced Motion), the springtail upside down part way through its hop, and the woodlouse's wave, its stillness when stopped, its antennae and the far rule. `tests/world.luau` 328 to 301 (a check per blossom: seventy blossoms now for 112): the roofs, chimneys and treetops on the glass counting the bungalows' roofs and chimneys, the bungalows one storey with one row of windows under a pitched roof, the fences, the two trees on round trunks beyond the side fences, the bushes to the lawn, their height, their frames and their clearance, the sill's Decor at 1,253 and 355, five regions in `World`, four looks in `WorldDecor` at their budgets, 37 boxes outside, and the floor and seam checks above. `tests/worldmap.luau` 308 to 346: five regions in priority order, the wall above's bounds, pace, spawn and no retreat, the wall's moved spawn, four seams both ways, the union, priority where the wall above meets the outer sill and the wall, the budgets, and the wall above's jambs on the shoulders, its brick from the window's head to the eaves and its gutter. `tests/rivalnav.luau` 355 to 364 (its per-region loops take in the fifth region's graph and seam). `tests/world_client.luau` 29 unchanged: its two placements on the shoulder's top are on its face. And one pin that ROBLOX-102 left behind: `tests/client_mating.luau` held the music at 0.18 x 1.6 in the mating scene's swell, and the music is 0.32 since then, so it failed in this round's full run (27 of 28); it is 0.32 x 1.6 now and passes 28 of 28 run again. All 38 suites: 170,053 checks, nothing failed (169,964 at ROBLOX-101; the full run's log has CLIENT_MATING at 27 of 28, the stale pin, and the suite was run again on the final tree). Strict analyze clean; 148 of 148 modules compile.

**Previewed** (`tools/preview/scene.py --world`; the specimen tray's standing bodies now walk a quarter stride over ten frames and the fliers beat on, and it gains a second row, a gnat, an aphid, a house fly and a woodlouse; new views `gutter`, `eaves`, `house`, `garden`, `bushes`, `outer_detail`, `wall_foot`, `spec_gnat_walk`, `spec_aphid_walk`, `spec_fly_walk`, `spec_woodlouse`). Looked at, before and after: `window` (from the sill: the bungalows' roofs and chimneys low in the middle of the glass, pale with distance, the bushes' crowns low at the sides, the trees' crowns as before), `house` (from the lawn: the single-story house, brick to the eaves under one pitched roof, the gutter and downpipe, the back door, the kitchen window's slit in its patch of laid brick, the bushes to the ground; before it showed an 80-stud slab of wall and lollipop shrubs), `garden` (from over the eaves: the fenced lawn, the bushes round its edges, the bungalows and the trees beyond the fences), `eaves` and `gutter` (the soffit, fascia, gutter with its lip and the eave course, the roof rising), `bushes` (from the outer sill), `world`, `bed`, `bed_life` (seed heads, veins), `outer_detail` (petals and seeds on the stone), `wall_foot` (moss in the joints), and the bodies mid-stride and mid-beat (`spec_fly_walk`, `spec_gnat_walk`, `spec_leafhopper`, `spec_woodlouse`, `spec_moth_air`, `spec_crane_fly_air`). They caught: the trees as lollipops by the house (moved out), the hills as domes inside the garden (made low and far), a camera inside the roof (moved). Honest limits: the previewer shades like Godot, not Roblox; the bushes read as lumpy balls of leaves in it (Roblox's `LeafyGrass` texture is not imitated); the long walls' mortar lines are lines, not bricks, so the patch of laid brick round the window shows from the lawn; and far things (the bungalows at 14.6 m, the hills at 27 m) are washed pale by the previewer's fog, and will be by Roblox's Atmosphere, whose density was tuned for the sill.

Not run in Studio: the insects' motion at 60 frames (whether the steps read as walking and the moth's flap as a flap on a television, whether a gnat's blur trembling and a crane fly's rocking look alive or busy, the crouch's flash before a leafhopper's leap at the real camera's distance, the far rule's ten placings a second at 25 studs), the bushes' `LeafyGrass` at 1 stud = 1 cm and their silhouettes against the lawn, the bungalows and hills through Roblox's Atmosphere (they may be too faint: the Atmosphere was tuned when the view was 1 to 3 m off), the house and its roof from the bed and from the descent, walking up the brick past y 35 into the wall above, along the soffit upside down to the fascia and into the gutter, streaming the wall above (152.7 studs from its far corner, inside the 160 target) and the readiness warning there, the looks' parts at join (about 300 more at full, 70 at low), part counts and frame time on a phone and the PlayStation. Out of scope: `shrub_west`, the gutter's retreat and the eaves' house spider, wall_west and wall_east (the drainpipe to climb, the back door).

### ROBLOX-104 - A self-test of the live place in Studio: press Play, read the verdict (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

Almost nothing since main ce64498 has been walked in Studio: the world core, the regions outside the window, the garden's life, the bestiary and the look have reached the playtest on the strength of the CLI suites and the previewer alone. The coordinator can drive Studio on Nathan's PC (open the place, press Play, click "Begin a new life", read Studio's log files) but cannot play it. So the live place now carries a self-test that plays the parts that matter inside a real Play session, in the real engine, and prints a verdict a script can read: every publish can be checked by pressing Play.

The trigger. `Client/SelfTest.start` runs only when `RunService:IsStudio()` is true **and** the Workspace attribute `SelfTest` is true (set from the command bar before Play: `workspace:SetAttribute("SelfTest", true)`); otherwise it refuses and returns false, so an attribute left set and saved with the place does nothing on a live server or for a player. The client asks for it in one line at its very end, inside the same `if` that tests the two conditions, so with the attribute off the module is not even required: zero cost. The client is at Luau's 200-local limit, so the hooks the test place's playback was already handed (the motor, the world, the prey, the life, the input, the menus, the notebook, the stores, the soundscape and the place hook) became a function in the client's module table, `M.hooks()`, called by both the playback and the self-test; it adds `detail` (the tier). No new local; the playback gets the same table as before. The server clears nothing and knows nothing of it.

The run (`SelfTest.run`, about a minute of play here, plus whatever streaming takes): it begins a new life itself, by the start menu's own "Begin a new life" (`CaughtPanel.choose("New")`, what the button's click calls; in the test place, which has no start menu, by the same `fresh` the choice calls), so the coordinator only presses Play. Then, each in its own `pcall` so an error is a FAIL and never ends the run: the `World` folder and a folder for every region; for each of the five regions the spider held at its spawn (put back every frame, so it cannot fall through what has not streamed in) until the region's folder holds every one of `Region.pieces` (30 s deadline; a FAIL names what is missing), then placed on the surface its spawn lies on (found by rays along the six axes: the wall's spawn is on a face), a ray finds ground under it, it is still there a second later and still attached, and `WorldClient`'s region is that region; three seconds walking on the sill (more than 1.5 studs, never off the floor); the line from the outer sill's front edge: the drop key held, onto the line within 15 s, then six seconds of letting down measured, on the line throughout and within a stud of its fall, at 0.6 to 0.9 studs a second (the game's 0.75, ROBLOX-99); the sill's flies, the bed's gnats, aphids, springtails and leafhoppers, and the wall's moths made, each drawn as a folder of its id under `Flies` with 1 to 40 parts (the fly's budget); the woodlice made and drawn with 1 to 24 parts (`BugBodies.PARTS`); the moths at dusk (the life clock's hour put to 20:00, as the world_client suite does: the dusk profile within 3 s and a moth in the air within 30 s; the hour put back to noon); the notebook opened (`openNotebook("game")`) and closed; a rest in the crack (the eat key pressed there at noon), its checkpoint kept with `sill_crevice`, the rest ended by leaving, and the return to it (the reset key) back resting in the crack; the music `Sound` playing with a volume above 0 and the room tone silent (ROBLOX-102); then, as numbers and not verdicts, the frame time over 5 s in the bed and on the sill (frames, mean and worst render-step time, `GetRealPhysicsFPS`) and the parts in the workspace by top-level child at the client's tier. At the end the spider is put back where a life begins.

The lines, all to Output: `[SELFTEST] BEGIN ...`, then `[SELFTEST] PASS <name>`, `[SELFTEST] FAIL <name>: <why>` or `[SELFTEST] SKIP <name>: <why>` for each of the 20 checks, `[SELFTEST] INFO <what>: <numbers>` for what was measured, `[SELFTEST] ... <name> (<t> s in)` every 5 s of waiting so a hang shows where it hung, and always last `[SELFTEST] END pass=<n> fail=<m> skip=<k>`. `tools/studio/selftest.ps1` (PowerShell, for the coordinator) finds the newest log under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Roblox\logs` modified in the last few minutes that holds a BEGIN, strips whatever Studio puts before the marker (the timestamp, the thread, `[FLog::CreatorOutput]`), prints the latest run's lines as they arrive, and stops at END (exit 0 for fail=0, 1 for failures) or its deadline (exit 2). It was written, not run: there is no PowerShell here.

Proved on the CLI first. `tests/selftest.luau` (new, in `CLIENT_TESTS` and the default list) builds the folders the server builds (`WorldBuild.world`, as world_client does), sets the attribute, loads the real client and runs the self-test through its one line with a host of its own: the run is a coroutine, each wait is that many simulated frames of 1/60 s, the clock is the simulated one, and the frame sample says it is the shim's. It passes 20 of 20 there in 49 s of play (2,964 frames; about three and a half minutes of CPU): the line let down at 0.752 studs a second, 7.5 studs walked in 3 s, 4 flies on the sill, 3 gnats, 3 aphids, 4 springtails and 2 leafhoppers in the bed, 4 woodlice, 2 moths on the wall. The suite's four checks: the line starts it; the run reaches END with every check a PASS, fail=0 and skip=0, in under three minutes of play; heartbeats, the INFO lines and the spider back at the sill's spawn with the hour put back; and it refuses outside Studio and without the attribute. The start's path (the test place has no start menu, so `fresh` rather than the menu) and the host are the only differences from Studio, and the shim says nothing about streaming, the engine's physics, sound or frame time: that is what the Studio run is for.

Pins that moved: none; one suite added. `python tests/run.py` runs 39 suites, all green with nothing failed: 170,057 checks, ROBLOX-103's 170,053 and SELFTEST's 4; no other count moved. Strict types clean; 149 of 149 modules compile (the client at -O0, -O1 and -O2, still under the 200-local limit).

Not run in Studio: all of it. The first Studio run is the real test of the test: the deadlines (45 s for the new life, 30 s for a region to stream in) and the dusk's 30 s for a moth to fly are guesses; the hold at a spawn reports the spider's place to the server every frame as the game does (`WorldClient.update`), which is what should bring each region in; if a check fails in Studio the line says why, and the first thing to ask is whether the check or the game is wrong.
### ROBLOX-105 - The garden's bestiary, part B: ladybirds, millipedes, a garden snail and harvestmen, the aphids' walk, and nothing that is not food shown to the senses (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

Part B of the bestiary round W3b (`docs/GARDEN_BESTIARY.md`'s ambient table, Nathan: "More more more bugs and types of bugs and information on them"): four ambient bugs that are not food, on the woodlouse's pattern (ROBLOX-96: a small seeded sim per kind and region, no raycast, bodies in `Client/BugBodies`, saves per region, a generated page whose sources were opened), animated the way ROBLOX-103 animates the bugs, and Nathan's two open W3 calls taken with defaults. The sill is untouched (no ambient bug lives on it, and none runs while the spider is there: `PreyWorld.activeAt` is as it was). Godot's defaults are untouched (`Species.fly_like_prototype`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, the perception modules and their words); the four sims, the plants' paths and the walk are new modules or game tables.

**The aphids walk their plant** (Nathan's W3 call, "the aphids only shuffle on one leaf ... say if they should wander the plant"; the default taken: a little, slowly, perch to perch). `World/Garden.plants(bed)` gives each of the bed's plants as walks over its own faces: the dock's stem's bare north face (z lo, the one no leaf meets) is a highway from its foot to its top, and each leaf's top and the stem's east side is reached from it round the stem's corners (a point on each edge, its normal between the faces') and onto the leaf at the edge where the leaf meets the stem; the dandelion's highway is a ring of eight points on the soil round its stalk, each rosette leaf reached up its inner end, the stalk climbed from the east point. Every aphid perch is a node exactly on its perch; `Garden.plantPath` is the walk between two nodes (down one route, along the highway the short way round, out the other), `Garden.plantOf` names a perch's plant. `Prey/PlantWalk` walks such a list (place, normal turning from face to face along each leg, direction), plain data so a rest keeps it. The aphid's profile gains `body.creep` = { every 40 to 110 s, 0.12 to 0.25 studs a second, at most 24 studs } (game values), read by `Prey/PreyKinds.creep` from `Fly.rest` (one line in the frozen model's hooks, beside ROBLOX-101's sidestep): perched, calm, not walking, fidgeting or being eaten, and its time come, it draws a free walker perch of its own plant within reach from its own stream, gives up its perch for the new one at once (so nothing else spawns on it) and walks there over the faces, its body at its hover above the surface; startled on the way it freezes where it is (the walker's rule); caught or torn free, the walk is over (`PreyDirector.escape` clears it). It never flies and never leaves its plant (`tests/garden_life.luau` holds every tick of seven minutes: on the plant's faces, at most its speed along the way, on its own plant, three or more walks).

**What the senses are shown** (Nathan's W3 call: "whether the woodlice should show to the senses at all ... or be invisible to the perception HUD"; the brief: as "not food" if the pipeline supports it cheaply, else invisible). It does not: the perception modules (Godot-ported, golden) know three categories, movement, possible prey and fly-like prey, their words are frozen (`UI/PerceptionText`), and a movement contact carries no target id the HUD could caption, so "not food" would mean a fourth category through the golden sensor. So the woodlice and the four new bugs are left out of `PreyWorld.percepts` (`PreyWorld.SENSED` = false; `Woodlouse.percepts` is kept, unused, for the day a caption rule exists): they are seen with the eyes on screen, never as a HUD ring the player might hunt. The wasp, the rival and the mate stay movement as they were.

**The strike refused.** `PreyWorld.strikeAmbient`, after the directors' strikes: a window opened by a leap (the motor's `launched` as it leaves the ground, as the directors' own), open for 4 s and closed 0.05 s after landing; while it is open and the jaws are empty, the spider's sweep from last tick's place to this one is tested against every woodlouse and ambient bug of the active regions (each colony's `targets`: a creature, a place, a radius; plus the directors' 0.3). The first within reach is refused, once a leap: `event` = { kind = "refused", prey = kind }, shown for four seconds by the client's existing note line (`UI/PreyText.REFUSED`), and the creature answers (`struck`): the ladybird reflex-bleeds and keeps still for 6 s, the millipede coils at once, the snail draws into its shell for 25 to 45 s, a woodlouse runs for cover, the harvestman walks on. Nothing is ever held. The words, each from its page's sources: "A foul fluid wells from the ladybird's leg joints. You let it go." / "The millipede coils tight, armour out, and smells foul. You let it go." / "The snail is far too big, and it has drawn into its shell." / "The harvestman gives off a foul smell and stalks on. You let it go." / "The woodlouse's glands give off a secretion spiders avoid. You let it go."

**The four** (`World/Garden.AMBIENT` and `Garden.ambient(region)`: one spec per kind per region, its own seed `(region.seed + salt x 104729) mod 2^31-1`, its starting places for the full tier, ids carrying the region, `bed_a_ladybird_1`...; the low tier takes the first `low`). Game values throughout but the sizes, which are true at 1 stud = 1 cm.
- **Ladybird** (`Prey/Ladybird`; bed, 2 at full, one on the dock and one on the dandelion, 1 at low): walks its plant's faces (`PlantWalk` over `plantPath`) at 0.35 to 0.6 studs a second between seeded nodes, pausing 2 to 7 s; when its appetite is back (50 to 120 s after the last) it walks to a perch where an aphid sits, stops 0.35 short facing it, and eats it there in view for 8 to 14 s (`heldBy` keeps the aphid from setting off; the aphid is then `CONSUMED`, its director tidies it away and its population owes another, as after the spider's meal). Cheap: a few lines on the aphid's own fields, no new rule in the director. Ten minutes in `tests/garden_life.luau`: on the faces every tick, at most 0.6 studs a second, aphids eaten and replaced. Drawn 0.6 long (a seven-spot is 5 to 8 mm): a red dome with seven black spots set into its surface, a black pronotum with two pale patches, head, antennae, six legs in two tripods; struck, a yellow drop at each front leg joint (kept inside the body otherwise); eating, its head works. 22 parts at full, 18 at low.
- **Millipede** (`Prey/Millipede`; bed, 2 / 1): walks the soil at 0.15 to 0.28 studs a second round what stands on it as the woodlice do, but steers in an arc (it feels 0.8 studs ahead and turns at most 90 degrees a second, never folding back on itself), and every 60 to 140 s goes under the nearest stone hide spot for 20 to 45 s. Within 2.5 studs of the spider it coils (in 1 s) and stays coiled until the spider has been 5 studs off for 6 to 12 s, then uncoils (2.5 s) and walks on. The body follows the head: the head's track is kept as `trail` (a point every ring's spacing, 13 kept) and the drawing lays twelve rings 0.19 apart along it (2.4 cm), each with two pairs of legs (one pair at the low tier) stepping in a wave down the body; coiled, the rings wind into a flat spiral round the head. 63 parts at full, 39 at low.
- **Garden snail** (`Prey/Snail`; bed, 1 / 1): crawls 0.07 to 0.12 studs a second (about a millimetre a second) between seeded goals on the soil or, a third of the time, up the brick at the wall's foot over the bed (x -20..20, 1.5 to 14 above the soil), by way of the corner where soil meets brick; it rests 90 to 240 s between by day and 8 to 30 s from 18:00 to 6:00. It drops a trail point every half stud crawled (25 kept), each fading over 240 s. Its eye stalks draw in within 5 studs of the spider and come out past 7; it does not crawl with them in. Drawn: a shell 3 across (28 to 32 mm) with a dark band, its last whorls and the aperture's lip, the foot and head, the upper stalks with their eyes (sliding into the head as they draw in, laid inside it when in), the lower feelers; withdrawn, the soft parts inside the shell; the trail a pale ribbon of 24 legs at full, 12 at low. 36 parts at full, 24 at low.
- **Harvestman** (`Prey/Harvestman`; bed, 1 / 1, on the soil and litter; house wall, 1 / 1, on the brick): away by day (from its corner: the bed's first litter hide spot, the wall's foot at its west end, x -34 y -82), out from 18:00 to 6:00, walking its surface at 0.8 to 1.4 studs a second on a seeded wander round what stands on it, 0.9 above the surface on its legs; it pays the spider no heed. Drawn: an oval body 0.5 long with its eye turret and palps, eight legs of two rods each, up to the knee and down to the foot, the second pair longest (spanning about four studs), stepping in two alternating sets; away, drawn nowhere near. 20 parts at both tiers.

**Budgets.** `Garden.AMBIENT.budget`: the bed 240 at full and 110 at low (used: 2 x 22 + 2 x 63 + 36 + 20 = 226, and 18 + 39 + 24 + 20 = 101), the house wall 24 (20). With W3's and W3b part A's, the bed's living things are about 540 parts at full (its prey about 221, the woodlice 96, the ambient bugs 226); `tests/garden_life.luau` holds the ambient sums under the budgets at both tiers.

**The pages** (`tools/gen_notebook_pages.py` ROBLOX_PAGES; 37 pages): "Ladybirds: bright for a reason" (`ladybirds`, 477 characters), "Millipedes: the coil" (`millipedes`, 410), "Snails: eyes on stalks" (`garden_snails`, 427), "Harvestmen: not spiders" (`harvestmen`, 459), triggered by the first close look: `NotebookWatch.step`'s view gains `bugs` (`PreyWorld.crawlers`: every woodlouse and ambient bug of the active regions, each with its `kind` and `hidden`), and a bug of a kind with a page, out in the open, within `lookWu` = 4 studs opens it (`NotebookPages.looks` gains the four). Seven sources opened with WebFetch this round, quoted sentence by sentence in `docs/NOTEBOOK_SOURCES.md`: Kentucky's ENTFACT-702 and the Bohart Museum's note 055 (ladybirds), Buglife (millipedes), UF/IFAS EENY240 and the Missouri Department of Conservation (snails), Kentucky's Critter Files and the MDC again (harvestmen). Left out for want of an opened source: the colour of the ladybird's fluid, that jumping spiders refuse ladybirds, the millipede's leg counts, how long a snail's trail lasts, the trail as a surface silk will not hold (only drawn), playing dead, any leg length.

**Saves per region.** A region's entry in the checkpoint's `garden` root may hold `ambient` ({ ladybird, millipede, snail, harvestman }), each a colony of its own class (`Checkpoint.CLASSES` gains `Ladybirds`, `Millipedes`, `Snails`, `Harvestmen`), checked as the woodlice are: a kind the region has, the right class, every creature in a known state inside the region's bounds, its walk and trail in order. A ROBLOX-101 record (no `ambient`) restores unchanged and the colonies it lacks are made afresh from their seeds when the region is next whole (`PreyWorld.ambient`), so nothing it had is rerolled; a snail in its shell, a coiled millipede and a bleeding ladybird come back as they were and two worlds restored from one rest run on identically (`tests/checkpoint.luau`). The house wall may now hold an `ambient` without `kinds`. An aphid's walk and its `heldBy` are fields of the fly, recorded with it. `CONTENT_REVISION` stays `windowsill-roblox-1`, `SCHEMA_VERSION` 3, `WorldMap.REVISION` `world-2` (an optional part an old record lacks needs none; no box moved).

**The client.** No new top-level local: `M.World.bugs` is made with the detail tier (`BugBodies.new(Workspace, detail)`), the drawing line draws `PreyWorld.crawlers()` instead of the woodlice alone, the watcher's view gains `bugs`, and a refusal comes through the line that already shows `PreyWorld.event`. `BugBodies` draws each creature by its `kind` (none: a woodlouse, drawn exactly as before) in the one `BulkMoveTo`, with the far rule and Reduced Motion as the woodlouse's; a rig whose kind changes under the same id is rebuilt; the snail's trail sets a part's transparency only when it changes by more than 0.01.

**Pins that moved with counts.** `tests/garden_life.luau` 108,327 to 255,758: nine new cases (the plants as paths on real faces with every aphid perch a node; the aphids' walk over seven minutes, frozen when startled, ended by the jaws, alike from one seed; the ambient specs per region and their part budgets at both tiers; the ladybirds on their plants' faces eating aphids that are replaced, bleeding when struck; the millipedes walking, going under, coiling and uncoiling; the snail's crawl over the soil and up the brick, its trail and its fading, its stalks, its shell; the harvestmen away by day and out from dusk on the soil and the brick; the world's colonies at both tiers, never sensed, and a strike at each of the five refused in its words; the drawn bodies at both tiers), most of the count checks every tick; and three of W3's checks moved on purpose: the aphids' drift from their perches is held only when they are not walking to another (and to W3's 0.8 wander plus a fidget's 0.1: ROBLOX-105's walks draw from the aphids' streams, and one fidget now lands at 0.88, a thing the fly model always allowed), and the world's percepts no longer count the four woodlice. `tests/checkpoint.luau` 451 to 469 (the ambient colonies of the bed and the wall kept and put back, a snail in its shell, a coiled millipede and a bleeding ladybird as they were, two worlds alike after, a record without them restoring, and three refusals). `tests/notebook.luau` 1,082 to 1,184 (four pages and their sources, the watcher's close look at an ambient bug in the open and not at a hidden or far one or a woodlouse, the trigger list, the long game opens 33 pages, not 29). `tests/world_client.luau` 29 unchanged, two of its checks rewritten: in the bed the drawn bugs are the four woodlice and the bed's six ambient bugs, and on the wall nothing of the bed is drawn (the wall's harvestman may be). `tests/run.py` bundles `PlantWalk`, `Ladybird`, `Millipede`, `Snail` and `Harvestman`. All 38 suites: 317,604 checks, nothing failed (170,053 at ROBLOX-103); every golden and every other suite unchanged. Strict analyze clean; 153 of 153 modules compile.

**Previewed** (`tools/preview/scene.py --world`: the tray's third row at z 10, a ladybird walking and one bleeding, a millipede straight and one coiled, a snail with a trail and one with its stalks in, a harvestman; and in place after a minute and a half of an evening, the bed's aphids walking with them; new views `spec_ladybird`, `spec_millipede`, `spec_snail`, `spec_harvestman`, `ambient_dock`, `ambient_dandelion`, `ambient_snail`, `ambient_millipede`, `ambient_harvestman`, `ambient_wall`, at both tiers). Looked at: the ladybirds (red domes, the spots set into the dome, the pale patches, six legs; the bleeding one's yellow drop at its front leg; a black seam drawn down the back stood out of the dome as a spike and was taken out), the millipedes (the first draw was a string of beads: the rings are longer now and overlap; a sharp hairpin where one had turned on the spot is gone with the steering in an arc; the coil reads as a coil), the snails (the stalks drawn in first poked out of the side of the head and now lie inside it; the shell's colours toned to a garden snail's brown), the harvestmen (on the soil and on the brick, the legs spanning about four studs), the ladybird on the dandelion's leaf beside an aphid, one on the dock's stem by an aphid, the snail's trail as a pale ribbon on the soil, the low tier with one ladybird. Shape and placement only, not Roblox's materials or light.

Not run in Studio: whether the ladybird on the dock, the millipede's coil, the snail's stalks and its trail and the harvestman's legs read at the real camera's distance on a television and a phone (a 6 mm ladybird is a red dot from four studs away), the ladybird eating in view (does it read as eating?), the refusal notes on the real HUD, the strike window against the real motor's leaps (whether a leap that lands beside a woodlouse is refused when it should be, or one that misses is), the snail's trail transparency changes and its ribbon on the brick, the far rule for the millipede's 63 parts, the part budgets on a phone (the ambient bugs add 101 parts to the bed at low), and the pages' close look on a real step. Out of scope: slugs, the earthworm, bees, the ant trail (the bestiary's ambient table's other rows), the centipede at the stone (W4), the snail's trail as a surface silk will not hold.

### ROBLOX-106 - Nature's rules (W4): movement costs, death loses the day, threats that linger, mating that can fail, home before dark, cover (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-19)

W4 of `docs/OPEN_WORLD_DESIGN.md` ("Nathan's direction (2026-10-19), as rules", its "continued" section and the evening section's W4 items). The design called this round "ROBLOX-97"; that number was never used, and ROBLOX-106 is the next free one. Nathan: "hunger always a major concern"; "movement expends more energy"; "if you die you lose everything from that day"; "sometimes you worry about starving because a wasp or other thing won't go away"; "mating should not automatically be successful"; "Normal mode should feel like it doesn't want you to survive"; "being perched up high avoiding predators and hiding under leaves"; "scared I won't make it home at night". Every rule is the standard rules' (Normal) only: Easy and Free play keep ROBLOX-91's gentleness untouched, and a model, director or snapshot not told the new rules behaves exactly as before. Godot's defaults are untouched (`NeedsModel`'s rates, the wasp's and the rival's numbers, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, perception): everything new lives in `X.game` tables, a new pure module `Shared/Life/Nature`, and optional record fields. All draws are seeded (world seed 914), never `math.random`, and restores never reroll. The footfall and the rest of the evening section's "rare things" wait for a round of their own, as the design orders them.

**A seeded stream that does not read as a schedule** (`Prey/PreyRandom.mixed`). The new draws (each day's wasp windows, each visit's length, each rival leg, each night, each court) are keyed by several whole numbers. A Park-Miller stream seeded with a linear mix of them (as the older draws are) gives first draws for neighbouring days that step round the unit interval by a constant, which is exactly a schedule. `mixed` hashes its keys with FNV-1a (32 bits, exact in doubles) and discards two draws; `tests/nature.luau` holds the first draws of 200 days even (mean within 0.03 of 0.5) and unrelated day to day (lag-one correlation under 0.08), against the linear mix's two distinct steps.

**1. Movement costs energy** (`NeedsModel.game.moveCost` = { walkPerS 0.0004, linePerS 0.0002, jump 0.002 }, `NeedsModel.move`). Paid from the reserve at once, per second of action time (the fixed tick's: slow motion slows it, the garden's slow clock does not; it is the legs, not the day): walking on a surface, letting down or reeling in on the line, and each jump or hop once as it leaves the surface (`Nature.moved` reads the motor: a drop off an edge is not a jump, a seized spider does nothing). Standing, hanging still and resting cost nothing extra: the sit-and-wait economy. `useGameRules` sets a flag (`moveCosts`), not a copy of the numbers. A cost the empty reserve cannot pay is starving time at the awake rate, so a restless starving spider starves sooner. The numbers against ROBLOX-102's drain of 0.773 a day (11 waking hours, 825 s of play on the sill), a house fly being 0.35: **a still day 0.773** (2.2 house flies); **a typical day** (a third of the waking time walking, 275 s; 30 jumps; a minute on the line) 0.773 + 0.110 + 0.060 + 0.012 = **0.955** (2.7 house flies); **a restless day** (two thirds walking, 550 s; 100 jumps; three minutes on the line) 0.773 + 0.220 + 0.200 + 0.036 = **1.229** (3.5 house flies). A careful hunter keeps up on three flies a day; a restless one needs a fourth.

**2. Death loses the day.** A restore already brings back the rest's meals, growth and sightings and nothing since; what changes is that the end card says so. In Normal the card's last line is now `LifeText.DAY_LOST`: "In Normal a death loses the day: back at your last rest, the meals, the growth and what you saw since then are gone (the notebook keeps its pages). Another spider is a new life from the start." (`LifeText.NO_REST` when there is no rest to go back to). The choices are as they were, and they are the two ways on: "Back to your last rest" (when a rest exists) and "Another spider" (a new life), with the notebook to read; Easy and Free play never end a life, so they never see the card. The night gets its own card (below).

**3. Threats that linger.** The wasp (`Wasp.game.nature` = { shiftH 1.0, visit { 0.6, 1.5 }, lingerChance 0.08, linger { 3.0, 5.0 } }, `WaspDirector.nature`, set by the client when the rules are not gentle): each day's two windows slide by up to an hour either way (`dayWindows`, a draw per day and window; the planned hours, the two-hour gap and nothing after 17:30 hold), each visit searches 0.6 to 1.5 times its 72 s (43 to 108 s, about 1.05 on average), and one visit in about twelve (one every six days or so at two a day) lingers three to five times as long, 3.6 to 6 minutes on the sill: the "a wasp won't go away and I'm starving" visit. The draws are the seed's, the day's and the visit's number's, which the wasp's record already keeps, so a return to a rest meets the same visits and the record gains nothing. The rival (`RivalRules.camp` = { chance 0.15, seconds { 60, 180 }, four spots }, `RivalDrive.campLeg`): one patrol leg in about seven is a watch from one of four places on the floor round the crack's mouth (each outside the keep-out, two to five studs from the door, checked on the sill's real faces), held 60 to 180 s before it patrols on; it still sees, stalks and lunges from there, never enters the keep-out, and its watch counts down while the spider waits in the crack. The leg's draw is keyed by the leg number the rival's record keeps; the seconds left (`camp`) are an optional field of the record.

**4. Mating can fail** (`MatingRules.game` = { on, base 0.9, energyLow 0.35, energyFull 0.8, conditionFloor 0.3, breakCost 0.35, farCost 0.3, awayCost 0.5, longFloor 0.6, fairAt 0.85, cutChance 0.35, cutLevel { 0.35, 0.85 }, cutWu 10 }). When the meter fills the mate decides by a draw of its own (seed, mate, visit, courts so far): the chance is base x the spider's condition x the court's quality. Condition is the reserve from 0 at 0.35 to 1 at 0.8 (never under 0.3). Quality is kept tick by tick while the court goes on (`Mate.track`: its seconds, the seconds courting well, the distance over those, the seconds near but not facing, the breaks): 1 / (1 + 0.35 x breaks), times up to 0.3 off for courting from the edge of range rather than where the mate stands, times up to 0.5 off for the share of the court spent looking away, times a court that took over 1.5 times its eight seconds losing in proportion down to 0.6. So a still, close, facing court by a well-fed spider is accepted 0.9 of the time; at a reserve of 0.6 (the least a visitor comes for) 0.5; with two breaks, well fed, 0.53; thin and halting, 0.29. Refused, the mate walks off for the day (`reason` "rejected") and may come again the next morning; the words (`MateText`) say what weighed most: "thin" (the condition), "clumsy" (the court: "the courtship faltered"), or, when both were 0.85 or better, "choosy" ("Not every female takes a male"), so a good court refused by the base's chance is never blamed on the player. And on a third of visits the rival is set to cut in when the meter passes a drawn level (0.35 to 0.85): if it is free on the sill within ten studs of the mate then, the mate flees for the day ("The other spider pushed in, and the visitor fled the sill for today."). An eaten rival, or one the wasp took, cuts in on nothing. The chain after an accepted court (the pair, the sac, the guard days, the hatching, carrying on) is the old one. The mating cycle counts `courts`, `rejections` and `cut_ins`; the mate keeps its court's record and the cut level while a visit goes on; all optional. The notebook's "Eggs, silk and wind" page now says so in its "In this game" line (through `tools/gen_notebook_pages.py`: "In Normal the mate decides when the courtship is full: a thin spider, or a court that broke off, stood far or looked away, is often refused, and a rival may cut in.").

**5. Home before dark** (`Nature.game`: passes { 20.5-23.5, 23.5-26.5, 26.5-29.5 }, foundChance 0.25, coldUntilH 10, coldSpeed 0.6). Out of every retreat after dark anywhere but the sill (the outer sill, the walls and the bed: the sill behind the glass is the nursery), in Normal, the ground hunters pass three times a night, one at a drawn hour in each window; a spider exposed when one passes is found (`LifeSession.endLife("night")`: the card "The night found the spider." / "Out on the ground after dark, something that hunts there found it.", then Normal's lost-day line) or, if the draw spares it, has a cold night: "A cold night in the open, and something passed close by. You are stiff and slow until the morning warms." and walks at 0.6 of its speed until 10:00 the next day (`life.cold_day`, an optional field of the record). Each pass finds one time in four, so a whole night in the open is survived 0.42 of the time (0.75 cubed), and a spider home between the first pass and the second survives 0.75 of the time. The hours and draws are pure functions of the seed and the night, so a return to a rest never rerolls them; the count of passes is never saved and starts from the restored hour (the passes behind it are behind it: the spider was in its retreat for them). Dusk is told by the light alone: no notice comes before a pass.

**6. Cover is real** (`Nature.game` coverWu 6, covered 0.5, still 0.5, openMoving 2.0; `Nature.cover`). Each tick the client casts straight up from the spider (6 studs: a leaf, or anything solid, overhead) and hands the predators a factor on their notice: halved under cover, halved when still (so a quarter still under a leaf), doubled moving in the open. The wasp takes it with the spider (`spider.cover`, used only by a director told `nature`), the rival in its snapshot (`noticeScale`); their own notice rules are as they were, and in the gentle modes the factor is not handed in at all.

**The client.** No new top-level local: `M.Nature` and `M.nature` (what moved last tick, the night's count, the cover factor, the up-ray) in the table that already holds such things. The fixed tick pays for movement after the motor, hands the cover factor to the wasp and the rival, sets the wasp's and the rival drive's new rules when the rules are not gentle, hands the mate's tick `nature` and the rival's target, and after the life's step draws the night and applies the cold to the speed factor; a return to a rest and a fresh life clear the night's count. The end card's words gain the night's and Normal's last line. `CONTENT_REVISION`, `SCHEMA_VERSION` 3 and `WorldMap.REVISION` are unchanged: every new field is optional, absent in every record before, and checked when present (`Checkpoint`, `Mate.valid`, `Mating.problems`, `Rival.valid`, `Nature.validCold`).

**Pins that moved with counts.** New suite `tests/nature.luau` (28,889: the stream; movement's numbers, the three days, Godot's and the gentle models paying nothing, the motor's walk, hop and line read; cover's factors and the up-ray under the west leaf, on the open floor and on top of the leaf; the wasp's windows over 400 days and its visits over thousands, a lingering visit waited out from the crack and restored the same; the rival's watch places on the sill's faces outside the keep-out, its legs, its countdown and restore; courtship's condition, quality and odds, 300 seeded courts each at three conditions, the refusal and its words, the cut-in and the rival that cannot; the night's hours and draws, found, spared, the retreat, the sill, a restore that meets the same night; the end of a life by night and never in the gentle modes; the checkpoint's new fields kept, refused when bad, and old records restoring). New client suite `tests/nature_client.luau` (6, through the real client on the folders the server builds: walking's cost, the cover factor, a night in the bed that ends the life with its own card and Normal's words, a night that spares and leaves the cold until ten, the sill's night that is nothing, and Free play without any of it). `tests/client_mating.luau` 28 to 29: the chain from the courtship to the second generation now runs with `MatingRules.game.on` set off (it follows the chain after a mating; with it on, seed 914's draw refused that court, "choosy"), and a new check courts a thin spider in Normal through the client: refused on a full meter, counted, the words, the mate gone for the day, the cycle waiting. `tests/run.py` bundles `Nature` and lists both new suites (`nature_client` among the client suites). All 41 suites: 346,504 checks, nothing failed (317,608 in 39 at v35: ROBLOX-105's 317,604 and the self-test's 4); every golden and every other suite unchanged (the checkpoint suite's printed rest size 17,883 to 17,884 characters, the state suite's early rest 17,822 to 17,823). Strict analyze clean; 155 of 155 modules compile (SpiderClient under the 200-local limit).

Not run in Studio: whether walking's cost is felt (the bar's fall while walking is small tick to tick: about 2.7 flies a day for a typical day against 2.2 still), whether the lingering wasp reads as pinned rather than broken, the rival standing by the crack's mouth on the real sill (it must not look stuck), the courtship's refusal words and the mate walking off, the rival cutting in, the night card and the cold morning's slow walk on a television, the up-ray under the real leaves (the plants' undersides and the bed's leaves: a ray that misses a thin leaf is open sky), and the end card's longer last line at the large text size. Previews not made (none needed: no geometry or drawing changed). Out of scope, still to come: the footfall and the evening section's other rare things, a sac lost to a lingering predator, several rivals, the centipede at the stone and the ambush spiders, and the notebook's page on how a wasp sees.
### ROBLOX-107 - The map fills: the line fixed in the engine, the bushes beside the bed and the wall west of the window with its drainpipe (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-20)

Map plan regions 6 and the west half of 5 (`docs/WORLD_MAP_PLAN.md`), after a fix to the one check the v35 self-test failed in real Studio. The sill is untouched; Godot's defaults are untouched (`Species`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, perception); `WorldMap.REVISION` stays `world-2` (no box of any region before moved), `CONTENT_REVISION` and `SCHEMA_VERSION` as they were.

**The line: what went wrong in Studio, and how sure.** The self-test (ROBLOX-104) ran in real Studio and failed one check: `FAIL the line: left the line 2.0 s into the let-down, at (2.00, -2.70, -24.07)`. In the box world the same let-down passes at 0.752 studs a second. The cause, as far as the CLI can show it: the house_wall's brick is one box whose top (y -3) lies under the outer sill's slab, and its face is at z -24; the line hangs from the slab's front edge almost plumb against that face. The real engine let the body sit 0.07 from the brick, inside its 0.31 skin (the sweep is `Spherecast`, which does not see a part the sphere starts in, and a sweep that begins within a hair of a face it is nearly tangent to can miss it; nothing then pushed the body back out). Lowering on, the sweep down met the rounded edge of the wall box's covered top a few hundredths under y -2.7, with a normal steep enough (y about 0.97) to pass the let-down's landing rule (an attachable surface, facing up, below the anchor), and the spider "landed" on a sliver of brick no one can see: off the line. The box world reproduces it: `tests/scenarios.luau`'s new case puts the body where Studio had it two seconds into the let-down and lowers on; without the fix it leaves the line 23 ticks later at (2.00, -2.69, -24.05), against Studio's (2.00, -2.70, -24.07). That the landing on the covered edge is the failure is as sure as the CLI can make it (the place, the time and the mode all match). Why the real engine let the body within 0.07 of the face is inferred, not seen: the shim's sweeps are exact, so the case emulates the engine's tangent miss with a slop on the shim's "starts inside" rule (`BoxWorld.spherecastSlop`, 0.011, set for that case alone). Only the next Studio run can confirm it.

The fix, in `Movement/SilkController` (the game's controller, not a golden): (1) **a let-down lands only on ground under the body** (`Silk.landable`, `Silk.groundUnder`): besides the old rule, a ray straight down from the body's centre must meet the part the sweep touched within the legs' reach (height plus 0.35); a covered edge beside a face has nothing under the body, so the body slides on down the line. (2) **The skin is kept by overlap as well as by the sweep** (`Silk.unstick`, after each move on the line): `GetPartBoundsInRadius` over the world folder at the skin's radius, and the body pushed out of any block part it is inside its skin of, by the shortest way out from that box's nearest point (never through it: a centre already inside a box is left alone), the velocity into it taken away; a box it is touching that it could land on by the rule above is a landing, as the sweep's contact would have been. With the box world's exact sweeps the body is never inside its skin, so nothing changes there: the 18 earlier BOXWORLD checks pass as they did, the ten recovery scenarios to the same tick. The new case (BOXWORLD 18 to 19) holds three things: from Studio's place, six seconds lowered with the body never nearer the brick than 0.3 (it keeps 0.310); the sweep beside the face does meet the covered top with a steep normal, and there is no ground under a body there while there is over the soil; and with the engine's miss emulated over the whole let-down from the edge, the skin kept (0.310), the rate the game's (0.6 to 0.9 studs a second), and the line ending on the bed's soil after 118.9 s.

**The 129 ms hitch** (one frame in 285 in the sill's frame-time sample): no obvious cause found in the code. The sample starts straight after the rest-and-return check (a reset, a checkpoint load and the spider put back on the sill from the bed, which swaps the region and the prey directors), so a garbage collection or a streaming step on that first second is the likeliest; not chased.

**`wall_west` and `wall_west_upper`** (`World/Garden.WEST`, `westPieces`, `westUpperPieces`): the back wall west of the house_wall, face z -24, x -120 to -40 (not to -500 yet), split at y 0 as house_wall and wall_upper are (one region from the lawn to the eaves would not be whole inside the target radius from its foot). Below: the brick (one box, its foot on the litter at -90.4) and the drainpipe's foot: a 7-stud square column at x -50, 1.2 off the brick, standing on the litter (y -90) up to y 0, with its lower bracket at y -40 (a tie from the pipe's back to the brick and a collar round the pipe, one stud proud): 6 boxes. Above: the brick to the eaves, the pipe on up to the swan neck at y 92, the neck out under the gutter (z -61), the outlet up into the gutter's floor, the upper bracket at y 60 (brackets every 100 studs), and the soffit, fascia, gutter (floor, lip and a west end cap) and eave course on west of wall_upper's, which keeps its own end cap at x -41..-40 as the joint: 14 boxes. The pipe is the second way up to the gutter, all of it walkable boxes. The look (93 at full, 59 at low below; 88 and 71 above): the brick courses on the house_wall's and wall_upper's own grid (the bond carries across x -40) over a recessed mortar bed, the pipe as a cylinder over each run, the neck and its bend, the outlet, the brackets, a socket at the foot; at the full tier the damp patch at the drain's foot (darker litter and brick, moss), algae along the foot, salt bloom, lichen, an old cobweb behind the pipe, the gutter's rolled lip with leaves and moss in it, dirt streaks under the gutter. Seams: to the house_wall along x -40 (to y 0), to wall_upper along x -40 from y 0 to y 60 only (a seam line 110 long cannot have its far end's boxes inside the radius always sent from its near end; above y 60 the rest of wall_upper is inside the target radius, the streaming case says), between the two at y 0, and the foot to the litter. No retreat; the sill's pace (1.0). `wall_upper`'s look stops drawing the house's back wall, eaves and gutter west of x -40 where these are now walkable (it draws from x -120 out), and its drawn downpipe, two brackets and shoe are gone (154/59 to 150/58).

**`shrub_west`** (`World/Garden.SHRUB`, `shrubPieces`; `WorldMap.SHRUB_CHUNKS`): the strip from the bed's west edge (x -25) and the house's wall (z -24) out to x -500 and z -200, on a mat of deep leaf litter whose top is the bed's soil's (y -90, 2 deep), in ten chunks of 95 by 88 (five columns westward, each split at z -112), `shrub_west_a` to `_j`, each whole inside the target radius from anywhere on it (farthest box 97 to 112 studs, 160 the radius) and 42 or 43 boxes (60 the nav graph's ceiling). Each chunk has two bushes, box and privet, 44 to 54 studs tall: three stems from the litter, each with two branches out at 45 and 78 per cent of its height, a leaf pad (its top walkable) on each branch's end and a larger one on each stem's top; the first bush stands over the chunk's retreat, the gap under a curled dead leaf lying on two twigs (4.8 by 1.4 by 5.2, as the bed's stone's; `shrub_west_x_litter`, its mouth toward the house, the spawn in front of it), its stems clear of the leaf and its way in; three heaps of litter; every box inside its chunk, none overlapping another, nothing on the way in or over the drainpipe's foot. Seams: across the split, to the next column, and the first chunk to the bed's west edge, the house_wall's foot west of the bed and wall_west's foot, 23 in all. The garden's pace (0.33). The look (260 at full, 80 at low, every chunk exactly at its budget): the twigs, each pad as a hanging leaf cluster with a sunlit mat on top (the clusters cast shadows: the shade under a bush is real), a skirt of foliage on the litter, the bush's outer mass (three rings of leaf clusters from the litter up round the frame, 14 a bush, two at low), the dead leaf and its twigs, the heaps; at the full tier the crown, privet flowers, leaves lying on the pads, damp patches; then the litter itself to the budget. The view's four drawn bushes that stood in the strip (x -76, -150, -110, -215) moved out along the lawn's west edge and the hedge line, the same sizes and blooms (`Data/Flora`; `tests/world.luau` already holds every drawn bush clear of every walkable region).

**Life in the litter** (on W3's pattern): four woodlice a chunk from its seed (`Garden.bugs`), hiding at the mouth and back of the gap under the dead leaf and in the litter the low tier keeps; in `shrub_west_a`, which holds the drainpipe's foot, a hide spot at the drain and the first two woodlice begin on the damp in front of it (`Garden.drainFoot`; the pipe's foot is a box the life on that litter walks round, `Garden.standingFrom`, also read by `PreyWorld`'s woodlice and ambient bugs). Springtails, four a chunk (two at low), on twelve spots of litter round the hide spots, the bed's numbers (`Garden.BESTIARY.shrub`). No gnats, aphids or ambient bugs there; the ground beetle is a later threat. A chunk's woodlice carry its id (`shrub_west_a_louse_1`; `Garden.bugs` gives a `prefix`, `Prey/Woodlouse` puts it before the id): the full suite caught that a chunk beside the bed runs with the bed (its springtails are drawn from the bed's west edge, as the wall's moths are from the bed), and with plain `louse_1` ids the two regions' woodlice would have shared ids in the drawing; the bed's keep `louse_1`, so a kept rest's woodlice are the same ones. The wall west has no life of its own yet (the woodlice "at the drain" are the chunk's).

**Previewed** (`tools/preview/scene.py --world`, eight new views: `drainpipe`, `drain_foot`, `drain_neck`, `shrub_strip`, `shrub_chunk`, `shrub_leaf`, `shrub_under`, `house_west`). Looked at, and two faults fixed from them: the walkable bushes, twigs and flat pads alone, read as a stand of lollipops (standards on stems, which Nathan took out of the view in ROBLOX-103), so each bush got its outer mass of clusters and now reads as a dense bush to the ground; and the drawn shoe at the pipe's foot was a box 11 studs across standing out past the 7-stud walkable column over the damp where the woodlice begin, now a socket a little proud of the column. Seen right: the pipe from the litter to the swan neck, the neck and outlet into the gutter, the brackets, the strip of bushes along the wall with their shade, the dead leaf on its twigs in the shade under a bush, the house from the lawn with the walkable bushes behind the drawn ones. Shape and placement only, not Roblox's materials or light.

**Streaming and the self-test.** Each new region is whole inside the target radius from anywhere on it and the far side of each seam is there before it is crossed (`tests/worldmap.luau`'s streaming case over every region and seam); `WorldClient`'s readiness and the focus report hold for them. The self-test's region checks come from `WorldMap.regions()`, so it walks all 17 at their spawns now: 32 checks, not 20 (the CLI run's play time is still under its three minutes).

**The looks are persistent.** Every region's look is a persistent model (ROBLOX-95), always on the client, so the ten chunks' looks are there wherever the spider is: the looks outside the window are 3,647 parts at full (870 before) and 1,215 at low (286). Each region is within its budget at both tiers; whether the total costs frame time in the real engine is for the Studio run (the self-test's frame-time lines), and making looks stream with their regions is a choice left for a later round.

**Pins that moved with counts.** `tests/scenarios.luau` (BOXWORLD) 18 to 19: the let-down past the wall, above; the shim's `Spherecast` gains the slop that case sets (0 otherwise). `tests/worldmap.luau` 346 to 1,154: seventeen regions in priority order with their numbers, the 23 new seams both ways and their streaming numbers, the union of the bounds (x -500..42, z -200..16), the drainpipe unbroken from the litter to the gutter's floor with brackets every 100 studs, the gutter on west at wall_upper's floor, every chunk's boxes on its litter inside it (at most 60), two bushes with their pads, the dead leaf on its twigs over the gap, the way in open, and the new keep-outs and budgets. `tests/rivalnav.luau` 364 to 518: a route graph for each new region (its loops over the map). `tests/world.luau` 301 to 337: the World folder's 17 region folders (was 5), the 16 looks exactly at their budgets (was 4), the boxes outside 486 (was 37), and the drawn house's back wall meeting wall_west's brick at x -120 (was the house_wall's at -40). `tests/checkpoint.luau` 469 to 477: a rest under the dead leaf of `shrub_west_a` and of `shrub_west_j` recorded, accepted, put back and refused outside its chunk. `tests/garden_life.luau` 255,758 to 256,701: one new case (every chunk's four woodlice from its seed with their ids, their hide spots on its litter, the two at the drain's foot, twenty seconds of walking never inside a twig or the pipe or off the chunk; its four springtails on twelve spots of litter, at both tiers; no gnats, aphids or ambient bugs), and three counts in W3's case "the world's prey" moved on purpose: back in the bed by its west edge the bushes' first chunk runs too, so eight directors have been made, not seven, and the gate's world has made three, then six (not two, then five). `tests/world_client.luau` 29 unchanged, one check widened: in the bed by its west edge the drawn prey of the garden may be `shrub_west_a`'s springtails too (the full suite's first run failed there, which is how the shared woodlouse ids were found). `tests/selftest.luau` 4 unchanged: the run walks 32 checks to END in 64 s of play (49 before). All 39 suites: 319,558 checks, nothing failed (317,608 at HEAD: ROBLOX-105's 317,604 and ROBLOX-104's SELFTEST 4); every golden and every other suite unchanged. Strict analyze clean; 154 of 154 modules compile. The suite is slower: `tests/world_client.luau` took about three hours here (two cores shared), because the shim's every ray walks every part in the workspace and there are some 3,000 more of them (the ten chunks' looks most of all).

Not run in Studio: all of it. Whether the line's fix is the right one (the self-test's "the line" check and a let-down by hand from the outer sill's edge to the bed), whether `GetPartBoundsInRadius` and the push-out behave on the real engine's parts as on the shim's boxes, the drainpipe climbed from the litter to the gutter (the brackets' collars and the neck's underside are the hard parts for the real motor), the bushes from inside with the real camera (the camera inside a cluster, the shade), the dead leaf's gap as a rest, the woodlice on the damp at the drain, the frame time with ten chunks' looks always present, the part counts on a phone. Out of scope: the wall on west to x -500 and `wall_east` with the back door, the ground beetle, the hedge beyond z -200.

### ROBLOX-108 - Performance for a real release: the looks made near the spider, the rest's hitch, an honest low tier, and fast tests (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-20)

For a release on phones and the PlayStation. Nothing a player does or sees up close changes: Godot's defaults and every golden hold (`Species`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, perception); no box of any region moved (`WorldMap.REVISION` stays `world-2`); no save field, `CONTENT_REVISION` or `SCHEMA_VERSION` changed; the sill's Decor is as it was (persistent, the home, 1,253 parts at full and 355 at low); the lighting is untouched.

**The looks are made on the client, near the spider** (`World/LookPlan`, new and pure; `Client/LookStream`, new; `Client/WorldClient.streamLooks` and `update`; `Server/Main`). Since ROBLOX-107 every region's drawn look was a persistent model on every client wherever the spider was: 3,647 parts outside the window at the full tier, 1,215 at the low. Two ways were open: let the engine stream them (non-persistent models, `ModelStreamingMode.Atomic` a look, under instance streaming) or have each client build and destroy them from the seeded generators. The client builds them, for four reasons: a look is a pure function of its region's seed (`Region.decor`), so the client's parts are the server's part for part and nothing is replicated; the engine streams out only under memory pressure by default (`StreamOutBehavior`), so streaming would not have kept a budget at all, and when and what it sends cannot be tested here; a phone never builds the full tier's detail only to destroy it (`Client/WorldDetail` did that to every look); and the plan is a pure function the CLI tests ask exactly as a client does. The server no longer builds the looks; it hides every piece a look stands in for, as it did, and a client whose look fails shows its own copies of those pieces with a warning (as the server's best effort did).
The plan at a place, for a tier: (1) the region the spider is in and every region it meets, at the tier's detail, always; (2) the house's shell, wherever it is not drawn already, at a far detail: per wall kind a handful of big records of its low look (`LookPlan.FAR`: the house_wall's mortar bed and the three beds round the window; wall_upper's mortar bed, soffit, fascia, the roof's slope, the house's back wall either side and its eaves; wall_west's mortar bed and pipe; wall_west_upper's mortar bed, soffit, pipe and swan neck), the mortar beds drawn in the bricks' first colour and material, as the drawn house beyond the walkable walls always is, so from the far end of the garden the house has no hole where a wall's bricks would be (its boxes are invisible under its drawing) and the drainpipe is still there; (3) the other regions nearest first within 260 studs (160 at low), each at the tier's detail if it fits the budget, else at the full tier the low detail if that fits. A region held is reckoned 30 studs nearer and kept to the radius plus 30, so a spider on the edge does not make and destroy the same look over and over. Budget: 1,500 parts at full and 500 at low (`LookPlan.BUDGET`); the region and its neighbours alone are at most 1,473 and 487 (the first bushes' chunk: itself, the bed, the house_wall, the wall west and the two chunks beside it), so the budget is always met with every neighbour at the tier's detail. At the twelve places `tests/worldmap.luau` pins (the sill's spawn, the outer sill, halfway down the wall, the wall's foot, the bed's stone, the gutter, the wall west, the drainpipe's swan neck, the bushes by the bed and the house, where four chunks meet, the bushes' middle and their far corner) the plan is 1,467 to 1,490 parts at full and 338 to 499 at low; a walk of about 640 studs from the sill to the far corner, planned every two studs, never holds more than 1,489 (499 at low) and makes, changes or drops a look 38 times (27 at low).
The client follows the plan (`LookStream`): a look is a Model per region in the client's own `WorldDecor` folder (the name the server's had) in up to three sets, `Base` (the records the low tier keeps), `Full` (the full tier's only) and `Far`; full is Base and Full, low is Base, far is Far, so a region coming near adds its Full set to the Base it has and one going away destroys it. A set is built into a folder parented only when it is whole, and what it replaces (a Far under a new Base) is destroyed in the same frame, so a look is never missing where the plan had one nor doubled from one frame to the next. The plan is asked again when the spider has moved 2 studs or changed region; at most 120 parts are made in a frame, nearest first, except that a region the spider is in or beside with nothing drawn (the first frame, a return to a rest far away) is made whole at once. A new client at the bed's stone makes 388 parts its first frame (the bed, the wall and the first bushes' base looks, and 120 more) and holds the whole plan, 1,483 parts, after 11 frames. The self-test's part-count check prints one more INFO line: the looks held, planned, made and destroyed.

**The hitch.** The self-test's one 129 ms frame on the sill (ROBLOX-107 left it unchased) was found by timing every frame of `tests/selftest.luau`'s run with `os.clock` and the client's modules wrapped: in the shim the worst frame of the 3,840 is the one a rest begins (the rest-and-checkpoint check, just before the sill's frame sample), 886 ms there against a 35 ms mean, 829 ms of it `CheckpointStore.save` (`LifeLoop.makeCheckpoint`): a rest's record after a visit to the garden is about 300,000 characters (1,383 objects: every region's prey with each fly's senses and flight), and a save encoded it to canonical JSON four times and summed it twice (the entry's checksum, the in-memory store's storability and size check on the temporary key and on the current one, the read back), and on later saves six times and three. Changed, with every stored byte and every refusal as it was: `Json.canonical` spells the path a problem names only when there is a problem (it built a new string for every value), skips the escaping pass for a string that needs none, and `Json.checksum` reads a block of bytes at a time with the multiply written out (the same sums); `CheckpointStore.save` takes what it read back as good when its text is the record's text word for word (then its checksum and its checks are the record's, already passed), and the current entry likewise when it is word for word the one it put in place last; `MemoryDataStore.SetAsync` does not encode again a table it has written before when it is unchanged, value for value, against the store's own copy (never handed out). A first save now encodes three times and sums once, a later one five and once. The same frame in the shim: 886 ms to 353 ms (the save itself 829 to 321 ms; `Json.canonical` four calls to three). The rest of the frame path was looked at and left: the rigs', the flies' and the bugs' `BulkMoveTo` lists are reused (`table.clear`, ROBLOX-93), no per-frame table churn of size was found, the prey directors and their bodies are made when a region first comes whole (a few dozen parts), and the looks' first sight is now spread as above. Not measured in the real engine: whether the save's frame is now under 60 ms in Studio, and whether a GC step lands on it, is for the next self-test (its sill frame sample follows the rest).

**The low tier, honestly.** Looked at for everything drawn: the spider 693 parts at full and 265 at low; the sill's Decor 1,253 and 355; the looks now built at the tier (at most 500 parts at low, no full-only part ever made); the bestiary's springtail, leafhopper, moth and crane fly lighter at low (ROBLOX-101), the ambient four (ROBLOX-105), the rival always at low, the mate's brood capped at low. Two were not lighter: the house fly (39 parts at both) is now 29 at low (no bristles, no iridescent films over the wings: neither shows at a fly's size on a phone; four flies on the sill are 40 parts fewer to place every frame), and the wasp (about 60 parts, one at a time, for a visit) stays as it is, as do the gnat (22), the aphid (15) and the woodlouse (24), small bodies whose every part is a leg, a wing or a segment the motion turns. **A PlayStation is the full tier** (`Detail.choose`'s third argument, `GuiService:IsTenFootInterface()` in the client): a console is not a phone, Nathan plays on a PlayStation and the look was judged on that television; a console has no touch screen, so the touch rule already gave it full, and now a console that reports touch (a controller's touch pad) is never taken for a phone. The Workspace attribute `Detail` still wins either way. Not measured: the frame time on a PlayStation 4 (the slowest console Roblox runs on) with the full tier; if it struggles, `Detail = "low"` on that device is the lever, and a console rule by model is not possible (the engine does not say which console).

**Fast tests** (`tests/shim.luau`, `tests/run.py`). The shim's raycasts, sphere casts and part queries walked every part of the workspace and every root's ancestry for every query: with the garden's looks in the workspace, `tests/world_client.luau` took about three hours. Now: the queryable parts in the workspace's order are kept until a queryable part (or a folder holding one) is added, removed or has its `CanQuery` changed (the shim's `Parent` and `CanQuery` writes say so); each filter's list is kept until then; a part's box follows it when it moves (its `CFrame`, `Position` or `Size` written); a grid of 16-stud cells over each filter's list gives a short query only the boxes in the cells it touches (a box over more than 64 cells, or one that has moved, is tested by every query; a query over more than 64 cells walks the list); and a box that cannot meet the query's bounds is skipped before the exact test. Every query is given every box that could meet it, in the order it always was, so every answer is the one the old loop gave: every suite's counts are unchanged but where checks were added, and the printed numbers of the suites that print them (the recovery scenarios' ticks, the descent's seconds, the self-test's frames, the bestiary's feel) are the same to the digit. The drawn rigs' cost was most of what was left: `CFrame` times a vector or a frame is written out by component in the same order (the same bits, without four Vector3s in between). Times, the same two shared cores, before and after: `checkpoint.luau` 1,717 s to 10, `scenarios.luau` 301 to 4, `client_smoke.luau` 232 to 56, `client_ant.luau` 108 to 17, `client_rival.luau` 256 to 65, `client_mating.luau` 1,044 to 116, `look.luau` 191 to 101, `idle.luau` 206 to 78, `inspect.luau` 479 to 98, `state_client.luau` 195 to 49, `state.luau` 104 to 23, `notebook.luau` 89 to 22, `settings.luau` 55 to 11, `world_client.luau` about three hours (ROBLOX-107's run; this round's run of the old tree was still in `garden_life.luau` after half an hour when the round closed) to 230 s, `garden_life.luau` 18 s, `selftest.luau` 49 s, `nature_client.luau` 24 s. The whole suite: the first 36 suites took 85 minutes before (the five that follow, `world_client.luau` among them, some hours more) and the whole of the 41 takes about 18 minutes now (1,059 s), inside the round's target of 45.

**Pins that moved, with counts.** `tests/worldmap.luau` 1,154 to 1,645: one new case (every look's full and low counts against its budget and the 3,647 and 1,215 of all at once; the far looks, their parts and their brick; at each of the twelve places at both tiers the plan within the budget and adding up, the region at the tier's detail, each neighbour at it, the house's four wall regions drawn, every level allowed, the same plan asked again; the hysteresis on a two-region map; the walk at both tiers). `tests/world_client.luau` 29 to 30: one new case (the real client's looks in six regions within the budget and as planned, its region and neighbours at full; a new stream at the bed spread over frames with its region and neighbours whole the first frame; a low-tier stream at the twelve places within 500 with no full-only part; a look that fails warned about once, its bricks shown, the others drawn). `tests/checkpoint.luau` 477 to 485: one new case (the checksum against a one-byte FNV-1a across the block's edges; the canonical text and a problem's path as they were; a first save encodes three times and sums once, a later five and once; the current rest and its backup kept as before; a current rest changed in the store still set aside). `tests/flies.luau` 286 to 289: the house fly at low (29 parts, what it keeps and leaves out, it walks and flies). `tests/world.luau` 337 to 338: a console is the full tier. `tests/client_ant.luau` 3, unchanged in count: the suite now waits out the saving client's 1.5 s of real clock after the client loads, because with the faster shim its "could not reach its saving service" toast fell in the middle of a check of another toast (two of three failed once in the round's full run; they pass). `tests/selftest.luau` 4: one more INFO line, its 3,840 frames and every other line as before. Every other suite unchanged, and its printed output the same line for line (compared with this round's run of the old tree for the 36 suites it finished, with ROBLOX-107's logs for `world_client`, `garden_life` and `selftest`), but for the rest's size that `checkpoint.luau` and `state.luau` print, which moves by a character from run to run (the CPU-time instruments in the record), as it always has. All 41 suites: 348,958 checks, nothing failed (348,454 at HEAD: ROBLOX-107's 319,558, ROBLOX-106's NATURE 28,889 and NATURE_CLIENT 6, and CLIENT_MATING 29). Strict types clean; 157 of 157 modules compile (the client under the 200-local limit: the looks hang off `M.World`, the console test is inline).

**Previewed** (`tools/preview/scene.py --world`, a new view `far_house` and a new `--looks-at x,y,z`, which draws the looks a client whose spider is there holds instead of every look at once): `far_house`, from the far corner of the bushes back along the house, with every look and with that client's, at both tiers. With every look: the brick courses, the kitchen window, the drainpipe, the bushes along the wall to the bed. With the client's: the house whole, brick to the eaves under its roof and gutter; the first try had no drainpipe (its boxes are hidden under its drawing, and the far look had only the walls), so the pipe and its swan neck went into the far looks and the second shows it. Not there from far off, by design: the kitchen window, the back door, the brick courses (a plain brick-coloured wall), and the bushes more than about 2.6 m along the wall (their litter stays). At the low tier the near bushes read as lollipops (two clusters a bush: ROBLOX-107's low look, not this round's). `window` from the sill with the sill's client's looks: as before. The previewer shades like Godot, not Roblox; Roblox's Atmosphere will wash the far house more than this.

Not run in Studio: all of it. The looks made on a real client (the first frame's 388 parts and the spread, whether any pop-in shows at the radius or when a far wall's bricks come in, the far walls' brick colour against the near bricks in Roblox's light and Atmosphere), the server without the looks (its start time and memory, a join), the save's frame in Studio, the house fly at low on a phone, the full tier on the PlayStation 4 and 5. The self-test's new INFO line says how many look parts a client holds; its frame-time lines (the bed's and the sill's after a rest) are the measures to compare with v35's.
### ROBLOX-109 - The first minute: a splash, a title card, the intro of a new life, the cards in one typeface, and the store page's plan (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-20)

Nathan: the game "needs a good splash page and intro or something". It opened on Roblox's grey loading screen and then on a plain card over the sill ("The Windowsill / A small life on a windowsill." and eleven buttons, the saving notes among them). The first minute is now the game's own, in its voice and without hand holding: a splash while the world streams in, the start menu as a title card over a slow camera in the garden, a short intro the first time a life begins, and the death and ending cards in the same typeface. The lighting is as it was (the title card shows the morning the life clock starts at, 09:00); Godot's defaults, the goldens, the world, the saves' schema and `CONTENT_REVISION` are untouched.

**The words and the typeface** (`Shared/UI/TitleText`, new). Every word of the first minute is there: the title and its line (kept: "A small life on a windowsill."), the start menu's choices on the title card and in a life (Continue is one word on the title card and "Continue from last rest" beside Resume), a controller's line in its own names ("D-pad: move    Cross: choose    Circle: back" on a PlayStation, A and B otherwise, from `ControlText`), the skip ("Space: skip", "Cross: skip", "Tap to skip") and the intro's three captions. The typeface is Merriweather, a serif Roblox ships as a font family (`rbxasset://fonts/families/Merriweather.json`): Light for the title, Italic for its line and the captions, Regular for the rest, in warm paper white (244, 238, 226) over a brown-black ground; each `FontFace` is set inside a pcall and a family Roblox could not find falls back to its default face on its own. The title's size follows the screen's width, `clamp(floor(width x 0.1), 30, 58)` pixels: 58 on a monitor, 36 at a phone's 360 (one line at both).

**The splash** (`First/SplashScreen` and `First/Splash.client`, new; `default.project.json` maps `src/First` to ReplicatedFirst; the test place has none). It removes Roblox's loading screen at once and puts up a black field; the words and the drift are looked for each frame (they replicate after ReplicatedFirst); once the game says it has loaded the field fades over 2.5 s to a veil of 0.3 over the garden, the title comes up over 1.6 s, and the camera drifts (below). There is no bar: after 4 s of waiting a 9-pixel dot pulses in the bottom-right corner. When the client's start menu opens, the client sets the Player attribute `TitleReady` and the splash fades away over the title card in 1.2 s, never touching the camera again (the title card draws the same drift from the same clock, so the hand-over cannot jump). It cannot hang: each frame is one pass of a loop with nothing in it that waits; the words are looked for 10 s and their absence only means a dark field without words; a game that never says it has loaded is shown anyway at 30 s; at 45 s the splash goes whatever has happened and the plain start menu is what is left (the client opens it on its own as it always did); the whole run is in a pcall, an error anywhere takes the screen away and is written to Output once, and the script's own require is in a second pcall. The camera is only ever written (Scriptable, as the client's own camera sets it) and never handed back to Roblox's. The music cannot be the splash's (the soundscape is the client's): it rises under the title card instead, from the client's first frame (below).
