**The drift** (`Shared/Movement/TitleShots.drift`, new): the camera's place as a pure function of the game's clock (`time()`), so the splash and the title card, two scripts, put it in the same place on the same frame. Three keys eased between: (40, -82, -170) looking at (0, -25, -24), low over the lawn past the bed, the bushes and the window high in the wall, field of view 55; at 18 s (16, -20, -80) at (0, 0, -18), risen in front of the wall with the window in its deep reveal, 48; at 36 s (10, 6.5, -45) at (0, 2.5, -14), level with the outer sill looking in through the window boxes and the glass at the sill, 46; then it breathes there (1.6 studs side to side and 0.5 up and down, at 0.07 and 0.05 radians a second) for as long as the title card is up. At its fastest it moves 0.47 studs in a twentieth of a second (9 studs, 9 centimetres, a second, over a garden of metres); it never enters a box of any region.

**The title card** (`Client/CaughtPanel`, the "title" look; `Client/TitleCard`, the drift's frame; `Client/SpiderClient`'s `openMenu`). Before a life has begun this visit the start menu is a title card: the veil a gradient (0.15 at the top where the world is seen, 0.8 at the foot under the choices), the title and its line, the choices on a dark ground 50 pixels tall in a column at most 560 wide, centred in the screen, then the saving note in small italic and, for a controller, its line. The choices are Continue (when a rest exists), Continue the Easy life, Begin a new life, Begin a new life on Easy (once earned), Free play - gentle rules, Settings, and the Notebook once it has pages; the discovery notes and the text size, which are in Settings since ROBLOX-49, are no longer menu buttons. In a life the same menu is the card it was, Resume and Controls first. The keys are as before (the D-pad, the stick, W/S and the arrows move; A / Cross, Enter, Space or E choose; touch and the mouse click) and B / Circle now chooses a screen's back choice: "Keep this life" on the new life's confirmation, Resume in the life menu, nothing on the title card. The camera is the drift while a screen has the game and no life has begun (the start menu, a screen opened from it, the start's wait for the save); the music plays under it though a menu has the game, rising from silence over 8 s the first time (`Soundscape.setTitle`; nothing else plays). One fix the title card needed: a return to a rest (Continue) never said the life had begun (`session.started` stayed false: the drift would have held the camera and the life menu had no Resume after a Continue); `returnToCheckpoint` now sets it.

**The intro of a new life** (`Client/TitleCard`, `TitleShots.intro`). When a new life is begun from the start menu (New, Easy, or Free play with no rest of its own) and this save has never seen it, the spider is put just inside the crack facing out, the HUD goes, and the intro plays, about 14 s: from black (0.8 s) low across the sill toward the glass with the morning's light on the floor (0 to 3.8 s); low over the sill floor drawing in to the crack's mouth (3.8 to 7.4); a low shot at the mouth that follows the spider as it walks out, by the motor's own walk (from 7.4 s until it is past x -21.6, at the latest 11.5 s); then the view eases into the game's own camera behind the spider (12.2 to 13.6 s: the client renders its camera and the intro lerps toward it) and the controls are the player's, the controls card and the walking hint given their time afresh. Three captions in the notebook's voice: "A young bold jumping spider. Home is a silk-lined crack in the sill." (4.2 to 8.2 s), "It spins no web to catch food. It hunts with its eyes." (8.6 to 11.8 s), and "It has not eaten. It must eat today." (12.2 to 16.0 s, over the game; in the gentle rules "Hunger is gentler in these rules. It is never gone."). Each is backed by the notebook (the bold jumper, the retreat, hunting by sight) or the game's own numbers (a new spider's reserve, 0.55, lasts under a day at Normal's 0.612 a day). The skip: refused before 2 s; after, the jump key, Enter, E, A / Cross or a tap ("Space: skip" in a corner says so), and the spider is put where its walk ends and the game comes up from black over 0.45 s. Reduced Motion: each shot is held still on its middle frame and the shots cut, and so does the end. A screen opened over it (the life menu) pauses it; Resume, a new life or a rest end it. The intro is seen the moment it begins (a skipped one has been seen) and never plays again in that save: `intro_seen` in the progress record (`Persistence/SaveProgress`): optional, written only when true, refused when not a boolean, kept by a merge whichever side is newer, part of the client's change signature; no schema bump (a record without it reads as not seen). In Studio the Workspace attribute `IntroAlways` plays it for every new life from the menu, seen or not, for looking at it (nothing outside Studio reads it). While it holds, the notebook's watcher waits (the first page opens when the controls are the player's).

**The cards** (`Client/CaughtPanel`, the "card" look): the death and ending cards and the life menu in the same typeface and colours, their sizes, timing and words as they were (all in `WaspText`, `LifeText`, `MateText`).

**The store page** (`docs/STORE_PAGE.md`, new): the experience's description (923 characters, every claim in the game now), the icon's concept (the spider close at the crack's mouth, three-quarter front and low, no words) with a render of its composition, and five thumbnails with their cameras in the world's coordinates, the hour and how to take each in Studio.

**The self-test** (`Client/SelfTest`) gets one check, "intro", after the new life: the intro holding (from the start menu; begun through its hook where the save has seen it and in the test place), its skip refused at once and taken after 2.2 s, then the controls and the camera back and the spider standing out of the crack: 33 checks, not 32.

**Previewed** (`tools/preview/scene.py --world --title`, the frames from `TitleShots.views()`; and `store_icon`, `store_spider`). Looked at, and changed from them: the first drift began too close to the wall (a brick wall and a drainpipe filled the frame, no garden) and now begins low over the lawn with the bed's foot and the bushes in it; the first intro shot was low behind a pot rim with a stem across the glass and now looks across the sill's floor at the glass with the sun's patches on the floor. Seen right: the arrival looking in through the window boxes; the crack's mouth with its silk; the spider at the mouth, three-quarter, as the icon. The title card and two captions were mocked over those renders in Merriweather from Google Fonts (`tools/preview/title_mock.py`): the title card at 1280 by 720 and at a phone's 844 by 390, where the five choices and the small type fit. Shape, placement and layout only, not Roblox's materials, light or text rendering. The icon's composition is `docs/store/icon_preview.png`; the mocks are in `docs/store/`.

**Pins that moved with counts.** New suite `tests/title.luau` (16, among the client suites and in the default list): the words (the sizes, the choices, a controller's line in PlayStation's and Xbox's names, the skip, the captions' length); the drift (from its first key to its last, its sway, a field of view in range, never more than 0.6 studs in a twentieth of a second, never inside a box of any of the 17 regions over four minutes); the intro's timing, its camera (smooth inside a shot, never inside a box of the sill, the out shot on the spider) and Reduced Motion's still shots and cut; the splash run to its end through a host of the suite's own: ready (Roblox's screen removed, the drift on the camera at the clock, no write after TitleReady, gone after FADE_S), no words, no title card (gone at 45 s), a lookup that raises, a fault in a frame, a camera that refuses, no Players; its look (black, the veil's rest, the dot only on a wait, a going that only fades, the 30 s rule); CaughtPanel's two looks and the back button; `intro_seen` in the progress record; and through the real client outside the test place (the hooks taken through the self-test's door): the menu waiting for the save then the title card with TitleReady set, the camera on the drift, the music rising under it, the D-pad and B; the intro from Begin a new life (the spider in the crack, the HUD gone, the camera the intro's, the skip refused at 1 s and taken at 2.1 s, the spider put out, the HUD and the controls back); never twice; played through (the walk out, the three captions, the controls back at 13.6 s, the game's camera); Reduced Motion, a screen over it, Resume; Continue from the title card (a life that runs, the game's camera, Resume in the life menu); the death card's face and words. `tests/selftest.luau` 4 unchanged: the run walks 33 checks to END, the intro after the new life, in 66.9 s of play (64 before). `tests/run.py` bundles `TitleText`, `TitleShots`, `TitleCard` and `SplashScreen`, and its require rewrite knows `src/First`. The suites that drive the menu (`client_smoke` 25, `state_offline` 8, `state_client` 140, `notebook` 1,184, `settings` 1,151, `inspect` 37) pass unchanged: the test place has no title card and no intro, the buttons keep their names, and a new life begun through the menu's "New" in `client_smoke` starts an intro that the next check's `fresh` ends. All 42 suites: 348,470 checks, nothing failed (348,454 in 41 at HEAD: ROBLOX-107's 319,558 with ROBLOX-106's NATURE 28,889, NATURE_CLIENT 6 and CLIENT_MATING's 29th); every golden and every other suite unchanged. Strict analyze clean; 160 of 160 modules compile (SpiderClient at -O0, -O1 and -O2, under the 200-local limit: the client's new state is in its module table `M`).

Not run in Studio: all of it. The things only Studio can show: ReplicatedFirst's order (that the splash is up before anything else and that `RemoveDefaultLoadingScreen` leaves no grey frame), Merriweather's look and whether `Font.new` finds the family, the veil's gradient over a bright morning, the hand-over from the splash to the title card (the same frame of the drift from two scripts), the music's rise under the menu, the intro's camera in the real crack and its ease into the game's camera, the walk out of the crack on the real motor, the menu with a PlayStation controller and on a phone.


### ROBLOX-110 - Lock-on and the sidestep: the spider keeps its prey in its principal eyes and sidles round it, and the perception HUD can be turned off (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-20)

Nathan: "a lock on so you can track prey without the visual cues and the ability to side step". A jumping spider turns its body to keep a prey in its principal eyes and sidles round it, stalking; the player could only hold Focus at whatever the camera pointed at and walk relative to the view, and tracking meant reading the perception HUD's rings and labels. Now a press locks onto something the spider has seen, the camera and the body stay on it, the jump's aim is on it and the stick's left and right circle it at a creep; and the perception HUD can be switched off, the lock's only cue being four faint ticks. Godot's defaults, the goldens, the perception modules, the saves' schema, `CONTENT_REVISION` and `WorldMap.REVISION` are untouched.

**What can be locked, and which** (`Shared/Movement/LockOn`, new, pure). Only what the spider's own memory holds (`Perception/ObservationMemory`): a contact an inspection has placed (`hasPosition`), live, or lost with its last-seen mark still good. Never the world's list of things, so a target it has not seen, one only cued (a bearing without a place) or one hidden behind a box cannot be locked. A press takes the contact in the principal eyes now (live, inspected) nearest the middle of the view, within 20 degrees of the camera's centre ray; else the nearest placed contact within 12 studs and within 90 degrees of the spider's facing. Anything the senses give can be locked (a fly, the rival, the mate, the wasp, the test target): a lock is attention, not a promise of a meal. The woodlice, ladybirds, millipedes, snail and harvestmen are not shown to the senses (ROBLOX-105) and so cannot be locked; if Nathan wants them lockable they need percepts that are never food, which is a perception change of its own.

**What ends it.** A second press; Cancel when it has nothing else to cancel (an aim, a press, the line and a walk to the edge come first, as for Focus's toggle); the memory letting the contact go (sight lost for `memory_decay_s`, 6 s; the remembered spot looked at and found empty, which is what happens at once when the spider, turned to the mark, sees it gone: 0.57 s in the client test; the prey caught and eaten); the place it is believed to be beyond 16 studs; any reset of the memory (a return to a rest, a new life, back to the start). While it holds, the place it follows is the contact's: where it is seen, or the mark where it was last seen, which never moves (SPEC 21: no lock-on marker follows a thing through the pot).

**The keys** (`Client/SpiderInput`, `Client/SpiderClient`). The brief offered a tap of the Focus button; that would have made a held Focus and a tapped one two things, broken Focus's toggle setting (every toggle press is a tap) and left the controller with no free button. Instead Recentre does it, as the lock button does in many third-person games: R, the middle mouse button, the right-stick click (R3) and the touch Recentre lock onto what the spider sees, or with nothing to lock recentre as before; a press while locked lets go and recentres (behind a spider that is facing what it watched). Touch also gets a small Lock button (50 px, left of Cancel, by Jump; on a phone under 400 px wide beside Focus, on a short screen in the bottom row's free slot), shown only while something can be locked or a lock holds, labelled Lock or Unlock; it locks and lets go and never recentres.

**While locked** (`Client/SpiderCamera.setLock`, `SpiderMotor`'s new optional `face`). The camera keeps it framed: the orbit's yaw and elevation turn to look from behind the spider at it (the elevation the default 18 degrees less the climb to it, inside the orbit's limits), the eye view's gaze turns to it (within its pitch limit), eased at 5 a second (5.8 degrees from the middle of the view 1.5 s after a lock taken with the view 70 degrees away); under Reduced Motion the view holds still until the thing is 25 degrees from where it would be and then cuts. The player's look still arrives and the follow takes it back. Not during the mating scene's two-shot or the close look. The body turns to it as Focus does (the lock is the motor's `focusPoint`, so the gaze tilts to it within the species' limit and the principal eyes keep inspecting it). The jump's aim ray runs from the camera to it, so a held jump plans the landing on it or on the surface just behind it.

**The sidestep** (`LockOn.strafe`). While locked and standing, the stick's left and right (A, D, the touch arrows) walk the spider round the thing on the plane it stands on, at the distance it had (a pull back of 2 a stud of error keeps it there), and forward and back close or open the distance (never under 0.9 studs), the distance they leave being the one kept; the walk is the motor's creep (0.65 studs a second, salticids stalk slowly), and the motor's new `face` keeps the body turned to the thing instead of to the walk. W4's movement cost is the walk's (`Nature.moved` says "walk"). Without a lock, A and D are what they were; on the line forward and back reel and lower as before. The motor's surface following is its own: on a box's top, its side and its underside the real motor circled a point 2 studs off for 3 s and back for 1 s with the distance off by at most 0.001 and the facing by at most 0.31 degrees, staying on the face. Without `face` the motor's walk is the same to the bit (checked).

**The setting** (`Data/SettingsSpec`): "Show what the spider notices" (`ShowNoticed`, Comfort, on by default, which is the game as it was; saved with the others; 24 settings). Off, `Client/PerceptionHud` draws none of the memory's rings, last-seen marks, labels or edge chevrons (the view is still built); Focus's centre ticks stay (they are the player's own), and so do the lock's four ticks (`PerceptionShapes.lockTicks`: on the diagonals 22 px out round the locked thing where it is on the screen, 7 px long, alpha 0.6 with a dark edge; at 0.45 they all but vanished on the sunlit floor in the mock). No status line, toast or word says a lock is on: the ticks and the camera are the cue (Nathan's minimal HUD).

**The words** (`UI/ControlText`, `UI/PeacockText`). The lock's name per device (R, Right-stick click, R3, Lock), the touch labels (Lock, Unlock), and the controls card, still at most ten lines at 61 characters on every device, display key and pad style (`tests/peacock.luau` and the Studio PromptSpec measure it): keyboard "R lock on or recentre   Locked: A/D circle, W/S close in" (seven base lines, ten in all); controller "Right-stick click: lock on (stick circles it) or recentre", with slow motion folded into the first line as "RT slow-mo" and Focus into the second (seven and ten); touch "Lock follows what you see (← → circle it)   Recentre", the silk line shortened so it no longer wraps and the crack's touch line shortened to "In the crack: tap Change to change spider; hold: look closely" so the card stays at ten. There is no controls page in the notebook.

**The self-test** (`Client/SelfTest`) gets one check, "lock", after the sill's prey: the spider is put 3 studs from one of the sill's flies on its own surface in plain view of it, and then the lock is taken only from what its senses give: Recentre locks, the body turns to it, a second of the sidestep moves it at the creep, Recentre lets go: 34 checks, not 33 (on the shim: "INFO lock: 3.0 studs away, facing it within 1 degrees, a sidestep of 0.66 studs in 1 s").

**Pins that moved with counts.** New suite `tests/lock.luau` (LOCK 115, among the client suites and in the default list): what can be locked; which a press takes (looked at beats nearer, nothing behind, nothing beyond range, never a cue); press and release; every ending; the sidestep's direction (circling, the pull back, closing and setting the distance, an idle stick, always on the plane); the camera's goal and follow and Reduced Motion's cut; the real motor circling on a floor, a wall and an underside, and the walk untouched without `face`; the words, the card, the setting; the touch button clear of every other at six screen sizes; and through the real client on the sill's boxes: a target not seen cannot be locked and R recentres, a seen one is locked and framed and faced with the touch button saying Unlock and the ticks drawn, the sidestep at the creep keeping its distance and facing (0.968 studs in 1.5 s, the distance 2.400 to 2.401, facing 0.01 degrees off), W closing in, Cancel letting go and A/D walking again, the aim ray on it, Cancel taking an aim before the lock, R again letting go and recentring, the lock ending by itself when the thing is gone, R3, the touch Lock, Reduced Motion, the eye view, and the setting hiding the rings and words while the lock works. SETTINGS 1151 to 1163 (one more setting through every per-setting check: 24 in the table, 8 in Comfort). PEACOCK 270 to 272 (the keyboard's base card has a seventh line, checked for both pad styles). SELFTEST 4 unchanged (34 checks to END in 68.9 s of play, 66.9 before). `tests/inspect.luau` 37 unchanged, but its start now waits for the save status to settle: with no server the client turns "offline" after `SaveRules.CLIENT_WAIT_REMOTES` (1.5 s) of the wall clock, and since ROBLOX-108 made the shim fast that toast landed on the choose key's refusal and covered its words (the check failed the same way at HEAD on this machine, so it was not this round's; settled before any check, the run no longer depends on the machine's speed). Every other suite and every golden unchanged. All 43 suites: 349,103 checks, nothing failed (348,974 in 42 at HEAD, ROBLOX-108's 348,958 with ROBLOX-109's TITLE 16). Strict analyze clean; 163 of 163 modules compile (SpiderClient at -O0, -O1 and -O2, under the 200-local limit: the lock's state is in the module table `M`). `tests/run.py` bundles `LockOn`.

Not run in Studio: all of it. What only Studio and a device can show: the camera's follow feel at 5 a second and whether the player's own look fighting it is comfortable; Reduced Motion's cuts; the lock's ticks on the real sill (a mock over a Godot render is `docs/art/lock_cue_mock.jpg`); the sidestep's look on the real rig (the drawn legs walking sideways); R3 and the touch Lock button on a PlayStation and a phone; and whether Recentre as the lock key suits Nathan or he would rather have the tap of Focus.

### ROBLOX-111 - What the real engine said, and the self-test as a profiler: the bed at 22 fps, the let-down at 0.559 (Nathan, Claude, 2026-10-20)

The in-Studio self-test (ROBLOX-104) ran on Nathan's PC in real Roblox Studio at v36 and passed 33 of 34: every region (ground under the spider, it stays put), the new life, the intro, the walk, the lock, the prey, the woodlice, the moths, the dusk, the notebook, the rest and the sound. It failed one, `FAIL the line: let down at 0.559 studs a second, not about 0.75`, and measured the bed at 22 fps (111 frames in 5 s, mean 45.5 ms, worst 76.8 ms, physics 60 fps) where v35, before ROBLOX-108's look streaming and ROBLOX-107's bushes, had it at 60 fps (mean 16.7 ms, 870 look parts always there). The sill was at 60 fps (mean 16.7 ms, worst 23.0 ms). Godot's defaults and every golden hold (`Species`, `NeedsModel`, `Growth`, `Catch`, `WaspDirector`, `LifeClock`'s rates, `TetherMath`, perception); no box moved (`WorldMap.REVISION` stays `world-2`); no save field, `CONTENT_REVISION` or `SCHEMA_VERSION` changed; nothing a player sees changes.

**The let-down at 0.559.** The CLI box world lets down at 0.752 from the same edge. 0.559 / 0.752 is 0.743: about a quarter of the descent lost. Read against `Movement/SilkController`, three ways the engine can lose it and the box world cannot, each now counted:
1. *A tangent touch* (the likeliest, and fixed). The body hangs plumb from the slab's lip, 0.31 off the brick face below it (the slab and the brick are flush at z -24; the bend sits 0.03 out from the lip), so every move down is a sweep of a sphere along a face 0.01 beyond its radius. An exact sweep never reports a face it is moving along; an engine sweep that reports one as a contact (Distance 0, the touch at the start) stopped the move there: `move` set the body back on the face's skin, slid once, met the same face again, and ended with the tick's motion unapplied. A touch on enough of the sweeps loses the descent wholesale: in the box world with the touch emulated on every sweep the let-down stops (0.000 studs a second), and on every second sweep it runs at 0.245; on every third or fourth the tick's later moves (the over-length pull, the constraint's) make it up. How often the engine reports it is not knowable here, so 0.559 is not reproduced to the digit; the mechanism and its size are. The fix (`Silk.grazing`): a sweep's contact that the move could not have reached (the move going along or away from its face, or into it by less than the gap the body already has to it) is not a contact; the whole move is taken and the skin is kept by the push-out after it (ROBLOX-107's `unstick`). For a real contact the move into the face is at least the gap it closes, so the box world's exact sweeps never trip it: every box-world scenario, Chet's silk spec, the tether goldens and the ten recovery scenarios run to the same tick.
2. *The game's time behind the clock*: the client runs a tick per 1/60 s of frame time, at most a quarter second's worth a frame (`accumulator`), so frames over 250 ms during the let-down (a look being made as the spider passes down the wall) would lose game time and the rate by the clock would fall while the game's did not. The bed's worst frame was 76.8 ms, so this is unlikely to be a quarter, but it is now measured.
3. *A push-out with an upward part*: where the brick's top meets the slab (y -3), a body nearer the face than its skin would be pushed up and out of the box's edge each tick, against the descent. In the box world every push-out of the let-down is horizontal (0.000 up).
The self-test now says which: `INFO line ticks:` the ticks the line ran and the game's seconds they make against the clock's, how much line was let out and what share of it the body fell, how many times a sweep stopped the body and how much it took off, how many tangent touches were let pass, and the push-outs with their upward part (from `SilkController`'s new `stats`, counts no rule reads). And the check is judged in the game's time (the ticks, 1/60 s each: the dial is the game's), the clock's rate printed beside it (`INFO line: let down at 0.753 studs a second of the game's time (the game's 0.75), 0.753 by the clock` in the shim). If the next run shows grazes let pass and the rate at 0.75, cause 1 is confirmed and fixed; if it shows the game's seconds well under the clock's, it is cause 2 and the frame profile on the line (below) says what the long frames were; pushed-up studs say cause 3.

**The bed at 22 fps.** The self-test is now a profiler. `Client/FrameProfile` (new): the render step's function marks each of its parts with a lap (the save tick, the world and the looks, the panels, then per tick the percepts, the senses, the lock, the prey, the motor, nature, the wasp, the rival, the mating, the life and the camera's tick, then the light, the poses, the spider's rig, the other rigs, the hud and the Lock button's scan, the fly bodies, the bug bodies, the sound, the lines, the camera, the eyes and the perception HUD); each lap's time is summed over the frame and its mean and worst kept, with the Lua heap's growth inside it (allocation, KB a frame) and the parts the fly and bug drawers placed. Off, every lap is one call that returns (a player never turns it on; the self-test turns it on for its samples only). Each frame sample of the self-test prints three lines: the frame time as before, `INFO profile <where>:` (the render step's own time a frame, mean and worst, by part, the allocation and the parts placed) and `INFO engine <where>:` (the client's memory, the Lua heap, the instance and physics primitive counts, the moving primitives and the awake parts, each where the engine has it), and a census of what the renderer is given by top-level folder (parts, see-through, casting shadows, meshed). The frame time minus the render step's own time is the engine's (rendering, physics, other scripts). A new check, "frame time in the bed, settled" (35 checks): the time from the spider's coming to the bed until no look was being made, the plans and parts made during the sample (thrash would show), then the bed's frame again, then with the looks out of the workspace (3 s) and with the global shadows off (3 s), each put back at once whatever happens; and the line's let-down is profiled too (`INFO profile on the line:`).
What the CLI shows (the shim's numbers, CPU time of plain Luau with Lua-table vectors, so several times the engine's; relative, not absolute): in the bed the render step is 13 to 14 ms a frame, on the sill about 8; the spider's rig the most (6 ms in both places), then in the bed the bug bodies (3 to 4 ms for 190 parts placed a frame of the 438 held: the far rule keeps the rest to ten a second) and the fly bodies (2 ms for 162 placed), the prey under 1 ms, the senses 0.3, nature, the lock, the lock button's scan and the looks under 0.05 each. The looks settle 0.03 s after the spider comes to the bed (they were made while it was held there for the region check); no plan and no part is made while it stands still; the plans over the run are 36 (the self-test's teleports across 17 regions: 7,399 parts made and 5,932 destroyed, as Studio counted). No O(n²), no build while still, no thrash, no per-frame ray beyond one a tick for cover. The Lua of the bed is about one and a half times the sill's in the shim, not three: the CLI points away from the client's Lua and toward the engine, where the bed differs from the sill by what is on screen: the bushes' leaf clusters (ellipsoid meshes, some casting shadows: the census counts 863 meshed and 132 shadow-casting look parts held at the bed, 755 and 125 at the sill, nearly as many, but at the sill they are 90 studs below and out of view) and some 350 more moving body parts. Nothing was changed for the bed: the look and the lighting are Nathan's, and there is no evidence yet to change them on. The next run's `profile in the bed` and the two comparisons say it: if the render step is a few ms of a 45 ms frame and the frame without the looks or without shadows is near 16 ms, the bushes' clusters (or their shadows) are the cost and the next round makes their far side lighter; if the render step itself is large, its parts say which.

**The sill's hitch.** v35's 129 ms frame on the sill (ROBLOX-107 left it unchased; ROBLOX-108 traced it in the shim to the rest's checkpoint save and cut that) is gone in v36's run: the sill's worst frame was 23.0 ms over 300 frames.

**Pins that moved with counts.** `tests/scenarios.luau` (BOXWORLD) 19 to 20: the let-down from the outer sill's front edge on the real regions' boxes with the engine's tangent touch emulated (the shim's `spherecastGraze`, new, 0 but in this case: a sweep within that much of a box it is not closing on reports a touch at Distance 0; `every` reports one in that many): exact sweeps let down at 0.753 with no touch let pass and the counts adding up (360 ticks, the line let out at 0.75, the fall the rate's); a touch on every sweep, 0.753 with 360 let pass; the same without the rule, 0.000 (360 stops, 4.111 studs taken off). `tests/selftest.luau` 4 unchanged, its pins widened: the run walks 35 checks to END in 79.8 s of play (68.9 before), the new check among the passes, the new INFO lines (`line ticks`, `profile on the line`, the bed's profile, engine, census, settling and its three samples, the sill's profile and census) required, and the heartbeats counted against the run's time less its frame samples (the CLI host's sample is one wait, with no heartbeat in it; the new samples made the old count fall short). Every other suite and every golden unchanged, their printed output the same line for line (compared with ROBLOX-110's run) but for the rest's size, which moves by a character from run to run, and the CPU timings. All 43 suites: 349,104 checks, nothing failed (349,103 at HEAD). Strict analyze clean; 164 of 164 modules compile (SpiderClient at -O0, -O1 and -O2, under the 200-local limit: the profiler is `M.Prof`, no new local). `tests/run.py` bundles `FrameProfile`. No preview: nothing drawn changed.

Not run in Studio: all of it. The next run of the self-test is the evidence: its new lines are listed in `docs/HANDOFF-roblox-world.md` (ROBLOX-111).
